extensive enough to pinpoint where exactly they were, and really - he didn’t care. He had
spent so much money… and he was pretty sure the twins had bought some stuff as well.
Considering the shop had been completely dedicated to female fashion, he wouldn’t be
surprised to find himself with some of the more… extravagant pieces later that day. Logically
he knew he needed the clothes, but really, that had been so damn much money for only very
few select items. At least, if he still had his trust fund, he wouldn’t feel so queasy about
spending this much. Not to mention, Harry had a feeling they weren’t done yet. (*)
That reminded him: He needed underwear as well, especially with all these nice new outfits.
Though he hadn’t planned on buying the female kind. It wasn’t as if anyone would get to see
what he was wearing underneath… and if one of those idiots from the pub managed to peek
up his skirt, Harry would claim that was exactly the reason he wore the less revealing smalls
meant for men. Only now there were the enthusiastic twins out for an unholy shopping
spree… Right. It was so easy to forget he was playing a role when around these two. ‘Harley’
didn’t know them as good as Harry did, so it really wouldn’t be strange that he didn’t want to
go shopping for underwear with them. Did girls do that anyway? Shop for their undies in
groups? With friends maybe? Thinking about the disaster that had been asking out a girl in
fourth year, Harry recalled with a cringe that they did tend to go everywhere in groups. Then
last year the date with Cho had turned out to be another disaster, yeah, but he was still proud
of actually walking up to her gaggle and asking her out. Honestly, girls’ behaviour sometimes
felt like they were intentionally making it harder for a bloke… Right. Not going down that
road.
Catching the eyes of some teenage girls basically gawking at them, Harry decided that
walking through the city with a twin on each side was a strange experience. He thought they
were garnering looks for simply being there and then some. Though, it was probably the
twins. It wasn’t everyday that you saw (to the passing observer) identical twins that weren’t
just quite similar looking, but similarly good looking. Fred and George had this effortless
grace – if they wanted to – and handsomely rugged looks, the kind that simply drew
everyone’s attention. And Harry was not a random passer-by, bad eyes or no, he could see
that just fine.
“Blimey!”
Blinking at suddenly feeling quite alone, Harry grinned at the absolute exuberance his twins
were exhibiting. They had come across an open front shop selling more casual wear… at
least, that’s what it looked like to Harry. For George and Fred it could have just as easily been
an adventure playground. Glancing at the tags, Harry was relieved to see the cheap prices,
and let himself get dragged along.
The twins soon were all over the place making excited noises every time something else
caught their interest, reminding Harry vividly of their father. It didn’t escape his notice how
the shop keeper was watching them, but he couldn’t find it in himself to care all that much.
With the twins… everything felt so much lighter.

“Harley, Harley look!” George crowed, rushing to his side and nearly overthrowing some of
the stands.
Blushing slightly at the shining bright eyes focused solely on him once again, Harry took
in… the straw hat? He had no idea why a simple, though rather nice, straw hat caused such
enthusiasm, but he didn’t question his friend. He let George put it on him, squinting a bit
from under the broad rim.
“Freddie!” George called back into the depths of the shop. “Gred, get your freckled behind
over here!” Harry wisely did not question that form of address either. “Have your eyes ever
espied such cuteness?”
Self-consciously Harry reached up to the hat, not sure if he really wanted to be called cute.
Well, he supposed it was a bit late to be put off by that. ‘Harley’ had enjoyed the twins’
compliments so far… but the hollow ache in his chest every time they called him by his
assumed name was disconcerting. He watched Fred rush out from the back, arms laden with
what Harry had learned were called crop tops. As funny as it would be to see one of the twins
wearing these, mostly frilly as they were, he was rather sure it would be him in the skimpy
outfits. Then again, maybe wearing something that showed off his flat stomach would draw
attention away from his too flat chest. And some of them really did look pretty…
He blinked rapidly at the turn of his thoughts. He hadn’t really taken on particularly feminine
mannerisms, had he? Though, to be honest, Harry wasn’t sure what behaviour would be
considered specifically feminine. What had he changed about himself so far, really? He had
grown out his hair, dressed more or less socially appropriate, but there had to be more to
being female than that. And seeing all the varying hairstyles in the Muggle World also very
clearly called attention to the fact that the long hair was not much more than a cliché. There
had to be more to it, but whether it was because he felt the lines between the genders were
blurring to him, or just because he was generally clueless, Harry couldn’t pinpoint it. If it was
giggling, he wasn’t doing a very good impression of a girl.
“Shoes!” The twins suddenly shouted, startling Harry out of his pondering.
“…What?”
Back in the clothes store they had been getting him everything in various sizes when Harry
hadn’t been able to point out the correct one. Well, he didn’t feel so stupid anymore when
each clothing article ended up needing a different size to actually fit him. But shoes were
another matter altogether as he learned very quickly after entering the store.
He hadn’t actually ever paid any mind to the size of his plain black school shoes, as those
were issued by Madam Malkin with everything else that was part of the uniform. They fit,
which was all that mattered, and considering Dudley’s cast-offs had never done that… No,
Harry really had no idea what his shoe size was. His current transfigured sandals did fit, too,
sure, but that was because he had transfigured his trainers to actually fit, intent and all that,
and…
“Hey, what’s wrong?”

Fred was standing close, his arm sneaking around Harry’s waist in a supportive gesture.
Harry wasn’t sure why, but the heat he felt creep up his neck at the sudden closeness yet
again had nothing to do with the weather. What was going on here? Yes, he cared about the
twins, he had basically grown up with them. They were his friends, but… but this kind of
reaction…
“Nothing, sorry,” he smiled fleetingly up at the redhead, not quite meeting the blue eyes.
“Just… muggle stores are really a bit overwhelming, aren’t they?”
Which was true, he did feel somewhat out of his depths here once again and it wasn’t just
because he had no idea what shoe size he needed. That part would be a bit embarrassing, but
hey, maybe wizarding and muggle measurements differed or something? Like how
Muggleborn students always got so confused when the professors issued essays in inches
instead of centimetres, because the Wizarding World had completely ignored the introduction
to the metric system. The imperial units had given Harry, muggle-raised as he was, more than
enough headaches over time as well. (*)
Fred hummed in agreement and they both took a look around. The store they were in was
indeed on the grand side, various levels could be made out from where they stood…
including a slightly spastic George who was busy jumping from one shelf to the next. He was
clearly fascinated by the various shoe styles, making Harry briefly wonder whether the
choices in the Wizarding World were just so limited, or if maybe the Weasley’s had simply
never been able to afford the more extravagant stuff before now. Their store did turn quite the
profit, didn’t it?
He watched as the second twin was drawn into the excitement, observed how the two would
find a certain style of shoes, discuss it between themselves, and sometimes look around at the
many posters on the walls. Those posters showed too perfect models advertising the certain
shoes and the clothing styles they were apparently supposed to be worn with.
Harry for his part stood very still for long moments before shyly stepping closer to one of the
shelves. He thought about holding different sizes against his feet to determine the size he
needed, but his attention was soon drawn by the wonders that are female fashion once again.
Those over there would look really nice with his favourite skirt…

Chapter End Notes

(*) He means a pussycat bow blouse. For those of us who aren’t native speakers, a
pussycat bow or lavallière is a style of neckwear often associated with women's and
girls' blouses and bodices. It takes the form of a bow tied at the neck similar to those tied
around the neck of kittens, cats, and the like. Yeah, kinda sexist. It can be more or less
frilly and therefore help conceal Harry’s lack of female curves. ;)
(*) I have no idea about prices in the UK, but just remember: It’s the 90s, things were
presumably less expensive than they are today.
(*) In Britain adopting the metric system was discussed in Parliament as early as

1818 and some industries and even some government agencies had metricated, or were
in the process of metricating by the mid-1960s. Both systems are in use now depending
on the context.

Chapter 15: Realising
Chapter Notes

I’m pretty sure there is something for rigger42 in this. :D

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Harry stirred the water in the tub carefully with his wand, concentrating on heating in lieu of
an actual incantation. As he had learned while washing his clothes with the same method,
heating charms just didn’t cut it. Or maybe he was simply doing it wrong. He couldn’t recall
if he was supposed to know a proper heating charm by now, but he hadn’t yet reached
temperature spells in his re-reading of his school books. Cooling charms definitely went over
his head.
Deeming the water hot enough, he added the stuff Madam Primpernelle had given him that
morning – before the whole debacle with the twins happened. If he recalled correctly, this
specific potion was meant to get rid of dead skin cells and was to be kept away from his hair.
Well, the bath hopefully would get rid of the stale sweat coating his skin. Checking that the
door was truly locked, Harry tugged his long hair up and secured it into a bun, making a
mental note to find out what spell the Madam had used to keep it there. He sank into the
scented depths with a sigh, recalling the last and only time he had ever taken a real bath - that
he could remember anyway. Even though the tub in the prefect’s bathroom back in fourth
year had been more a pool than anything else, he found he actually preferred this humble
setting. Although that might be the lack of nosy ghost.
“No, not all that steam again!” The hollow mirror voice lamented. “How am I to accurately
tell you off for your appearance, poppet? I can’t work like this!”
Right. There was that. Not creepy at all.
Leaning back and resting his aching feet, Harry concentrated on transfiguring a rag into a
small hand-held mirror (sans the commentary, thank you very much). Ever since that moment
in the clothes store, he had been itching to take a closer look at his scar – or at what was left
of it.
The face in the mirror, especially up close without his eyesight smudging details, was curious
indeed. The eyebrows were finely arched in a way he couldn’t recall ever seeing on himself.
It did make his eyes appear bigger, his resemblance to Lily Evans emphasized. Well, big eyes
were something he knew many girls thrived for and he could understand why. Personally, he
could admit that pretty eyes were something he found attractive on a- …on people, and now
his were quite the expressive feature, if he said so himself. Trailing a finger down his
disturbingly soft cheek, Harry recalled all the time Madam Primpernelle had spent on his

face. Oh well, maybe he really should invest in skin care? The result of this morning certainly
had dazzled the twins.
He remembered thinking they were in the heart of London and how it couldn’t be all that
hard to find some store selling care products, right? It wasn’t. But when they came across the
pink monstrosity of a shop their map had recommended, he balked. It was the colour, he
thought, or maybe the sheer abundance of it, painfully dredging up memories of bleeding
hands and a certain psychotic. Nope. He could not bring himself to do this, couldn’t get his
feet to move another step closer to that freaking doll house. It really was only missing the
kitten plates.
“Why is it all pink?” Fred questioned and Harry hadn’t been able to give an accurate answer.
Indeed, why was it?
“It was like this in the other shop, too, remember?” George pondered while Harry found
himself suddenly very busy having a minor mental breakdown.
So he might have started enjoying this whole dressing like a girl (disguise! It was a disguise,
okay?), but he was not going to step even a toe into this… this… pink, cotton candy
madness! He also was very much aware there were a lot of other decent shops around, so he
might have some luck in any of those. No need for whatever the Crumple-Horned Snorkack
that cliché shop was.
“Muggles seem to be very into colour-coding stuff,” Fred continued to muse, looking at the
pink shop front with a sort of morbid fascination – like he was wondering how to best use
this new information for a prank.
Suddenly George snapped his fingers as if an epiphany had struck: “It’s like ribbon
symbolism!”
“Huh?” Thoughts of all sorts of awareness ribbons crossed right through Harry’s pink-tinged
frustration. “No, I don’t think that’s what-”
“Oh you mean like the pureblood way of colour messages and ways to wear ribbons like
mum’s trying to teach Ginny?”
“Well, she can wear coded messages all she wants, won’t do a thing if the recipient doesn’t
even know the ‘ribbon-language’ exists,” George snorted, though there was a slightly bitter
undertone to his voice.
“Harley, pretty,” Fred nearly immediately turned towards Harry, “Do you like ribbons?”
That had been the end of their little foray into colours and their symbolism in muggle and
wizarding culture. It had sounded vaguely interesting to Harry, but his thoughts had been still
stuck on his care product issue. The longer they had been walking around, though, the more
Harry got the impression that there was a lot more to cosmetic and stuff than he had thought.
It seemed very bothersome. Did he really need something else? He had the samples Madam
Primpernelle had given him, after all. Though… she had kind of made a huge fuss about his
skin… Oh well. He was sure not every girl did the care product routine.

Back in his tub, Harry lifted his bangs away from his face and carefully scrutinised his scar.
He had had vague thoughts about using muggle make-up to cover it up and give himself more
freedom, but maybe that wouldn’t be necessary now. It really had faded, not much more than
a thin and somewhat silvery pale line now. The form was still unconventional and if someone
were to look at it closely enough, his disguise would be busted. But with the longer bangs
and the pointers Madam Primpernelle had given him… yeah, Harry really thought he would
be able to… to be free. Free of the stamp of the Chosen One.
In the stillness of the small bathroom tucked away in Fergus Wanlockhead’s flat Harry Potter
smiled brightly.
It was sometime during the first week of August that the thought of not returning to Hogwarts
took ever more shape in Harry’s mind.
He was sitting on his bed, trying to tame and order his ever growing jumble of notes on all
his schoolbooks. This meant he was sorting his piles of ‘unimportant’, ‘vaguely interesting’,
‘could strike his fancy at some point’ and ‘Godric’s sock draw how could he miss THAT?!’
into the thick notebooks he and the twins had picked up at a stationary store. Also, ballpoint
pens. Yes, that had been another experience… Anyway, right that moment there were chaotic
heaps of parchment (and shreds of the same) stuck to the walls of his little bedroom with
easily cancelled sticking charms. The whole room was basically covered in scraps of his
notes, but Harry felt confident he could keep track of what was where.
He had finally reached his fifth year books in his self-assigned re-reading. These last books
were taking him the longest, he really had been very… occupied last year, spent very few
hours actually doing schoolwork and a lot more being tormented by either his visions or
Umbridge. Yeah, he hadn’t learnt much during that time, but he was doing his best to make
up for it now. Seriously, he had no idea how he even managed to sit through half as many
OWLs as he had…
So now he was starting to transfer all his notes into his books, one page per spell and whole
chapters worth of interesting theory notes in-between. But he constantly had to make little
breaks or stand up and walk across the rather tiny space just so he could decipher what was
written on a certain parchment. Harry’s handwriting with a quill had always resembled
chicken scratch, but this sadly was another problem altogether: His eyes were dry and
starting to hurt and he was slowly but surely reaching his wits’ end. All this time at BandyLegs and he never had struggled so much. It was obvious his magic couldn’t help him with
this – there was no room to lead him around, no people to identify. Only his own atrocious
handwriting blurring in front of his near useless eyes. And now he had a headache.
Right, he needed some fresh air. It was hours still until he would need to be present for work
and going over all these notes only served to remind him of what he still needed. He thought
of Remus and his advice to visit Oblique Lane with its second-hand book shop. Leaving the
pub was always a little adventure with his sight, or lack thereof, but he braved it each week to
buy groceries. Hell, he had gone to Gringott’s a few days ago, all on his own. He didn’t count
the venture out into Muggle London, because he hadn’t been alone then, had simply needed

to follow the twins and their magical presences. Also, leaving Bandy-Legs gave Harry a
perfect opportunity to dress up. Yeah, he had really just thought that.
Decision made, Harry abandoned his notes for the moment to go through his new clothes.
So far they all resided on and around the lonely chair in his room, but maybe he could find a
way to hang some of them up? He was rather sure the nice blouses and dresses weren’t meant
to just lie there… no matter how carefully he had folded them. And indeed, Harry had been
meticulous in the care of his new stuff; it was, after all, the first time he had really chosen
something for himself and not been forced to dress in whatever was available. Not even
receiving his first school robes had felt like this, because the uniform followed a required
dress code. This… this was something else. These were all things he had chosen for himself,
things he had evaluated and tried on and decided he wanted. Sure, it helped that the twins
thought they all suited him quite nicely, but Harry had fallen in love with the fabrics and cuts
all on his own.
Thinking about where he would be going, Harry decided on a long, slim-fitted skirt that was
tied high in the waist and somewhat imitated the less formal robe cuts he had seen around the
magical streets. The cut was too severe for the flowing and colourful muggle styles of the
moment, and Harry had been eyed rather confusedly for the choice, but Fred and George had
agreed with him that it suited the more conservative wizarding fashion. He would be walking
the line between the expensive day robes every ‘respectable’ witch or wizard was expected to
wear and the muggle fashion that most Muggleborn, or those who could not afford otherwise,
wore.
To think just a few weeks ago Harry had no idea at what a disadvantage his purely muggle
clothes put him. Now, with a little more exposure to wizarding culture, he could see the
difference and he also felt it was somewhat his duty to assimilate himself into this world
more. He was, after all, living in the magical world now, so it made only sense to him to dress
the part. Though, whether he agreed with the prejudice connected to certain clothing
choices… Having listened to the various conversations he overheard on a daily basis, be it in
the pub or on Carkitt Market, Harry had a deeper understanding of the preconceived notions
people associated with clothing styles. Really, it was not all that different to the muggles,
simply another set of bias. Yes, he enjoyed dressing in the fascinating muggle fashions he had
just started to explore, but now Harry was more perceptive of dressing appropriately for each
situation.
So for a trip into Diagon Alley with the intention of finding reliable information in form of
books that he possibly would have to bargain over Harry would dress the part of respectable
young witch (with just a dash of curiosity and open-mindedness towards the muggle culture).
Though, he would definitely change into something less restricting for the evening at BandyLegs.
Now, dressed in the ankle-length skirt and one of the nice blouses tucked neatly in, Harry
ventured over into the tiny bathroom to tame his hair. He could scarcely believe it, but the
few times he had washed his hair since Madam Primpernelle spent time pampering it had
worked wonders. He had had no idea that a simple shampoo, magical or not, could make
such a difference.

“You appear quite agreeable today, darling.”
Yeah, even the damn mirror agreed. Sitting on the edge of the tub, Harry concentrated on
plaiting his now much smoother hair. Oh, it was still rebellious, but it also looked and felt so
much healthier. It helped that Harry was slowly getting better at doing it up in simple styles
as well and kept away from drying charms. Those apparently were the worst choice one
could make when it came to hair. Whatever the cause, his hair was now certainly a far cry
from the riotous and slightly matted mane it had been before.
Seriously, this summer was intense. Or maybe Harry had grown soft now that he didn’t have
to work hours in the Dursley’s garden… Wishing he had brought the cute straw-hat George
had found for him, Harry sighed at the oppressive heat as he reached the intersection of
Knockturn to Diagon Alley. Despite the burning summer sun, the midday crowds flocking to
Florean Fortescue’s Ice Cream Parlour were not diminished in the least. He’d bet none of
these people sucked at cooling charms like he did; although, most of them were sitting in
every available shade. Harry eyed the cool treats a bit longingly as he walked past the
parlour. He kind of missed Mr. Fortescue’s free sundaes.
Oblique Lane was supposed to be an underappreciated side street of Diagon… right… and
going by that name, Harry supposed the lane would be somewhat slanting away from Diagon
Alley… Well, there was nothing to it. He would have to search the whole of the blazing alley.
If the wizarding district felt like this, he did not want to even think of the temperatures in the
Muggle World today what with all that concrete. Taking a deep breath of the too hot air
around him, Harry straightened his blouse’s bow determinedly. He would start right here and
go down the whole lengthThere was a familiar face standing close to the ice cream parlour, not doing much of
anything, only standing and staring suspiciously at the crowd, and managing to appear
creepily suspicious in turn.
Mad Eye Moody.
Harry very nearly stopped in his tracks at the sight of the grisly war veteran looking so out of
place between the families and oh-so-sophisticated other shoppers. Panic crawled up his
spine as he hastily turned his head to stare in the other direction, away from Moody, and
forcing himself to keep moving. If he kept going, the ex-auror would have no reason to
suspect him.
But why was he here? He was obviously not doing anything that required being in Diagon
Alley, he was just standing there… scrutinising passer-bys, looking for something. Looking
for Harry. Fuck. Biting his lip to stop himself from constantly glancing at the man, he forced
himself to believe in his disguise. It was fine. Moody would not recognise him. There really
was no reason why anyone would be looking at him and seeing Harry Potter. He was Harley,
just Harley, a little nondescript waitress of a pub in Knockturn Alley.
Keeping his back ramrod straight and his head held high, Harry walked briskly past the man,
trying his best to look like he didn’t give one Kneazle-lick about him. Harley had no reason
to give him the slightest bit of attention, after all. Now he could only hope the paranoid

Moody wasn’t in the habit of using that eye of his to glance through random peoples’ clothes.
Could be a bit incriminating if he clocked Harry male when dressed the way he was right
now.
And he wasn’t going to think further on Moody’s possible voyeuristic ability.
Now very glad for the midday crowds, Harry moved past the ice cream parlour. He kept his
gaze mostly on the ground, though his head held high, only glancing up every other shop to
make sure he didn’t miss the intersection to Oblique Lane he was trying to find. There could
only be one reason why he was seeing Order members standing around the alley like sentries.
They were looking for him, expecting him to pop up in the magical district like some gullible
idiot. (Well, he was, but he wasn’t dumb enough to do so without any disguise!) They even
had specifically staked out what they suspected to be his most likely haunts, he realised a
second later. There was another one near the Quidditch shop… and... Yes, now that he looked
for it, Hestia Jones was stood near Flourish and Blotts.
Why was Dumbledore so insistent? The war was over before it really could start up again,
Voldemort was gone. There was no bloody reason why Harry shouldn’t be out and about,
there was no reason why these people would spend their summer searching for him. What
were they trying to achieve anyway?
Clenching his hand in an attempt to keep it from nervously rubbing at his scar, Harry walked
on. He wished he knew where he had to go, walking up and down the alley was exactly the
kind of obvious behaviour he should be avoiding.
What reason could the headmaster have to not only have insisted on Harry staying hidden
away at Privet Drive, but to even mobilise the Order to apprehend him after he had
disobeyed? If this had been before that last fight, before the horrific experience in the
Ministry Atrium… yes, if Voldemort were still out there, even if only in spirit form as he had
been up until Harry’s fourth year, he would understand. It was unlikely that Harry would
have disobeyed Dumbledore in the first place if that were the case.
But it wasn’t. So, what was this about? The only thing Harry could think of was the
Horcruxes and how he had never told the headmaster more than the basics about his
experience when he had been possessed by Voldemort. He never told the man that he knew
there had only ever been three of the vile soul containers. There just had been no reason to do
so when the fight was over and done with anyway. He had, perhaps naively, assumed that
Dumbledore already knew. Like he always seemed to know everything.
Finally, Harry noticed an almost hidden entrance, half obscured by overhanging potted plants
and the advertisement of the shops framing it. That had to be the lane Remus had told him
about, though honestly, he could have done with a more accurate description.
Ducking into the side street, Harry chanced a small glance back, hoping no one, or at least no
Order members, had seen him doing so. He didn’t see anyone suspicious or at least not more
suspicious than most magical folk appeared to him anyway. Seriously, those robes were the
ideal clothing to hide weapons in and Harry was pretty sure it was only the wizarding
tendency to abhor any kind of physical labour that stopped non-magical weapons from being
more popular. Okay, maybe now he was the paranoid one…

Oblique Lane was a narrow and rather… oblique… street. Yeah, really. It looked a lot less
lively than Diagon, too. In fact, it appeared to be a mostly residential area. The summer sun
was throwing thick rays down through overhanging roofs, and shadows of the foliage above
danced over highlighted dusty patches of cobblestone. It was peaceful, Harry decided after a
moment. He couldn’t make out where the little street ended, but his magic tugged him
towards the sign for a bookstore about three quarters down the path. Well, he realised it was a
sign for a bookstore the moment he stood right in front of it, damn his eyes.
The store was silent and even dustier than the cobblestone outside, but there was also a heavy
feeling in the air, like the rows upon rows of books emanated the very magic used to
manufacture them. As Harry stepped closer to one of the shelves, he realised that that may
very well be the case. These were second-hand books, all of them, and he could feel the
different magical residues left on them – probably even from their former owners. It was
vaguely like sensing people around him, only much much softer. It was the sheer number of
books around him, whispering, that made it possible for him to feel it at all.
“Hello?” He called into the depths of the store while running the tips of his fingers over the
old and worn spines. “Anyone here?”
There was no answer, but Harry wasn’t sold on it. There was definitely someone here, but he
supposed, as long as Harry didn’t try to take anything from the shop without paying first,
they had no actual reason to make themselves known. This wasn’t Diagon Alley, he was the
only customer at the moment, and there had barely been anyone out on the street too. So, they
either hadn’t expected anyone to actually come in here, or they were keeping an eye on him
without him noticing. There didn’t seem to be any anti-theft wards, at least he couldn’t sense
any like he had the wards in St. Mungo’s. He shrugged. All the better to roam in peace
without some shop keeper dogging his steps.
Harry started out with his Hogwarts list, going through the rows to collect second-hand
editions of his needed books, but soon he found himself pulling out others as well. It went
from books on the same material to corresponding topics and at one point completely veered
off as he immersed himself in a text about wizarding history and culture and how they
influenced each other.
Harry was utterly fascinated and didn’t even notice when a middle-aged man with what
appeared to be prematurely greying hair stepped up behind the counter.
The chapter that had captivated him into sitting down right where he was standing talked
about the necessity of graduation in British wizarding society opposed to other countries. It
said passing one’s exams was a must to even be allowed to practice magic. If you didn’t
graduate you were essentially deprived of your innate ability to use magic for the rest of your
life, and without that ability you were considered useless for society. This was apparently
enforced through the destruction of your attuned wand - which had Harry unconsciously
reaching for his own, assuring himself it was still there and whole.
The book went on a small discourse then about how it was only a few decades ago that 90
percent of the people who for whichever reason did not graduate, left the Wizarding World
completely. And glancing down at a tiny, nearly illegible footnote told Harry exactly what
happened to the remaining 10 percent that stayed. It was not pretty, apparently, but suicide

seemed to be one of those topics not talked about in the magical world. He supposed it was
another culture thing, but then again, it reminded him of Aunt Petunia whispering with the
other stay-at-home wives of Privet Drive. In both worlds there seemed to be a stigma to the
subject of taking one’s own life and Harry wondered about it. Was the cause for this stigma
rooted in history? Did other countries handle the issue differently?
But back to the whole not-graduating-matter that ended in destroyed wands. There were no
figures for the present, but Harry had a feeling they wouldn’t look much better. These people
vanished from society, one way or another.
In fact, Hagrid was the only one who suffered the fate of expulsion from school, wand broken
and all, that Harry could recall ever having heard about. Though it had been used as a threat a
lot, now that he thought about it… And that Hagrid had stayed on school grounds as well as
in the magical world was likely only because of Dumbledore – well, and his giant genes,
probably. For Hagrid there really was no other place to live than the magical world.
Harry shuddered, thinking maybe he had no choice in the matter after all. Maybe he really
would have to go back to school, if he wanted to or not. Then he turned the page and came
across one name that had him perking up and continue reading more carefully. Newton
Scamander. Respected expert in the field of Magizoology and bestseller author. And very
much expelled from Hogwarts.
So apparently being kicked out, or leaving before seventh year, didn’t have to be the end of
everything. That is, if the student in question took their leave after taking the Ordinary
Wizarding Level, because then they were deemed to be full-fledged wizards and may retain
their wands, as Newton Scamander had apparently done. And look where that bloke had gone
in his life!
Harry breathed a little easier then. He had sat his OWLs, had even passed seven of them with
the possibility of re-sitting another, and more than that: He had not been expelled, he was
only toying with the thought of not returning. Maybe that was kind of the equivalent of
finishing Secondary School without A-Levels? Whatever the case, it was a relief to know that
no one would be able to take away his wand and cut him off of his magic.
He made doubly sure that he understood the information correctly, before adding the book to
his pile. He would be looking for material on Ministry issued OWLs and NEWTs, just in
case, because he was absolutely sure Hermione once mentioned that one could actually take
their exams there. In modern times one was not necessarily forced to attend Hogwarts for a
recognized education as long as one passed the Ministry exams.
He found what he was looking for not long after and decided then and there that he would
need to read this specific tome in-depth. There appeared to be a lot more courses available for
exam at the Ministry than what were being taught at Hogwarts and Harry was confused. But
if he understood the convoluted phrasing correctly, there also would be no one trying to stop
him from taking certain NEWT exams. He was automatically approved for all possible
NEWT exams if he could prove a passing grade in the corresponding OWL. No Snape
restricting him from NEWT level just because he hadn’t managed an O in Potions. He
wondered if he was also allowed to take whatever OWL he wanted considering there didn’t

seem to be any official pre-exams to qualify for those… Then again, that possibly tied into
the whole need for graduation thing? He was confused.
But no matter, there was now a very big point on his not-going-back list that was tipping the
scale rather precariously. It also helped that retaking his history OWL at the Ministry
wouldn’t stir any annoying attention and meddling as it definitely would at Hogwarts. He was
not in the mood to be questioned on his decisions on this. But, if he really, honestly decided
not to go back, he would need help. There was no way he would be able to master the whole
material in self-study, and he somehow really wanted those NEWTs, whether they were
needed or not.
Perhaps he should also look for a book on careers in the Wizarding World? He hadn’t much
of a clue what choices there actually were, it had always been somewhat expected of him to
follow in his father’s footsteps. Only, becoming an auror didn’t sound all that interesting to
Harry anymore ever since he started working at Bandy-Legs and found himself exposed to so
many new experiences. Although, he had to admit he hadn’t questioned the career chosen for
him before, had just gone along with it as he had with so many other things. So maybe he
shouldn’t point fingers there. But no more. Maybe Remus would be willing to help him cram
if he actually decided to leave Hogwarts…
“Young Miss, if I may,” a gentle voice interrupted Harry’s thoughts and he jumped in place.
There finally was a shop keeper, it seemed, and looking at him Harry felt strongly reminded
of Remus. And not only because this man was clearly a werewolf himself - he had met
enough to notice the signs easily by now.
“I’m sorry,” he flustered, realising his position sat there in the middle of the store reading
books he hadn’t paid for. “I didn’t mean to-”
“I could conjure you a chair, if you would prefer so. There really is no need for you to ruin
your pretty clothes on my dusty floors,” the man smiled at him in a way that again reminded
Harry of Remus Lupin.
There was something scholarly about him, in his manner, the way he dressed, even in the
absolute calm he seemed to project. Yet Harry was very much aware of the underlying
lycanthropic magic, the powerful wolf that ruled both men’s lives once a month… and in
Remus’ case sadly also outside of the full moon. Yeah, he could see why his former professor
would recommend this store, if this man was the owner.
“Are you sure?” Harry questioned a little embarrassed, only to blush abruptly as he noticed
he couldn’t get up as easily as he had expected. His slim-fitted skirt was restricting his
movement a lot more than he had realised before; there would be no getting up without
pulling himself up on the shelf he was sitting next to.
“Certainly, little Miss,” the friendly voice sounded a bit amused and a hand, not calloused but
definitely strong, appeared in his line of sight. “Let me help you up. The name’s Lycus Job
(*),” he introduced himself as he pulled Harry up with a careful tug, stepping out of his
personal space the moment he regained his footing.

Harry muttered a soft thank you while patting down his troublesome skirt.
“If you want to stay out of that heat to read for a bit, the light’s best over there. You’ll have a
nice view of the street, too,” Mr. Job nodded over to a wide window Harry hadn‘t even
registered before.
He felt a bit discomfited being caught out like that, but hey, that skirt totally had it out for
him! Anyway, here he was, going out because of a reading-induced headache finding himself
back in a book shop. Reading. Hermione would be so proud.
“Thank you, but I really don’t want to impose,” Harry added, still a bit flustered.
“It is no matter, little Miss,” Mr. Job smiled, before turning and going over to where Harry
had piled his chosen books. “I do enjoy my quiet, but you don’t appear all that disruptive,
now, do you?” He bent down and hefted the books into his arms to bring them over to the
window.
“O-Okay,” Harry agreed, taking a deep breath to regain his equilibrium. “But please tell me
when you need me to leave, Sir,” he insisted.
Lycus Job chuckled quietly at that and Harry watched him conjure a nice stuffy armchair next
to the window but still shielded from the blazing sun.
“It’s Lycus, little Miss,” he corrected gently and Harry felt himself blush hotly again.
“Harley!” He blurted. “I-I mean… how rude of me. I’m Harley,” he stuttered, feeling ready to
hit himself. Being out of Bandy-Legs really put a damper on his self-confidence it seemed.
Harry slowly made his way over to the nice chair Lycus had conjured for him, being mindful
of possible obstacles his eyes might register too late.
“Make yourself at home, Miss Harley,” Lycus nodded. “I’ll be in the back if you’ve need of
me.”
And just like that Harry found himself sitting in the peaceful niche by the window, his books
stacked neatly beside him, and the sound of the shop owner rummaging in the back acting the
only soundtrack.
He sighed contentedly and leaned back. However he would decide on the Hogwarts issue,
graduation, and the whole she-bang, he would not let Dumbledore interfere with his summer.
It was his very first summer in freedom, there was no way he would let anyone take that
away from him.
And if he rubbed at his faded scar in the dark of his bedroom at night, searching for that
something that used to be there, no one had to know.

Chapter End Notes

(*) Lycus: Latinized form of the Greek name Λυκος (Lykos) meaning "wolf". Ain’t I
creative today? ;)
(*) Job: Not talking about a job, but the biblical mention. I think it’s from the Hebrew
name ‫'( ִא ּיֹוב‬Iyyov) which means "persecuted, hated". In the Book of Job in the Old
Testament he is a righteous man who is tested by God, enduring many tragedies and
hardships while struggling to remain faithful. Reminded me of the woes of werewolves
under Ministry reign.

The whole setting basically screams for it, but I’m not sure if I can pull it off. At the
very least there will be some more critical commentary concerning characters like
Dumbledore and the Wizarding World as a whole, but bashing in my opinion is a bit
more than that. So, what do you think?

Chapter 16: Stumbling
Chapter Notes

We all seemed mostly on the same page when it comes to what is generally called
bashing. So, decision: Unless the story somehow develops in that direction (which I
don’t see as of yet, but who knows), there won’t be bashing per se. We WILL have more
critical commentary.
If something appears to be bashing at first glance, don’t despair, there is probably a good
reason for it, and everything will explain itself at some point. ;)
Aqua_Lilly and SquishySkeebo ? It's finally time!

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Only Knuts. He could have laughed himself silly if it hadn’t been such a sad thing for the
owner of the shop. How was he surviving on that miniscule a profit?
The time spent at Lycus Job’s store had been a refreshing change of pace, not to mention very
enlightening. Harry had lost himself in books over tea for a while. There had only been one
other customer that he took note of and the man had only hurried in and out, apparently
picking up a pre-made order. The sun had managed a good chunk of its way across the sky
before he resurfaced from the written world. It was to Lycus preparing a fresh cup of tea for
Harry and sitting down across from him. That had been somewhat awkward, to be the one
served considering his job and experiences growing up, but there was something about
Lycus…
They had chatted amiably over nothing and everything for a while – that is, after Harry
calmed his racing heart and told himself firmly not to be such a scaredy-kneazle. Just because
Remus had recommended this shop, and the owner just so happened to be another werewolf,
didn’t mean his disguise was busted. Though, Lycus being a werewolf probably meant he
could smell the truth just as much as Harry’s friends back at Bandy-Legs could. Not that
anyone had mentioned it, but the way they sometimes looked at him… well.
At one point Harry even randomly inquired about a book on cosmetic charms such as teeth
whitening, hiding blemishes, (nail) colour changing, and the like. He remembered Madam
Primpernelle mentioning something along those lines and couldn’t help but wonder.
“Now, I don’t have the fancy new kind,” Lycus had said, looking thoughtful for a moment,
“But I remember my mother used to have a booklet with all her nifty charms…,” he trailed
off, looking around the shop in a way that suggested he expected his mother’s charms
collection to appear out of nowhere …or form itself from the accumulated dust motes.
Harry’s eyes went wide.

“Oh, I wouldn’t want to take an heirloom-”
“That is quite alright, Miss Harley,” Lycus interrupted with a half-smile before standing and
starting to peruse the shelves. “My mother is long gone and it would make me happy to know
that little treasure of hers was still useful.”
Harry had watched the man wander ever deeper into the shop while muttering to himself.
Now the booklet of the late Mrs. Job was safely tucked away under Harry’s pillow to be
carefully studied late at night (when he wasn’t likely to get caught- err… interrupted.. oh
whatever). The man might have meant it as a present, but Harry had sworn to himself to
simply keep it safe for when Lycus one day had children of his own.
But the afternoon with the friendly store owner had taught him a lot more than possibly nifty
beautifying spells. What Harry learned that day was not only knowledge hidden between
dusty pages of books. No, talking to Lycus had provided him with insight on the issues he
had noticed in passing ever since coming to live directly in the Wizarding World. Working at
Bandy-Legs and listening to the patrons there could only teach him so much, after all. And
Harry felt it would have been beyond rude to question certain customers about their heritage
and how the Ministry mistreated them for it, no matter how insanely curious he was.
Lycus, however, had no such qualms once he realised Harry knew what he was and had not
run for the hills: Creatures, werewolves especially, were not only prevented from owning a
vault or being properly employed. No, they were also forbidden from renting or even buying
a place in Wizarding Britain.
Harry had thought Remus lived in the Muggle World for financial reasons, but the truth of the
matter was that he wouldn’t even be allowed to rent a small flat in the wizarding part of the
country if he owned all the Galleons in Gringotts. Technically not even in Knockturn,
although it was an open secret that the people there sometimes rented out to wolves, if never
for long periods of time. So, werewolves in general had no way of staying in the Wizarding
World permanently.
The Ministry was banning and shunning creatures on all fronts, yet for the most of them they
did it through more subtle ways. Ways that weren’t likely to even be noticed by the general
public. Obviously they had no reason to hide their disgusting actions when concerning
werewolves as lycanthropes especially were publicly reviled after the war(s). Those like
Greyback, living up to every cliché and openly siding with Voldemort, had ruined the
perception of their entire species.
Another restriction, this one on creatures in general (and damn if Harry wasn’t curious about
how ‘creature’ was defined in this context), was being unable to inherit or uphold any titles.
Had Harry not talked to Lycus, it wasn’t likely he would ever have learned of this kind of
oppression. The werewolf only managed to live the way he did because he had inherited the
small building his store was located in before he was turned – and because very few had
become aware of his lycanthropic affliction. He wasn’t turning much of a profit, in fact, it
was only just enough to survive on, as Harry had surmised.

Not that Lycus had told him such directly, but comments about him being lucky because he
didn’t have any rent to pay, or how fortunate it was that wizarding homes didn’t have to pay
for water and heating, told their own story. Harry wasn’t sure how those worked here, but
considering heating spells and Aguamenti were a thing, he could make an educated guess.
Maybe it had something to do with runes?
So now here he was with stacks upon stacks of books that only cost him a few Knuts a piece
and his head full of thoughts on the unfairness creatures had to live with every day. It wasn’t
right and Harry didn’t want to believe all wizards would stand behind these restrictions if
they were made aware. If he started to believe that… No, he couldn’t go down this kind of
road right now.
Turning back to his books, Harry concentrated on one specific insult. He was sure Hermione
would have felt just as miffed as he currently did, on both accounts, though Harry felt more
insulted on Lycus’ behalf than the books’: The absolute lack of regard wizards had towards
second-hand materials if they didn't happen to be priceless heirlooms. Ridiculous, they didn’t
do updating, but buying second-hand was also frowned upon. It was a shame and Harry felt
bad for paying next to nothing, but it did come in handy for him at the moment.
On his way home, slightly high on how little he had actually needed to pay for all his books,
he had indulged in a trunk. Now all his beautiful new clothes were carefully stowed away in
the magical space – magical meaning that, even though it was only a basics version, it had
two and a half compartments that never would have fit in a non-magical trunk: One with a
few different sections to stow clothes that weren’t in danger of creasing, also storing Harry’s
shoes, and a second to hang up his dresses, skirts and silk blouses. The ‘half a compartment’
was basically a sectioned drawer now holding a few odds and ends. It still meant he had no
place for his books, but the next bigger edition would have cost a whole ten Galleons more…
Maybe he should start reading up on expansion charms and try one on his satchel. Better not
tamper with the magic on his brand-new trunk, he was quite fond of the spelled water
resistance, and rather relished the so-called 'feather-weight'.
With a somewhat exasperated sigh he got up and started looking through all those wonderful
clothes, running his fingers giddily over satiny fabrics. Circe, his wardrobe really did look
like that of a (very feminine) girl now. His one lonely pair of jeans was so worn out, he
wouldn’t even be comfortable wearing it un-transfigured now. And it wasn’t only the worn
thin material and the permanent stains, no, there was more than one hole lingering that
resisted a Reparo. One especially had decided to appear precariously situated on his backside
and that was when Harry finally relented and cast the garment aside. Perhaps he could
repurpose it somehow...
Then again, he had more than enough other clothes now and he couldn’t dress as Harry Potter
at the moment anyway - also, he just didn’t want to. Smiling, Harry thought about Harley’s
outfit for the night, fingers tracing along the soft fabrics once more and admiring the feeling.
Then his glance dropped down to where his new shoes were lined up, an impression of a
certain excursion popping into his mind.
“If the shoe fits, strut,” said the George of his memory as he slid some kind of strappy heel
on Harry’s foot.

He didn’t really like the style, it looked more like a death trap being attached to his innocent
foot, but that wasn’t what mattered about this moment. George was gazing up at him with
such a Look… Harry couldn’t put it into words, but it wasn’t any way he had ever been
looked at before.
He didn’t end up buying the high-heeled death trap, and he didn’t exactly enjoy shopping for
footwear anywhere close as much as he had enjoyed modelling for the twins. A lot of the
shoes had been so Merlin-be-damned clunky, there was simply no way Harry would be
wearing them – and not just because they would be impractical at work, but because he was
unwilling to lug around so much extra weight.
Still, that day Harry had ended up buying more than the planned-for pair of light-weight
canvas sneakers that he would be able to dye different colours with a spell. No, he personally
also decided on something called ballet flats as a simple way of compromising between
wizard and muggle style that would suit his skirts. A pair of actual sandals also found its way
into his overflowing shopping bags. He had kept away from anything with a heel, too tired at
that moment to learn walking on them – and maybe too embarrassed. George and Fred had
tried, but Harry had persisted. It wouldn’t be comfortable to work with heels anyway.
Still, he could basically smell the mischief when the twins had finally acquiesced… And
right he was. Unpacking and resizing his many bags when he returned to his small room at
Bandy-Legs, Harry had found a lot more than his sensible choices: There was a pair of what
he thought the very nice shop assistant had called Dr. Martens boots with some sort of
flowery print, one pair of unfortunately heeled shoes that he, with a shudder, even
specifically remembered. The enthusiastic young man helping the trio around the shop had
said something about a ‚Marie Antoinette style’. Apparently, Muggles these days had a thing
for this form of more or less historically accurate footwear. Harry had thought the so-called
‘Fluevogs’ in ‘Marie Antionette style’ looked suspiciously like the footwear he had seen
peeking out from under some of the robes of the more… elderly … wizards around Diagon
Alley. Yeah. No. He was rather sure the twins had only bought those for the laughs, not to
mention: heels. He was not sure he could do this. Nor did he really want to. (*)
Talking about heels, Fred and George had managed to one-up even the ugly historic shoes,
though Harry secretly didn’t have anything against the look of the knee-high platforms he
had found at the very bottom of the bag. In fact, he had blushed quite fiercely imagining
wearing these shoes when next he met the twins…
Putting ever more work into his disguise the further along the summer went was soothing in a
way. It assured him that he wouldn’t be recognised, even if the unthinkable happened and one
of the Order made their way to Bandy-Legs. Yeah, doing it to further his disguise was a nice
excuse for more girly styling and changing outfits unnecessarily (even if part of him insisted
that it wasn’t unnecessary if the other blouse simply looked prettier to that specific skirt).
Snorting softly to himself, Harry admitted, if only in his own mind, that he had come to
honestly enjoy being Harley and dressing up just because he could.
“You have your secrets, and I have mine,” Treasach stated mysteriously as Harry sat with him
during a lull in the early hours of the night.

He liked sitting with his serpentine friend, feeling the soothing waves of the man’s magic
wrap in gentle ribbons around him. There was never a need to fill the silence with words
when he spent time with Treasach. It may mean that Harry didn’t really know all that much
about the Donas as a person, but if his summer experiences had taught him anything, it was
to listen to and to trust his magic. Harry’s magic incidentally very much appreciated
Treasach’s presence, always calming him down from the stress of the job or the paranoia that
came with being who he was. All the more surprising was the sudden non sequitur.
“Huh?” Was Harry’s highly eloquent answer. It earned him an upturn of his friend’s lips
before those slitted eyes pinned him into place with an unreadable look.
“It is as I said before,” Treasach finally continued in that quiet and rumbly voice of his. “You
are not alone anymore and I think soon you will truly know what that means.”
That said, accompanied with another soft caress of the man’s magic, Treasach left Harry to
his confusion. Harry wouldn’t see him for a while after this conversation, but that’s another
story to tell another time.
This evening Bandy-Legs seemed to be unusually empty. Which was not to say there weren’t
any patrons, but it certainly wasn’t the same raucous atmosphere Harry by now was used to.
More or less anyway. Treasach had left the pub shortly after their little chat, if one could call
it that, and Harry hadn’t seen his favourite pack of werewolves yet, but he could make out
some of the older patrons. These people were all more the ‘here to chat’-type than the ‘here
to drink’-type and generally didn’t create even half as much noise as the younger clientele
whenever they frequented Bandy-Legs.
Considering the state his rather hung-over boss was still in, Harry preferred it that way. But it
did make for a few awkward moments in-between re-filling glasses and chatting a bit with
the more friendly customers. It wasn’t like he could spend more than a few minutes on
cleaning up a mess or similar matters what with magic on his side before the need to occupy
himself with something else arose. As welcoming as the people here had been, this was still
Knockturn Alley and doing things the muggle way if you had the magic to avoid as such…
Well, it wasn’t exactly something one wanted to be seen doing all that often. In a place like
this abstaining from using magic, as Harry had learned early on, implied either a lack of skill
or a lack of magic itself. Both of which were not particularly wanted at Knockturn. Also, it
was basically screaming ‘easy victim’.
He was just going over the books in an attempt to look busy, trying to remember all Fergus
had taught him about accounting, when it happened. The din of voices in the pub was low
enough with only the calmer and somewhat more serene customers present, so the awful
creaking sound of the swinging doors was easy to make out from across the parlour.
Instinctively Harry glanced up, prepared to greet whoever had made their way in. Instead, his
heart skipped a beat.
“Well, brother-mine, what say you?”
“I say I’d be a bit disappointed if it wasn’t for our sweet bonfire fairy behind that bar over
there.”

“Her presence just adds this touch of wickedness, doesn’t it?”
“Wicked Harls.”
“Beautiful Ley.”
They bowed in unison before confidently making their way over.
“Can’t get enough of that, we can’t,” they chorused, successfully having drawn the whole
pub’s attention when they reached the counter - and a deeply blushing Harry.
Fred and George were at Bandy-Legs, and Harry was honestly debating transfiguring himself
into one of the floorboards and pretend to be part of the decor. This could not end well. But
instead of moving even a single muscle, Harry could only watch wide-eyed as the twins made
their way across the room, moving around any obstacle expertly in a way that spoke of
experience he couldn’t quite name in that moment. It was only seconds before a roguishly
smirking Fred propped his forearms on the bar counter leaning towards him, his brother
mimicking the position but with his back towards Harry. It struck him then that the twins for
all their flirtatious antics were very much aware of their surroundings. They knew exactly
where they were and how the appearance of obvious Light wizards (Weasley’s simply are too
recognisable) might be received.
“What are you doing here?” Harry hissed at them, trying to keep his voice as soft as possible
yet still conveying his ire.
What the twins were doing was simply irresponsible and dumb and so bloody dangerous!
Didn’t they realise that even though they might watch their backs, it didn’t truly matter with
most of the more menacing patrons here?! They didn’t need to be Death Eaters to be a threat!
These people visited a pub at Knockturn for a reason and George and Fred were part of a
very well-known family. A family famous for their steadfast opposition to anything Dark.
And according to Knockturn grapevine the Ministry’s stand at the moment basically called
anything not a full-blooded, Light-oriented human, Dark! Seriously, Kingsley needed to get
his wits together.
“Well,” Fred drawled, eying what he could make out of Harry’s legs from his position.
“…thought that would be obvious,” George added, throwing a casual wink over his shoulder
before resuming his watching of the parlour.
“…considering you’re here,” Fred continued, letting his smouldering gaze drag from Harry’s
legs upwards along his nicely accentuated waist and over the modestly high-necked collar of
his new summer dress. “Didn’t we promise a visit to our beloved ‘waitress with the kick-ass
reputation’?”
Harry’s answering glower didn’t seem to have the desired effect as he could clearly see
Fred’s lips twitch in amusement… Speaking of, he had never noticed the sensual tilt to those
lips before, how strange, had they always looked like this? Always been shaped so… so…
Blinking rapidly when he caught up with his ridiculous thoughts, Harry forced himself to

concentrate on the words actually falling from those sensual, luscious, tempting… errr…
from Fred’s completely normal and very ordinary lips.
“Is it really all that surprising that we simply enjoy being with you?”
Before Harry could react to that outrageous statement, George added his two Knuts as he
turned to give the disguised boy his own once-over. Though, maybe that should be called a
twice-over as his leering gaze dragged itself from the crown of Harry’s head down to his feet
encased in the aforementioned Dr. Martens boots – and back up again.
“That and we really wanted to see what you made of those outfits we went shopping for the
other day.” He smirked at Harry’s continued fumbling for words and added: “I see you
couldn’t resist the boots.”
“They are awesome, aren’t they?” Fred cheerily, and quite frankly way too excitedly for the
topic, blurted as he leaned forwards and overtop the counter to look at Harry’s feet.
The gleeful excitement finally had Harry snapping back into present, his heart rate calming
down to somewhat normal, though he could still feel the adrenaline rushing in his veins.
What did Fred mean ‘they simply enjoyed being with him’? If that didn’t sound like the
setting of some elaborate prank... No, no he couldn’t think like this. They wouldn’t do
something like this to him. Still, the twins really shouldn’t have come here“And just what are you?”
The voice of Fergus Wanlockhead was dark and menacing as he loomed over the two
Weasleys from behind. Even though the twins had grown quite tall over the last summer,
Fergus still had enough inches on them that they would have to look up at him.
Up close as he was Harry watched the slight widening of Fred and George’s eyes when they
realised, they had presented the whole of the pub their unprotected back for the last few
minutes. Well, Harry would have warned them or even protected them, if anyone had tried
anything – but they couldn’t know that for sure. After all, he was just sweet little Harley to
them. As they turned, Harry had trouble keeping a straight face. Fergus’ generally forbidding
presence wasn’t made any less intimidating by the dark circles under his eyes. Those, as well
as a fierce headache causing a perpetual frown, were remnants of his latest hangover… but
the twins didn’t know that either.
“Good sir!” Fred greeted jovially, but Harry knew him too well to be fooled.
“A pleasant evening, isn’t it?” George added in a similar tone of voice, but again, Harry
heard more than the light-hearted front.
The owner of Bandy-Legs eyed the redheads harassing his waitress with furrowed brows.
They didn’t look like the general folk frequenting his pub, but Harry knew the man would
never dismiss a possible threat on grounds of looks. No, their appearance might only be
coincidence, but their behaviour towards Harry was very much suspicious considering
‘Harley’ had never received any visits before. So, instead of verbally acknowledging the
twins, Fergus Wanlockhead made an abrupt gesture with his hands and the wards on the pub

froze the two interlopers in place. Then he glared at Harry with a raised eyebrow that was as
much questioning as it was challenging. Were the two bothering him or could he vouch for
them?
Staring back at his boss a little wide-eyed, Harry made a split-second decision. No, neither of
the twins was bothering him in the way Fergus thought they might. But could he really vouch
Fred and George wouldn’t, in whatever way, disrupt the goings-on at Bandy-Legs?
Well, these were the infamous Weasley twins, pranksters supreme, so, nooo… he could not
promise they would behave. But… really, if any of the patrons got caught in a prank, in a
place like this, it was their own damn fault. Harry, as an employee, might be moderately safe
inside the wards, but as previous encounters had shown, he still needed to defend himself
from anything physical. Anyone not keyed into the wards was fair game. And everyone knew
that.
So, Harry shrugged and subtly shook his head, indicating just as silently that George and Fred
were not a threat. At least not to him, anyway. With a grunt of his boss Harry watched
movement return to his friends and was half expecting disaster. But, very much to his
surprise, the twins simply rolled their shoulders experimentally before formally addressing
one Fergus Wanlockhead.
“We apologise for any unintentional…,” Fred started.
“…disruption of your établissement,” George continued and Harry goggled slightly at the
unexpectedly accurate pronunciation of a French term. “We certainly did not intent…”
“…to cause undue reservations,” Fred ended and Harry felt like groaning at the very
intentional emphasize on certain words. He could already smell mischief. Again.
Fergus Wanlockhead stared at the two redheaded terrors for a moment longer, before snorting
in what might have been disgusted amusement.
“Get a drink,” he advised them gruffly and added: “We don’t do on the house.”
That said, the owner of Bandy-Legs turned and left to his favourite gambling table further in
the back. Harry could feel the magical presences of some of the more notorious patrons back
there and decided to go round for drink orders in a few minutes.
“What a courteous chap,” George mused, turning back to Harry, though his eyes followed
Fergus across the parlour.
“Quite,” Harry drawled, and then sighed. “Alright, spill. What’s this about?”
Faking hurt, Fred dramatically clutched at his chest, and mimicked falling onto one of the bar
stools. George, though, finally looked back at Harry, a thoughtful little crease appearing
between his brows. For a second he felt like the proverbial potion ingredient under a master’s
inspection, then the moment was gone and George graced him with a warm smile.

“Good evening to you too, Harls. How have you been? I think, two butterbeers will do for
now, thanks.”
Harry glowered again, but then decided George’s smile was disturbing, and turned to get the
ordered beverages.

Of course, it were his werewolf friends that made the situation simultaneously worse and
better. A good hour and a half after George and Fred had arrived, time they exclusively spent
at the bar watching Harry go about his work and chatting with him, the small pack arrived in
their usual boisterous fashion.
“Harley-bunny!”
“Sweetheart of my furry existence!”
“…Firewhiskey, please?”
It was a disaster in slow-motion watching the twins turn and take in the four werewolves just
marching into the pub like they owned the place. Harry could see Fred cock his head, could
make out George raising an eyebrow, then both turned to each other, a silent conversation
obviously taking place. And then their eyes were on him.
Instead of answering the unasked question, Harry quickly concentrated on his other friends,
watching as they sat down at their usual table near the door. He had the firewhiskey out
before either twin could comment and was charming the tumbler glasses spill-safe. Not that
the pub was even half as packed as that first day all those weeks ago when his boss had
intentionally pushed him right into the deep end. But he knew Faolán and his three pack
mates – if anything could end up on the floor, be it the drinks or even one of them, then it
would. (*)
“Who’re your friends, sweet one?” The lone female wolf of the pack, Chloe, questioned as
Harry presented their table with the first round.
“Yes,” George’s voice sounded out of nowhere, causing all of the wolves and Harry to tense
up in surprise, “Who are your friends, sweet one?” He drawled, a strangely dangerous edge to
his voice as his arm casually draped itself around Harry’s waist.
There was a pause. The air felt charged with tension as the two groups scrutinised each other.
Harry wanted to kick George.
“Well,” Faolán, alpha, finally spoke up, “Have a seat, drink with us. Maybe join us for a
game or two.” Then, leaning forward, he added with a smirk: “Say, fancy a flutter?”
That statement set the pace for the rest of the night. The twins started an elaborate game of
maybe-poker with Faolán’s pack, while Harry worked and tried not to think about how much
firewhiskey he had already brought to the table. The twins were hell-bent on keeping up with
the wolves and seemed to completely dismiss the advantage lycanthropy was in this case.

They really should know better. Harry distinctly remembered Sirius trying to out-drink
Remus during a holiday that they had all spent together at Grimmauld Place. The less said
about the result the better, Remus certainly had had fun. Well, at least until the next day when
Sirius wouldn't stop whining at them all.
At one point the six of them migrated to the larger table in the back where Fergus and his
friends were gambling the night away. Bandy-Legs had cleared out by that time, the group in
the back being the last. It meant Harry could clean up the counter and the few tables that had
been occupied through the night, before going back there with more drinks. He attempted to
leave out the twins as he refilled glasses, but as inebriated as they were, Fred and George still
noticed.
“Ah, mo nighean mhaiseach,” old Ruaidh laughed at him as the twins protested loudly,
proclaiming broken hearts dramatically, “Let the lads have some fun.”
Harry wasn’t so sure this was all in good fun, he would have to be blind to not notice the
competitive vibe between Fred, George, and the wolves. His boss and his accomplices…
err… friends simply enjoyed the show, knowing all too well how this would end in the
morning. Now that he thought about it, maybe the twins hadn’t realised who they were
attempting to drink under the table? A tug on his hand just as he was about to turn away had
Harry yelping as he lost his balance. There was laughter and in the next moment he found
himself sitting slightly askew across Fred’s lap.
“Wha-”
Harry was about to protest, for a moment uncomfortably reminded of a similar situation, but
the intense look in Fred’s eyes stopped him in his tracks. The deep blue gaze was slightly
bleary, but there was also something so very gentle there… And was that recognition? No,
no, no that could not be“Be my lucky charm, please?”
Harry blinked. The voice had rumbled in the surprisingly broad chest he was leaning against,
warm arms had instinctively wound their way around him, keeping him as much seated as
they kept him from losing his balance again. He felt the eyes of most of the game’s
participants on him as heat rose high in his cheeks. He darted a quick glance at George, not
sure what he had expected, but it certainly wasn’t the curious mix of barely hidden jealousy
and contentment.
Taking a shaky breath, Harry looked back at Fred and gave him a single nod in answer. And
though Harry submitted to his strangely comfortable fate, trying in vain not to shift too much
in place, he was at all times aware of Fergus Wanlockhead keeping a close eye on Fred’s
hands.
In the small hours of the morning Harry waved goodbye to the swaying forms of Fred and
George, not even wanting to imagine Molly’s reaction to her sons playing whatever in a bar
in Knockturn and getting completely pissed. And Molly Weasley would find out. Yeah, no.
That was not something he wanted to be present for.

“The two fancy-pants left their ridiculous vests,” his boss’ gruff voice spoke up from the
back of the bar.
“Oh, I’ll go. If I hurry, I can still catch them,” Harry decided, already summoning said items.
“Back in a sec!”
And out he was, following after the twins to hand them their garishly coloured clothes.
Seriously, what had those two been thinking when they commissioned these? Because that’s
what the vests had to be, specifically commissioned for the insane Weasley twins. There was
simply no way that had been on sell in an actual store. Then again, this was the Wizarding
World…
The street was very dark. To Harry it had never really sunken in what a difference the lack of
electricity could actually make. Knockturn Alley at night, and he supposed any other magical
district as well, was literally dark as night, or as dark as night should be. Harry had never
truly realised how much light pollution there really was in the Muggle World. The only
reason he could even discern where he was going was because the looming buildings stood in
stark relief against the starlit night sky.
Turning a corner, he still breathed a sigh of relief as a few sparse gas lanterns came into view.
Though, the eerie green light they were emitting was casting ominous flickering shadows
along the cobblestone and towering walls. Alright, this was creepy. Nearly facepalming when
he realised that he could simply use a Lumos, Harry was just about to cast the spell when he
heard it.
Sounds of a struggle, the slurred voices of the twins. He was running before he completely
registered what he was hearing.
“Bloody Gryffindors, think they can just flounce down Knockturn without consequences,” a
harsh voice he didn’t recognise muttered.
“Whatcha think they were doing here?” Another voice, this one slightly higher, answered as
Harry reached the last corner between him and what sounded like an ambush.
He shouldn’t have let George and Fred walk home in the condition they were in. He could
have just as easily painted the targets on their backs himself.
“Don’t care, but I do know they’re gonna regret ever stepping a foot into the district.”
Peeking around the corner, Harry squinted to make out if there were more than the two
assailants he could hear. To his luck the two were either rather dumb or they did not expect
anyone in Knockturn Alley to actually care about what they were doing – they were standing
right in front of one of the eerie lanterns. The struggling heap on the floor had to be the twins,
but he couldn’t see whether they had been spelled that way or if they were simply too drunk
to get up on their own.
“How about we start with some blood burning, cooking you inside out, huh, Weasleys?” One
baited, but whatever the twins answered was drowned out by the other’s question.

“Why not a Crucio?”
“Mate, I don’t want any aurors on our tail!”
“Oh yeah… but I want at least one Entrail-Expelling Curse in there. Let’s see how funny they
think they are when dangling from their intestines.”
The casual way they talked about their plans turned Harry’s stomach. Not wanting to wait for
them to actually start cursing, he acted. It was a Gryffindor move, but there was no time to
plan. One second he was trying to make out his targets, the next he was already moving.
“STUPEFY!” The flash of light arced brightly across the street, hitting the one currently
holding the twins at wandpoint dead on. Harry dived and rolled. “STUPEFY!” And there
went the second.
Staying crouched for a second longer, Harry eyed the downed men suspiciously. That... had
been too easy. But they were properly stunned, not moving an inch, and the twins were still
struggling to get up.
So, Harry carefully made his way over, wand at the ready, but everything seemed to be clear.
One last glance and he kneeled down to assess what was wrong with Fred and George. They
were bound with conjured rope that he easily cut through, listening to the twins curse up a
storm. A slightly trembling breath left him. He wasn’t sure they had even truly
comprehended what had just nearly happened.
Heaving George to his feet, Harry suddenly felt it.
But it was already too late.
His last thought before everything went dark had him wondering how by Merlin’s saggy third
ball he ended up in this strange situation of reversed damsel in distress…

Chapter 17: Wa(n)king
Chapter Notes

(you have no idea how often I checked that I didn't misspell the title... until I just gave in
