See the end of the chapter for more notes

The first thing he noticed was the scent.
Breathing in, Harry could tell he was not in his bed at Bandy-Legs, not on his cot at Privet
Drive, not in the hospital wing of Hogwarts, and also not in his four-poster bed in the
Gryffindor dormitory. A small voice in the back of his head also assured him that he was not
stuffed away in a dank and dark cupboard.
Keeping his breathing even and his body relaxed, Harry tried to identify his surroundings and
whether he was alone or not. Listening closely, he couldn’t hear anything moving at least, but
the scent kept pushing to the forefront of his mind. On closer inspection it wasn’t foreign, he
just couldn’t place it in his sleep-addled state. It was also rather nice actually… Mentally
shaking his head, he slowly spread his magic around the room, feeling along furniture and
walls, ‘tasting’ in a way that made no sense whenever he tried to put it into words. It was not
something he would have been able to do before this summer. When Harry felt no other
magical presences close by, he finally dared to open his eyes.
He was in a bedroom. Placed in the middle of an enormous, and surprisingly modern, bed.
This was not the style he was used to seeing in the Wizarding World, which was unsettling in
its own way andWHY ARE YOU WORRYING ABOUT YOU-KNOW-WHO?
-flashed in the corner of his eye and had Harry whipping his head around.
YOU SHOULD BE WORRYING ABOUT U-NO-POO THE CONSTIPATION SENSATION THAT'S GRIPPING THE NATION
What.
And suddenly it all came back: The twins visiting Bandy-Legs and getting so utterly pissed
they barely could walk by the time they finally left. Harry running after them because they
had left their ridiculous, and probably ridiculously expensive, vests behind. The ambush.
Harry stunning the attackers and then… then what? He remembered the sudden flare
registering on his magical radar, honed by his time getting along half-blind. But it had
already been too late and the twins had been too drunk to shield and… and some spell must
have hit him from behind.

Glancing back at the flashing, and very magical, advertisement, Harry suddenly had an
inkling just where he was. There weren’t many people around with this specific brand of
insanity errr… humour.
Getting up from the bed that had to belong to Fred or George, Harry patted himself down,
noting the horrendous state of his once-pretty summer dress. The skirt was ripped at one side
and there were large dirt marks from his dive-and-roll around the street. The sight nearly
brought tears to his eyes, but he clamped down on the urge to immediately try and save the
garment. First his wand, then the twins, then whatever came next.
Resolutely ignoring his state of dress, Harry followed the tug of his magic and found his
wand on the bedside table – stumbling over his shoes on the way. So obviously someone,
hopefully the twins, had brought him here, had taken enough care to put him to bed and take
his shoes off. He could only hope they hadn’t tried to make him comfier otherwise…
That taken care of, he braced himself and peeked out of the only door he could find. It led
into a moderately sized …war zone? No, no he supposed this was meant to be a living room
as there was a comfy looking couch, but holy Kneazle-poop, this was a complete hazard of a
living space!
Oh and there were the twins, snoring away in a jumble of limbs on the aforementioned couch.
Daring himself to step out into the utterly cluttered space, Harry took a more careful look
around now that he knew where Fred and George were at. Squinting past all the odds and
ends and stacked boxes, he made out rather nice wood flooring and walls painted in a pale
sea green. There was also a fireplace half buried beneath soot, what appeared to be a
bathroom next to the bedroom, and a small arch leading into a kitchen that was giving off a
very questionable smell. At the other end of the room he thought he could make out one more
door, but his eyes weren’t up to the challenge and he wasn’t sure traipsing through this mess
on his own would be wise.
This had to be the twins’ flat, 93 Diagon Alley: Weasley’s Wizard Wheezes, their joke shop.
Well, the shop would be on the ground floor. George and Fred had spent hours talking to
him… no, Harley about their store, but it hadn’t fully sunken in what it all meant. He knew
they had become quite successful with WWW, had even heard them talk about it to the
Goblins back at Gringotts. Yet it took actually standing in the midst of it to realise how far
those two had come. They were independent, free. …And they really had no housekeeping
skills whatsoever.
Glancing over at the still sleeping twins, Harry chewed on his lower lip nervously. He had no
idea what happened after he was hit with whatever spell knocked him out, but it looked like
Fred and George had taken care of everything and brought him here. He felt fine at least.
They even let him sleep in their bed and- Wait, was this the only bedroom?
Did that mean…
Blushing furiously, Harry turned and slipped into the ominously smelling kitchen. He
remembered all too well how much the twins had consumed last night, and also quite clearly
recalled the memory of the massive hangover Sirius once had nursed after attempting to drink
Remus under the table. There is no such thing as outdrinking werewolves. Stupid.

So he would let them sleep for now and maybe prepare a light breakfast as thanks for last
night- The kitchen was filthy. He wasn’t even going to try and identify what was growing on
the counter or living in the various cupboards. And he certainly was not even going to look at
that strange blob in the sink. Nope.
Drawing his wand and wishing for the awesome cleaning stuff he used at Bandy-Legs, Harry
went about vanishing everything that should not inhabit a kitchen. He was quite sure the
fungus beneath the small table had eyes, and couldn’t get the Scourgify out nearly fast enough
to get rid of any remnants. Mentally shoving up his non-existent sleeves, he worked his way
around the room, making liberal use of all the nifty spells he had picked up in the last weeks.
Only when he felt he could move around without anything trying to bite his ankles (or
growing on them, who knew with the twins), did Harry stop to think about his next steps.
There wasn’t much in the lonely cooling cupboard that looked still edible. In contrast to what
he thought used to be a couple apples, the eggs and flour seemed surprisingly save, as did the
sugar and salt. Although he wondered what most of these items were meant to do under
cooling charms. Now, if he could only get his hands on some milk…
As if on cue a chime sounded, startling Harry enough to nearly drop the stack of bowls he
had been inspecting. The aforementioned cooling cupboard flashed once and everything went
quiet again. Huh. His wand drawn and expecting the worst – who knew what concoctions and
experiments ran rampant here – he opened the door again. And blinked at the fresh jug of
milk. Well, if there were house-elves acting as laundry service, then why not have milk
magically delivered into your cupboard?

Some time later Harry was deeply immersed in making an ever-growing stack of pancakes,
his thoughts aimlessly drifting over all that had happened these last weeks leading up to him
standing in the Weasley twins’ kitchen making breakfast.
“Dumbledore’s farts, I’m having that dream again,” a sleep-roughened voice near-moaned
from behind him.
Jumping in place, shooting the latest pancake out of the pan right at the ceiling in a bout of
startled accidental magic, Harry spun in place. There was George, bare-chested and rumpled,
standing in the doorway. Harry could make out Fred just getting up from the couch himself,
groaning and holding his head in clear indication of a headache.
George stared a moment longer, eyes slightly unfocused yet very much riveted on Harry.
Maybe he was still half-convinced this was a dream, though why he would be dreaming of
Harry in his kitchen... He could feel himself colouring deeply when Fred turned into the
room, stopping at his brother’s shoulder in confusion, and then catching sight of Harry. There
was a stretched moment of silence, the air charged with… something.
SPLAT!
The pancake peeled itself off the ceiling and landed between the three staring parties.

“That’s the dream you’ve been going on about, brother-dearest?” Fred burst out laughing, his
voice reminding Harry of sandpaper it was so rough from last night’s shenanigans. “There
something you wanna tell me about that food kink?”
It wasn’t only Harry flushing at the statement, though he doubted George was blushing for
the same reasons. Seriously, no one’s voice should be allowed to sound so incredibly sexy
and the way those pyjama pants were riding low on freckled hips-… Suddenly he realised
what he was thinking and his heartrate spiked. How could he even entertain thoughts like
that? No matter how nice the two had been to Harley, they were operating on completely
wrong information. He was not the slightly mysterious waitress from a shady pub down
Knockturn Alley. He was not the fun-loving girl they liked to spend the day with, he was not
a girl at all even and… Harry jumped into motion, rushing to clean up the mess he had made
to escape his thoughts. The absurd urge to burst into tears yet again only added to his
bewildered uncertainty of the situation.
“Woo, hey,” Fred cried in surprise as Harry nearly skidded to his knees, completely forgoing
his wand as he tried to get the pancake off of the floor. “There’s no need for that… hey…”
Pushing passed his still slightly stunned brother, Fred crouched to Harry’s level and caught
his scrambling hands in his.
Harry’s breath hitched. When had it happened, when had he started to allow himself to think
of the twins in such a way? They would drop him the moment they realised the truth, or
probably even before that. He wouldn’t be the shiny new toy forever, at some point they
would have to realise how truly messed up and plain boring he was.
“Hey, Harley?” Fred tried to catch his gaze, but Harry wouldn’t meet his eyes. “Ignore my
loud mouth, please. Don’t worry over any strange things I might say, I’m just used to acting
the clown. Normally it puts people at ease, you know? Please, just… you don’t have to do
this, okay? Come on, have a seat at the table. Okay?”
It took a moment for Harry to realise Fred was babbling. It took a second longer to calm his
rapid heartbeat enough to realise that now both their hands were filled with pancake mush
and Fred was looking at him in apparent concern. There was something self-deprecating
taking root on the twin’s face and Harry didn’t like it. Fred and George Weasley should never
look like they doubted themselves, like they weren’t the most amazing kind of people.
Finally he nodded and let himself be guided to the small table in the kitchen. There he let
Fred further direct him to sit and even let him clean up their hands. Out of the corner of his
eye he saw George moving over to the stove, dousing the fire and bringing over the heaping
plate of finished pancakes. He was gifted one of George’s special reassuring smiles and asked
if he wanted coffee or tea. He hadn’t ever truly tried coffee and said so.
“Blasphemy!” They chorused, making him honest-to-Merlin giggle... utter a very manly
chuckle that was only disguised as a giggle because he was currently Harley.
“How very beastly of you,” Fred winked and got up to put on the kettle. “Then you simply
have to try this blend. Lee got us hooked when he brought it back from vacation in Ethiopia,”

he exclaimed and came back over to the table to hold out a jar full of whole coffee beans
towards Harry. “Take a whiff,” he urged.
Eying the dark beans for a moment, Harry mentally shrugged and complied. He blinked. That
smelled… pleasant. Tart and a touch bitter, but earthy as well and… was that a hint of
chocolate? He hadn’t realised his eyes were closed until he snapped them back open at the
twins’ snickering. Flushing yet again, Harry pulled away from the coffee.
“That,” George stated, eyes roving over Harry’s face with a somewhat hungry gaze, “Has got
to be the cutest reaction to coffee I’ve ever seen.”
“It just smells nice,” Harry muttered embarrassed, gaze going back to where Fred was filling
some of the beans into… what was that?
“A grinder specifically for coffee,” said twin explained and Harry realised he must have
asked out loud. “See that crank? It kind of activates a grinder in there and voilà, ground
coffee at your service. Though, I hear some muggles buy it already ground,” he added with a
shrug.
Harry watched with raised eyebrows as Fred went about explaining the coffee grinder and
making up three cups with a flourish. There was something about the whole act of brewing
coffee by hand, the preparation, the feeling the smell invoked, that called to Harry. How
strange. The Dursley's were all for pretending to favour tea as the apparently more
sophisticated option, never even bothered with so much as a coffee maker. Although Harry
suspected Vernon bought some on the go to work and he knew Aunt Petunia had a little jar of
instant coffee hidden in the back of a kitchen cabinet.
This, the whole procedure, even leaving out Fred's obvious dramatics, reminded him of the
affected tea making ritual his aunt would fall into when guests were around. Affected as in
the way she went about it, traditional… he wasn’t quite sure what feeling he was looking for.
Ceremonial maybe, but in Fred’s case it just felt like he was being extra to make Harry feel
special not to make himself look a certain way.
Then, over a breakfast of pancakes and hangover potion, the twins had him trying coffee in
different versions (pure, with milk, with sugar, with both, and even once with honey) until
Harry felt a very pressing need make itself known.
“You liked that?” George questioned suggestively as Harry sat down his empty cup with a
sigh.
“Very,” he agreed, wiggling slightly in place. He definitely needed the loo now.
“Then it’s good that you know us,” Fred wiggled his eyebrows in a way that gave Harry a
headache from looking at it. “You won’t find coffee in the Wizarding World, at least not in
Britain. Old farts all stuck in their way, they are,” he nodded sagely, before continuing:
“There’s even a legend to this specific blend of coffee… think it had something to do with a
king, a goat, and something called monks, right, Gred?”

But George wasn’t really paying attention to his twin, a fact that had Fred do a double take
before he realised what had caught his brother’s attention: Harry was shifting in place, eying
the arch leading towards the living room. There was a faint blush covering the bridge of his
nose as he nibbled on his lip.
He really, really needed the loo, but couldn’t bring himself to ask. It wasn’t as if he had any
experience in visiting a friend’s house! Sure, he had spent time at the Weasley’s before and
obviously used their bathroom, but this was different. He wasn’t currently living here and
somehow Harry thought there might be social cues he had to adhere to. Was it okay to simply
use the loo? Should he ask for permission first? But, asking for permission to use the loo felt
too much like school, and the twins certainly weren’t adults he was obligated to treat as
superiors, and…
“Harley?” His head snapped around to look at George and he was met with amused blue
eyes. “The bathroom’s the door next to the bedroom.”
Harry flushed even further. There was his answer, but now he felt incredibly stupid.
“Actually,” Fred interjected, eying Harry’s state of dress, “You should take a shower. Those
jerks ruined your pretty dress, but I’ll borrow you a jumper of mine, yeah?” This time the
wiggle of his eyebrows was all but lewd and Harry stared for a moment too long.
“Um, yeah… thanks?” He finally managed to peep, suddenly noting the frown George was
aiming at his brother. “I- I’ll just go now, okay? Okay.”
That said, Harry was out of the kitchen and in the bathroom fast enough to make himself
slightly dizzy. He closed the door and locked it securely - not that that would do much
without magic, but he trusted the twins. Well. He trusted them to have enough manners not to
barge in on who they thought was a girl while showering. Molly Weasley would have her
sons’ heads, if they ever dared something like that.
Right. Calm breaths.
Taking care of his business first, and avidly listening for any sign that the twins might not be
as honourable as he pegged them to be, Harry carefully took stock of his body. In the
confusion of waking up in an unexpected place, falling into the ingrained routine of cleaning
and cooking, and then being confronted with the lovely insanity that was Fred and George, he
had conveniently pushed aside any thoughts about why he was in this situation in the first
place. He hadn’t even asked the brothers what had happened after he had been hit with that
spell! The spell… Now that he thought about it, there was a twinge in his back.
Shedding the sad remnants of his summer dress, Harry turned to look at his back in the (very
nice indeed) floor-to-ceiling mirror on one wall. His skin was grimy in the places that had
touched the cobblestone street, excluding his hands. It was another ingrained habit to always,
and very meticulously, wash his hands before starting anything in the kitchen. Harry spared a
moment for a sarcastic little mental thanks to his neurotic aunt. But beneath the grime there
was quite the nasty bruise located on his lower back, spreading rather spectacularly up
towards his shoulder blades. Harry pinched the bridge of his nose. He hadn’t even noticed the

bruise before, but now that he was aware of it, there would be no ignoring the constant
twinges. Sure, he had had worse by far. But that didn’t mean he wanted to be in pain.

There was a shampoo potion in the shower that was apparently geared towards red hair. He
hadn’t known that was a thing, or even that one needed to differentiate between colours when
it came to hair care, but oh well. It was the only shampoo available to him right now and
there was no way he would go back to using soap on his hair. Madam Primpernelle would
suffer an aneurysm if he dared (and maybe, just maybe, he had become a little too vain for
that). The potion smelled like the bed he had woken up in, just without the musky note that
had lingered on the pillows. Harry blushed. This was how the twins always smelled and he
had come to very much like this scent.
Breathing deeply, Harry turned his face into the spray of water, enjoying the heat and steam
for long moments. He was at George and Fred’s flat, in their shower, using their shampoo,
and… well, the water really was very nice…
Harry’s mind started to wander, the intoxicating scent of the twins lingering around him. He
had woken up in a bed large enough to house more than one person easily… did they share?
Why would they? Interesting images rose from the recesses of his mind, thoughts circling
and questions giving way to indulgent fantasies. It wasn’t long before the rest of his body
took an avid interest. Drifting along with his fantasies, cushioned by the hot steam of the
shower, Harry let his hands wander with his thoughts…
Minutes later found him blushing furiously as he panted in the afterglow of the impromptu
wank. As he watched certain proof vanish down the drain, Harry had to admit to himself that
his feelings for the twins were a lost cause. He could not deny it any longer. No one had ever
made him feel the way Fred and George did, no one had ever invoked such reactions either.
Sure, as a teenage boy he had had wet dreams, had jerked himself off from time to time, but it
had never been to the thoughts of actual people. To be completely honest, he didn’t think he
had ever done anything else than concentrate on the feeling of his own hand. It wasn’t like it
took all that long to get off either, he was only 16 after all.

Chapter End Notes

Long author note of doom:
You might have noticed that the story isn’t tagged explicitly as incest, though the
relationship tag suggests as much. I’m actually on the fence with this issue.
It’s a story, not reality, so no children would ever suffer from the consequences of an
incestous union even if we assume that magic makes mpreg possible. (And if there were
children, in my storyverse magic would have a safeguard that would only fail after
generations of inbreeding, see the Blacks etc.)
I was originally going with a constellation that would have both Fred and George have a
romantic relationship with Harry, but not with each other. They would be in a triad, so if
any intercourse occurs, all three would likely be present, but the twins would not do
anything with each other directly.
…Then Harry’s little fantasy popped up and I was wondering whether the twins would
be willing to act out some scenes for Harry, even though they normally wouldn’t be with
each other in that way. They are obviously very close so far, but not technically
romantically so.
As I said, I’m on the fence. What are your thoughts on the matter?

Arc II : Body

Fred had caught out of the small enclosure was sitting.
“Making itself at home,” he chuckled, “Sure looks cozy.”
He watched Harley go cross-eyed as she reached up to locate the tiny fuzz ball. The
expression, like so many other things, was heart-wrenchingly familiar, reminiscent of Harry
in such an obvious way… there just had to be a blood relation! That realisation and the
sudden soft golden shimmer emitting from Harley sealed the deal for Fred. He knew that
magic, he knew the way it made emerald eyes glow“What-” Harley had finally located the fuzz ball on her head and brought it down to gently
hold it in her cupped hands. “Wasn’t it blue just a second ago?”
Indeed, the Pygmy Puff had changed its shade for reasons that were currently eluding him,
his thoughts coalescing into one burning need to know right then and there:
“Do you know Harry Potter?”
Fred abruptly blurted, colouring as he caught sight of George face-palming in the
background. Yeah, that had not exactly come out the way he planned. If he had planned it in
the first place. Sue him, he was a Godric forsaken Gryffindor!
“I- wha-,” Harley spluttered, wide-eyed and suddenly pale. What a strange reaction.
There was a beat of silence, then:
“…need to go,” she added in a rush and just like that she had pushed her way out of the
backdoor of the store.
Out of the formerly magically closed and hidden door. Well, if she was related to Harry
Potter, small incidents of wandless magic like this were to be expected. Unless…
The door bounced off of the shop’s outer wall and fell shut behind him with a loud THUD!
Not that Harry really heard it.
Why? Why did the day have to turn like this? Everything had started with him waking up in
the twins’ bedroom, weird- but not all that strange for Harry who had probably become too
used to waking up unexpected places after bouts of unconsciousness. Then the morning had
been… He felt himself blush as he hurried down the narrow alley behind the building. The
morning had been a rush of warm and surprising feelings, the twins so gentle with him, so
comforting in their own unique way. That is, until Mrs. Weasley had turned up. He shuddered
at the memory of the woman sneering at him. The Weasley matriarch was probably the
closest Harry had ever come to a mother figure ever since his birth mother had died. And
now she… no. She didn’t know Harley was Harry.
Not yet anyway.

With a sigh Harry stopped at an intersection, leaning against an old brick wall to catch his
breath. No, Mrs. Weasley hadn’t known who Harley was, but she had instantly disliked ‘her’.
And now the twins had made the connection. Harry had known this moment would come,
had known he couldn’t play them forever, but… It had been a dream, he supposed. What else
really was being Harley anyway? He was hiding away from reality, living the life of someone
else. Harry Potter was not meant to have the simple life of Harley, the freedom of anonymity.
But oh did he want it.
A tiny squeak had him glancing down. The Pygmy Puff was looking up at him out of
disproportionally big and glossy eyes from where Harry had squished it to his chest. It really
had changed colours, the pretty cornflower blue now accompanied by shades ranging from a
soft powder pink over strong red and orange, yellow, green, purple and darker hues of blue. It
was a veritable rainbow and the sight had a wan smile tug at his mouth for a moment.
He sighed. He would have to go back at some point and either pay for the fluff ball or give it
back. If he was still welcome there. Maybe he should just send Hedwig with some
Galleons… He had lied to them, repeatedly. He felt like he, as Harley, had used Fred and
George, no matter how much the two had assured him they enjoyed helping him out. Or
rather ‘her’. Harry buried his face into the soft fur of his Pygmy Puff and groaned. This was
all such a mess.
A clearly audible, disapproving sniff had him blinking and looking up again.
As much as his blurry sight might work against him, Harry was pretty sure he was not where
he was supposed to be. Standing at an intersection, just beyond what appeared to be a broader
road, he just knew the backdoor of the twins’ shop should not have led him here. And he
didn’t even have to hazard a guess who was so clearly disapproving of him – yet again - as
the tall and elderly witch was still standing at the small side-alley’s mouth, staring down her
nose at him. Glancing passed her, he squinted. The manner in which the people blobs moved,
the way their magic thrummed with a sense of entitlement and superiority, ruled certain areas
out. This was… Horizont Alley? How had he ended up here?
Though Harry couldn’t quite fathom how the backdoor of the twins’ shop on Diagon could
spit him out on Horizont (magic?), it really didn’t matter in that moment. He hugged the
rainbow puff close, half hiding the tiny one in the overlong sleeve of the jumper he was still
wearing in lieu of his summer dress, and moved on. Harry ignored the indignant lady, having
had enough of outraged reactions to his appearance and whatnot. Still, the further along the
street he moved, the more Harry became aware of how much he was standing out. Wizarding
fashion was absolutely prevalent on Horizont. He was acutely aware of his underdressed state
of jumper-dress and still wet hair, and it didn’t help his unease that his eyesight was once
more turning him around.
He had no idea in which direction Knockturn was supposed to be. Knockturn and BandyLegs and the safety of his small room hidden away in Fergus Wanlockhead’s flat.
“Forge?”
“Yes, Gred?”

“Do you think-”
“It’s as I said, brother mine: No ordinary girl could possibly be this mesmerizing.”
“But-”
“I don’t know. So let’s find out.”
“Yeah, let’s.”
The flicker of sinister magic, crackling around the person blob it belonged to, caught Harry’s
attention. He knew that magic. And even though the person he knew it belonged to wasn’t
someone he fancied meeting again, or, at the least not in his current get-up, he turned and
started following behind.
Following Sanguini’s magic through the throng of people out in the magical district was
much easier than one would expect. As he knew from his time at Bandy-Legs, a vampire’s
magic was just too different to be confused with that of an ordinary wizard. Although, at least
according to his DADA books, vampires had very little active magic left after their
transformation, they still had an inherent magic that came with the very being they turned
into when… turned. They were, after all, the walking dead, but not in a zombie-ish way. No
decay, no groaning, no loss of self. Vampires were frozen in time. Well, at least their bodies
were. If anything, their characteristics became emphasized, though that might just be because
living for centuries was bound to make even the most boring person somewhat eccentric.
Either way, as a vampire Sanguini’s magic stood out quite starkly against the mellow
wizarding signatures all around, making it easy to follow. Harry had been turned around so
much, eyes weary from trying to place vague structures of buildings while ignoring the
flickering of so many different magical auras, that he was simply glad to have something to
concentrate on. Now he could only hope the vampire really was on his way to Knockturn. If
Harry could just reach familiar ground, he would be alright.
He was ducking around a person blob, following the vampire’s magic to an intersection- and
there was Knockturn. Harry breathed a sigh of relief as the looming darkness ahead
registered. It wasn’t the passage from Diagon, but what mattered was that he finally
recognised his surroundings. The day had already been long and it wasn’t even midday yet.
“Silverware! Talismans! You need a protective amulet, Sir? Works wonders against those
pimples for sure. No? How about a charm to attract the ladies then?”
The vaguely familiar voice broke him out of his concentrated pursuit of the vampire’s magic.
Frowning, Harry turned towards the incessant teller’s advertising… only to come across
something, he had so hoped to not ever cross paths with while Harley. Again.
Mundungus Fletcher was standing at a dingy little stall, loudly praising the wares he had
piled in front of him… and there was a flicker. A tiny, smudgy little flicker of familiar magic,
no, Family Magic, clinging to the odds and ends spread out on a rather stained tarp.
Narrowing his eyes, Harry tried to pin the fluttering magic down, to identify why it felt so..

so… Distracted as Harry was, he hadn’t noticed how his feet unconsciously stepped closer
until Fletcher’s voice once more broke through his concentration.
“Ah the silver be pretty, eh, lil’ missy? Might wanna have a look at the medallions, no?”
Harry glanced up at the man, not sure what he had expected. It was obvious the Order
member didn’t recogn- He knew that coat of arms. That, right there engraved on the
silverware and each of the sophisticated jewellery pieces, was the Black coat of arms. Waswas Mundungus Fletcher, the seedy man Mrs. Weasley had tried to get out of the house at
Grimmauld but who Sirius personally had fought with her over, trying to sell Black artefacts?
The anger was hot and fast and just there. It rushed through his veins, narrowing his vision
down to just the unshaven face of Mundungus Fletcher. How dare he? How dare he do that to
Sirius?! How dare he act like- like- …like Sirius was dead and gone and“See, this right here works wonders keeping the nasties at bay,” Fletcher nearly crooned at
Harry, eying the hem of his jumper-dress distractedly. “Wouldn’t want a beast like a
werewolf to catch you unawares, no?”
“BULLSHIT!” Harry finally burst out, way louder than he had intended to.
But there was just NO WAY he was going to stand here listening to this two-faced thief
telling lies to sell off Sirius’ household, his family’s legacy! And defaming werewolves when
Harry only ever met the exact opposite of what this disgusting bleeder was fabricating! Harry
felt his magic surging, crackling. With a hissing sound the tarp covering the stall suddenly
caught fire, flames lurching up and making Mundungus jump back with a shout.
“Watch it!” The man yelled, fumbling for his wand to put the flames out.
Harry sucked in a breath.
He was making a scene.
Shit. Crap! Stepping back from the affronted and somewhat scared looking Fletcher, Harry
whirled around and hurried into Knockturn, not sparing a single glance back. He had drawn
too much attention. This was not good. What if the thief had recognised his voice? What ifAnd Harry had lost sight of the vampire as well. Not that it mattered now, he knew well
enough where he was at.
Taking a shaky breath once he had rounded a corner, Harry felt his lips twitch in a bout of
self-mockery, allowing the irony of the situation to rule his thoughts for a few moments. Here
he was, Harry Potter, Light poster boy, knowing his way around Knockturn so well he could
navigate the alley nearly blind as he was, yet he got turned around in ‘perfectly respectable’
streets. Not to mention how much more at home he felt here in the shadows in contrast to the
entitled environment of Horizont. Fletcher had caught him off guard. A lot had taken him by
surprise today already. Setting flames to a hawker’s stall…

Dashing sudden tears away carelessly, Harry resolutely continued on his way. If the press
ever got wind of this, any of this really, he would be proclaimed the next dark lord long
before he could step on Rita Skeeter’s scuttling bug-arse.
Bandy-Legs was empty when he climbed the… bandy-legged.. stairs and stepped into the
parlour. None of the lights were lit yet and as the relatively cool air surrounded him, Harry
felt like an invisible pressure had been taken off of his lungs. Already the sun was baking the
streets and he didn’t even want to imagine the temperatures outside the magical district with
its somewhat regulating wards.
“So you’ve found your way back,” the voice of his boss came from the murky depths of the
bar, reaching Harry’s ears just a second before his eyes made out the movement of the
hulking figure.
It was a little disconcerting that he hadn’t immediately caught onto Fergus Wanlockhead’s
presence with his new magical sense, but Harry put it down to being distracted and feeling
safe in this place. Still, now that he was actually here, Harry realised with a start that the last
his boss had seen of him was when he had called out about running after the twins. Harry
thought he remembered saying something along the lines of being back in a few moments.
“There was talk ‘bout a scuffle,” Fergus continued, leaning against the counter, and folding
his arms over his broad chest. “But not the usual,” he added and Harry wasn’t sure about the
emotion he could detect in the rumbling voice.
With everything that had already happened this morning, it took Harry a moment to truly
remember why he had woken at the twins’ place. Right. He- no, Fred and George had been
attacked in Knockturn on their way back home. He told his boss as much while carefully
manoeuvring a path through the parlour without knocking into anything.
“Shouldn’t have tried to out drink the wolves,” Fergus scoffed, but then he frowned at Harry.
“And you shouldn’t have run after them on your own, brat. Going for groceries in the middle
of the day and running out at night are two sides of a Quidditch field. On both occasions you
willingly walk into danger, but there’s a difference between having the sun at your back and
having no light at all.”
"Or too much of it, for that matter," Harry muttered under his breath, memories of playing on
the wrong side of the Quidditch pitch and basically being blinded by the sun surfacing.
Then he blinked as Fergus’ words truly registered. It took him another second to puzzle
through the slightly convoluted… wizarding saying? Harry wasn’t quite sure if his boss had
just quoted something or simply made that up on the fly…
“For fuck‘s sake, Harley!” Fergus Wanlockhead suddenly burst out, making Harry flinch in
surprise. Never before had his boss even raised his voice against him. “You ran out in the
middle of the night to catch up to some drunkards! You could have waited for the morrow or
just let the stupid fucks get their shit themselves! And don’t give me that look, I can still
bloody see the magical residue clinging to you!”

The silence after the man’s tirade was deafening and only interspersed with Fergus’ heavy
breathing. He was staring past Harry and into the shadowy depths of his pub, a dark frown
marring his stoic features. And Harry, after his rapid heartbeat calmed down enough to truly
take in what his boss was saying, felt guilty. He had acted a Gryffindor, yet again, and
worried someone who had taken him in, provided him a place to stay, food, work“Where did it hit you?”
“Huh?” Broken out of his self-deprecating thoughts, Harry was baffled that after all this
Fergus was still concerning himself with Harry. “I- I’m fine, it probably was only a Stunner,”
he hurried to explain, unsure why those dark eyes were scrutinising him once again.
Fergus gave an aggravated sigh.
“No. The dark spell, where did it hit you? I can see the magical residue clinging to you, but
it’s all fuzzy. Now. Where?”
Wait. Fergus could see magic? Like… like Harry could ever since he lost his glasses? Why
had he never said anything? Then again, Harry himself hadn’t exactly been forthcoming with
information about his own ability. It had just been so weird! Then the rest of the words
registered and Harry felt the ghost of the spell that had knocked him out last night tingle
down his back. It had been easy to ignore, he had had by far worse and was used to work
through pain, but now that he had been reminded of it, he felt the large bruise situated
between his shoulder blades keenly. Still, it was just bruised, really“Harley.” The way his boss said Harry’s assumed name brooked no argument.
“My back,” he muttered a little sullenly, attempting to shrug it off and just ending up wincing
at the move. It wasn’t like he could go see Madam Pomfrey or one of the healers at St.
Mungo’s as Harley and going as Harry was out of the question.
Fergus hummed thoughtfully, walking around Harry slowly, clearly inspecting… something.
If the man truly could see magic, Harry supposed he must have had much more practice for
Harry himself was pretty sure he wouldn’t be able to distinguish between magical residue and
the general magical aura of a person. Although, he had just been able to recognise the Black
Family Magic on those trinkets… Seeing magic was very confusing to him, but to be fair he
hadn’t truly taken the time to search deeper into his sudden ability. It was simply another
strange thing happening to Harry Potter.
“Well…,” Fergus muttered as he came back around to face Harry, “Since the last lunar
celebrations are just done with, the next opportunity will be at the end of September
earliest… but this year’s Mabon is followed by a lunar eclipse.. ah but there’s still a full
moon ahead this month, so maybe…”
Harry watched nonplussed as his boss seemingly rambled to himself. Fergus did that
sometimes and it had taken Harry a while to realise when he was being talked to and when
the man was speaking parts of his thoughts out loud. But it was the topic that made this
instance so special. Normally Fergus’ ramblings were about something bar-related or
grumblings about his latest hangover. This right there? This was a side to Fergus

Wanlockhead he had never encountered before. Harry couldn’t even hope to begin to
understand what his boss was going on about, already lost after he had mentioned lunar
celebrations – Lughnasadh, wasn’t it? The festival he had gone to, had met the twins and
danced…
Harry was off in his head, no longer trying to decipher Fergus’ mutterings. George and Fred
had made him feel special and so so safe that day – and any day they met after. Every touch,
innocent or daring, any look, any word spoken had left an invisible mark like tiny anchors
embedding in his heart. They had grown to be a bit of an army, he realised. Caught him up in
their ever-growing web of affection. Had it really only been a few weeks, not even a full
month?
“Yes,” the decisive tone of voice drew him back a while later, glancing up to meet his boss’
dark gaze, but finding him still staring at something Harry couldn’t quite grasp. “I don’t feel
comfortable waiting till Samhain, that ain’t healthy with so much residue muddying up your
core even if it’s just clinging to the outer edges right now.” He nodded to himself, still eying
something Harry supposed had to be magic-related. “You’re too young to chance it, wouldn’t
want to risk discordance, right?”
Here he finally met Harry’s gaze, clearly expecting him to agree, although“Um…”
Harry was confused. But then again, he had become rather accustomed to that state of being
by now.
