Chapter 20: Worrying
Chapter Notes

(or 'the info dump' as I like to think of it)

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Still 15th August 1996
The thin fabric slowly, reluctantly knitted itself back together under the guidance of George’s
wand. The seeming defiance made a confused smile tug at his lips, the begrudging way the
dress reacted to his magic reminding him of its owner. Must be the muggle fabric, he mused,
which, apart from being strangely soft, had a tendency to attract static. Something totally
unheard of for wizarding fashion, because well, static, magic, electricity. Still, they were
determined to repair the pretty dress and return it to…
Maybe-Possibly-Harry. It was all just too convenient to be a coincidence.
And wasn’t that a clusterfuck in and off itself. Harry Potter running around in dresses and
playing the biggest prank ever on the whole of the Wizarding World, even giving his friends
the run-around. They had spent so much time together, had been so close, and yet- Harry had
been drawing red herrings across their tracks for nearly a month and even though George
wondered whether he should feel betrayed by the whole farce… to him it just made too much
sense. After what happened at the Ministry, no, after what he and Fred knew happened to
Harry every summer, he couldn’t be mad about the deception. Harry deserved the freedom
only anonymity was able to grand him.
Was he sad that their friend didn’t trust them not to bust his secret? Yes.
Would George have been happy to be included in this mother of all pranks? Of course.
Was he slightly miffed in his prankster-pride to not have seen through the illusion? Hell yeah!
Did he want Harry away from those …relatives of his? Of fucking course.
Still, the way Harry chose to go about all this was interesting to say the least. Hiding in plain
sight was one thing, doing so in tantalizing dresses, alluring skirts and fabrics? Another
matter altogether. It was baiting the trouble. It was flirting with detection. Bold and dramatic
like a Gryffindor, yet so unexpectedly sneaky. No one would expect this.
The opposite of the spectrum.
Slytherin.

And they so wanted in on this scandalous deed.
Or was it more than that, more than a way to hide? After all, being who he was, not to
mention growing up with the people he had, couldn’t have left Harry with many avenues of
getting to know himself. What if the whole crossdressing was Harry finally expressing
himself in a way he never could before? Was there, just maybe, still a chance for George (and
his brother. Maybe. Perhaps. Nah.) ?
No, he reminded himself with a mental jerk, he shouldn’t make this kind of assumption. Just
because Harry happened to wear women’s clothing (disguise? Well, Harry was known for
being… creative in a pinch.) didn’t say anything about his sexual orientation. And, bloody
hell, just the thought of anything remotely relating to sex in connection with Harry had his
body reacting like he was 13 again. Or 12, he wasn’t quite sure when he had started lookingAnyway, he could not just assume. People had done that too often already where Harry was
concerned.
Fred, chin hooked on his twin’s shoulder, tucked close to the warmth of his neck as he
watched the progress on the dress, did nothing to abate George’s body’s wayward reaction.
He supposed it was simply an automatic response to another warm body when thinking about
Harry… Oh well. His twin and him, despite what some sources may say, had never gone
beyond mutual looking and cuddling. Maybe some light experimenting. They were siblings,
after all, no matter what magic made possible.
Like it is the case with most people growing up together in close quarters, there wasn’t any
attraction per se between the two. Physical anyway. Although, all these facts sure didn’t stop
them from using the rumours to their advantage when it came to thoroughly flustering
people. Still, when puberty started changing their bodies, it seemed only natural to compare
with the one they both were closest to. But that was just it. The two of them just were. Had
always been. That didn’t mean he could prevent his body reacting to stimuli – he was bloody
18, after all.
Far beyond looking went what they did with others, true, but never had they been in a
situation like the one with Harry. It had been their fifth year, Harry’s third, when they realised
it. And since then… tcha. It had not quite been a competition, because Harry was very much
13, very much the Boy-Who-Lived, and very straight. Only, apparently maybe not quite as
straight as they had thoughtNo, George grumped to himself, that was him being back again at assumptions based in
preconceived notions and prejudice.
George grimaced. The Wizarding World may not discriminate law-wise against the variety of
recognised sexualities and genders, but depending on the circles one frequented certain
prejudices did tend to make themselves known. Such as young women accompanying nonrelated males alone may be considered easy. Such as assuming that a male favouring female
clothing must be inclined towards their own gender. And that in a society that wore robes on
a daily, seriously…

“Brother mine, I feel you’re mixing ingredients up,” Fred muttered from his place face still
tucked against his brother’s skin. He was being especially clingy, but then he had spooked
Harry enough to chase him off after their promising morning.
George blinked, glanced at his twin, and mentally backtracked.
“True,” he finally admitted.
It was an easy trap to fall into, throwing gender and sexuality into the same cauldron. They
could belong, he supposed, but they did not necessarily have to. Yet another preconceived
notion he needed to get his head around. Sometimes growing up in a secluded place so
removed from both magical and muggle society, never truly being immersed in either’s
traditions, really sucked. He had never felt deprived of anything growing up, apart from their
parents’ attention that is, yet leaving the Burrow had been like being thrown into the Black
Lake on a hot summer’s day: Refreshing but shocking to the system.
There was just so much they hadn’t known before. So much tradition, culture they only truly
learned about earlier this very year. Sure, some things they knew, were peripherally aware of,
but most things, like the Olde Ways of connecting to one’s magic, were deemed ‘outdated’
and replaced by muggle ways.
Their parents didn’t completely abandon their magical roots, he could still recall visiting the
festivals as a small child, but they also never taught them. And Hogwarts under
Dumbledore’s rule was much the same, all geared towards integrating muggle beliefs and,
though not forbidding, no longer teaching wizarding heritage. Like the origins of the
festivals, like Lughnasadh and the upcoming Mabon, all of the cycle of life and death that
was so closely connected to Magic.
George, and Fred he supposed, understood why their parents did what they did, he realised
they firmly believed in making the Wizarding World as welcoming as possible for the
Muggleborns. Really, he understood. Why this had to mean they had to forsake their own
traditions, forget where their very culture originated…
He sighed as the deliberately obnoxious chime of a bell alerted them to new customers.
Weasley’s Wizard Wheezes demanded its owners’ attention once more.
The rainbow Pygmy Puff was sitting neatly snuggled into the lone pillow of the small bed up
in Harry’s room. Stroking the vibrant fur softly, Harry thought back on the conversation he
just had had with Fergus.
Apparently being hit with magic not aligned with or meant to work with your core left a
certain amount of residue, multiplying with each spell. In general, this residue would slowly
disperse with time and healing, but there was a limit. Although his boss hadn’t said as much,
Harry surmised that being hit with something ill-intentioned, or say any of the Unforgivables,
would be worse the longer the curse was kept going. Considering the amount of times Harry
had been in contact with dark magic over the years, it made him seriously question the
prowess of the adults in his life. Why had no one ever mentioned this to him? Madam

Pomfrey maybe? Dumbledore?? Or better yet, why had no one ever done anything about it?!
He was fricking known for surviving one of the aforementioned Unforgivables!
Well, Fergus was about to change that. But Fergus Wanlockhead was also a man living on
Knockturn Alley who Harry had only known for a handful of weeks. How sure could he
really be the intimidating bar owner was telling the truth?
Lunar celebrations?
Muddy cores and rituals to clear residue?
Sighing, Harry scooped his tiny furry friend up so he could let himself fall face-first into the
pillow. As much as he wanted to wallow in self-pity for a while, he knew he had to sort this
out. Analysing stuff, looking at things from a purely logical angle… wasn’t exactly his strong
side. It usually was Hermione who did these things, but- Hermione was not here, and ever
since escaping the Dursleys, Harry had this intense urge to become more independent. He
couldn’t rely on others, be it his friends, teachers, or bloody Dumbledore, forever. Look
where that would have ended this time! If what Fergus said was true, and Harry was very
inclined to believe him, then he would have been stuck on Privet Drive, locked up with only
the ridiculously slow owl post to contact anyone, and suffering from this dark magic residue
– without anyone being the wiser!
Although, truth be told, he had no idea what magical residue did to one’s core. Fergus had
mentioned ‘discordance’ like Harry should be aware of what that meant, but no matter how
much he wrecked his brain, he couldn’t for the life of him recall ever hearing about it. Which,
admittedly, didn’t have to mean much, and maybe he should just ask his boss about it, but…
Turning on his back, and wincing as the movement aggravated his bruise, Harry frowned at
the slightly stained ceiling. Fergus had been nothing but helpful ever since they met. He had
given Harry a safe place to stay, something to do, and even the means to buy the necessities
he had left behind with the Dursleys (and then some).
There was no reason Harry could see why the man would make up something so out there,
why he would devise such an elaborate plot. And for what? There was nothing to gain from
fooling Harry in this way – not even if he had somehow realised who he really was
sheltering. Sure, considering he was a bar owner on Knockturn, the man might have leanings
to the Dark (not that Harry had noticed anything that seemed harmful so far), and the ‘Dark’
was supposedly still very much after ‘Harry Potter’.
But no, that wasn’t quite true, was it?
Harry shared a commiserating look with the Pygmy Puff.
After spending so much time immersed in the magical district, seeing the Wizarding World
from the inside so to say… Harry had started to realise that there was more to magic than
Dark and Light. And really, what even meant ‘Light’ these days, if the very Ministry was
condemning good people like Remus, Faolán and his pack, not to mention the nice bookstore
owner Lycus Job, to existences at the very bottom of society – purely for a medical condition
they suffered from once a fucking month?

As the summer days went by, Light and Dark had started to feel like not much more than
political factions, or more like political parties that happened to be the most powerful around,
but weren’t truly representative of the actual population.
Then there were the books. Even with only having scratched the surface of all the knowledge
held in Mr. Job’s, no, Lycus’ bookstore, Harry couldn’t deny the realisation that his
understanding of magic was woefully incomplete. And something was telling him that this
wasn’t entirely down to his lack of study over the last five years. No, going by the very topics
he had encountered during his time at Bandy-Legs, during his talks with the people around…
it was obvious Hogwarts, prized school that it was, only taught a minimal part of the whole
magical spectrum.
In fact, if it wasn’t for this summer’s experiences, Harry would never even have heard about
the whole ‘grey magic’ of said spectrum. Hogwarts curriculum was all Light yay, Dark nay.
There was no in-between, you were either good or bad. But the books, and everyone he had
overheard talking this summer, strongly suggested there was more, that there was a part
residing between ‘Light’ and ‘Dark’. And this part, Grey magic, which seemed to be all about
intention, was simply non-existent in their school books.
Which, following his new understanding, made it frighteningly obvious that the school was
under leadership of a Light mage. A mage who just so happened to be the very leader of what
Harry had started terming the political Light. Light that maybe, just maybe, didn’t really have
to do much with what magic was all about.
The thing was, he had relied a lot on Hermione’s knowledge over his entire Hogwarts career
so far. He hadn’t really looked deeper than what was necessary, had gone with what his friend
pointed him to. Hermione’s knowledge, as impressive as it was, mostly stemmed from books
found in Hogwarts’ library or bought in Flourish and Blott’s. He doubted his friend had ever
ventured passed the set routes for students, and he was absolutely sure she had never
ventured passed Diagon Alley. She was, for all intents and purposes, a Light witch. And that
wasn’t her fault, but Harry couldn’t help but feel different the longer he thought about the
topic. It just seemed to make no sense to categorize magic only in ‘good’ and ‘bad’, ‘Light’
and ‘Dark’. Or categorize at all beyond what the political factions said.
You could kill with a simple cutting charm commonly used in Herbology class if you knew
where to aim, and he had a feeling a lot of what was understood and condemned as dark
might actually have rather harmless origins. Wasn’t Cruciatus basically overstimulating
nerves? What if one were to only cast a really mild version of it, would it feel like the tickling
charm? Maybe help with blood circulation or testing for nerve damage?
Rubbing at the space between his neatly plucked brows, Harry wasn’t sure what to think
about his own theories. All this sounded really out there, he was probably getting way ahead
of himself.
But the one thought prevailed: What if, just what if, magic couldn’t be categorised in ‘good’
and ‘bad’? What if it truly was all about the intention behind the spell, the intention of the
caster? What if ‘Grey’ really was simply a synonym for Magic itself, magic as it had always
been, devoid of human morality clauses?

17th August, late evening
“So what?” Fred asked, sounding dubious from where he was hefting crates from the small
storage room up in their apartment down the spiral staircase into the shop.
“What do you mean, so what? If it’s him, he’s bound to be on edge now,” George called back
to him, his brother focused on counting today’s checkout. The shop was doing well, but the
work was starting to wear on them.
“Huh?” Fred wasn’t really listening, the heavy crates full of their latest products demanding
his attention. Most couldn’t be levitated; the magic of the spell would be interfering with the
product’s properties.
“Forge, brother, we can’t just up and tell him,” George sighed, looking up from the cash
register. “You asking him the way you did, already made him run once.”
“Well, we gotta ask somehow,” Fred frowned, setting the crate down with a huff and
stretching his back.
“No,” George rebuked, frowning at his twin. “No, think about it. If Harry really goes to these
kinds of lengths, us coming at him with the truth is just going to scare him off. Again.”
There was no missing the slight accusation in his tone.
“Gred, don’t you think you’re overthinking this?” Fred frowned right back, fed up with his
brother’s worrying. He had been at it for two days now and all Fred wanted to do was seek
out Harry and clear the air. Yet they were still stuck at the shop, work swamping them. “And
even so, where’s he gonna go? We know where he stays now.”
George put his quill down with a bit more force than necessary, leaning over the counter to
pin his twin in place with a Look.
“He’s hiding,” he enunciated every word carefully, like Fred was being especially slow. “Not
just from the Order, but from everyone. Right in front of their noses!”
Well, true. And wasn’t that some awe-inspiring shit.
“It’s quite the prank, it is,” he agreed a little dreamily.
“Yeah…,” George clearly approved wholeheartedly, eyes going vacant for a moment thinking
about the huge scope of Harry’s prank. “But! That’s exactly why we can’t just walk up to him
and bust his disguise. I think, we need to let him come around on his own,” he implored of
his twin.
“Oh come on!” Fred complained. He had never been a fan of the waiting game, no matter
how convenient it could be in a prank. Couldn’t they do something already? “I want in on
this! If it’s him, he might freak at first, but we just tell him how it is. It’s not like we would
rat him out.”

He was sure of this too. But it was the Gryffindor in him talking, and deep down he knew this
well. His brother George was much better at thinking long term, but really, this was Harry. He
knew he could trust them, right?
“But you don’t know that!”
He didn’t really have a book on ritual magic. In fact, Harry didn’t really have books on
specific topics in general, apart from his school books that is, as he had favoured buying
NEWTs material and summary-like tomes at Lycus’. To put it simply: Harry didn’t know
enough about magic to select books on specific topics yet, so he had gone for overviews,
mainly history because it’s what had sparked his interest that day.
So, he was stuck with either school books or history overviews. Magical history overviews,
because even in shops like that of Lycus Job one didn’t find muggle topics (Unless one
wanted to count the ridiculously outdated and heavily prejudiced stuff they all sold on
Muggle Studies). Don’t get him wrong, after receiving his OWL results and realising he was
eligible to re-sit his history exam, magical history was exactly what he wanted. At least after
he realised it actually wasn’t all goblin wars, but could very well focus on all the fascinating
traditions and their origins he had observed this summer. Still, even these out-of-the-way
books (Harry just knew he would never have found them in Diagon’s main book shop) didn’t
really help in his current quest and had the habit of side-tracking him with random yet
riveting information. Like now.
Up until this summer he had never even heard the term ‘magi’. Yet, it was apparently the goto-term when talking about wizarding folk in general. Going by A Mostly Neutral Point of
View, a book he was fairly sure he would never have found in Flourish & Blotts, ‘magi’
described ‘all genders of humanoid magic-wielding folk’. That sentence alone sparked many
more questions, like what did they mean all genders? Why did it sound like there were more
than two in the magical world? (*) What about people like his friend Treasach the donas? He
was humanoid in stature, but not all features. Was he magi, or would he be denoted by his
species?
Harry thudded his head down on the heavy parchment pages with a groan. He could see
many trips down Oblique Lane in his future. Hopefully Lycus would be willing to help him,
because he was pretty certain he would never get the hang of the logic behind wizarding book
titles. All of this, magi, rituals, the possibly-senseless-magical spectrum, were only more
points on his ever-growing list of this summer’s revelations. And he needed a break.
“Right,” he huffed, tired but determined to set his own hang-ups aside for some time, “Let’s
get ready.”
The declaration earned him a short meep from his furry companion. Harry fancied it sounded
like a ‘Yessir!’ with the way the Pygmy Puff was sitting at attention.

Before changing into something more fitting for work, Harry made use of the small charms
booklet Lycus Job had gifted him with. Had that only been a few days ago?

Sitting down on the rim of the shower bath, Harry carefully flipped through the worn pages,
fingertips following the handwritten lines a little reverently. The charms were, as Lycus had
said, those his mother had used in her time. But it was clear by the differing scripts and
fading ink that the booklet had been handed down in the family for generations.
Looking through it now, Harry concluded that each person owning it had added their own
spells or made additions to the already existing ones. There was a teeth-bleaching spell
written in already yellowing ink on the early pages that had a warning added in fresher blue
about overdoing the bleaching and ultimately making the teeth as easily breakable as glass!
Harry shuddered. It was fascinating and a bit scary what people had done (and were still
doing) in the name of beauty. He knew there were tons of books like this in the stores,
Lockhart the vain git coming to mind as well. But it made him wonder how common it was
to hand down books through generations, maybe on different topics and not just cosmetic
charms. Maybe each family had a certain trade or something…
Glancing up into the, for once silent, mirror, Harry briefly entertained the thought what his
parents might have taught him had they lived. Had the Potters, Purebloods that they were, a
trade to their name? Something handed down through the generations, father to son and so
on? Would his Dad have given him something similar to Lycus’ mother’s booklet? He
chuckled quietly imagining James Potter showing a tiny Harry actual pottery. It would have
been a mess, he thought.
As it did most times he paid his reflection any attention, which admittedly had become a lot
more frequent recently, his gaze was inevitably dragged to his lightning bolt. This scar, even
faded as it was now, had determined so much of his life. Briefly Harry reflected upon his
childhood and how he actually used to like the ‘cool scar’ that made him ‘special’. Back
when magic was just stories and a way to raise his uncle’s dangerous temper. Back when he
was more often ‘Boy’ than ‘Harry’. Back before everything, really.
As he had learned later this scar also marked the day he lost everything while the Wizarding
World gained a respite from all the terror that had ruled it for years. It would turn out to be
only a hiatus, but most wizards, or should he say magi, would probably never know that.
Sure, they hailed him as their Saviour now, but they only really saw the end result of what
happened at the Ministry. They didn’t actually have to suffer another war and yes, he was so
very grateful for it, but all of it made a simple life for him, as Harry Potter, very much
impossible.
And it wasn’t just about not being able to walk down a street without being mobbed. It wasn’t
even about being unable to uphold a simple job because people would never look upon him
with the same expectations they would any other young man. They would always assume,
always judge differently, always make it impossible to measure up to the bar they expected
him to uphold.
Harry had more than the one scar, he knew. He had accumulated quite a few during his
Hogwarts years and he had only just passed OWL-level. Not all of them were visible and no
other magi had ever truly cared about them. He had saved these people, that was all that
mattered. No one cared about the How. No one bothered to ask how he dealt with everything
from that day long in the past to today.

He wondered how different everything, he himself, the world he now lived in, would have
turned out if Voldemort had never gone after the Potter family. Well, he certainly wouldn’t
have ended up hiding himself behind ruffles and skirts. …or would he? And was he truly still
hiding? Yet again his thoughts drifted to what the book had said: all genders of humanoid
magic-wielding folk. All genders…
A questioning chirrup from his rainbow-coloured companion brought Harry back into the
tiny, not quite grimy, bathroom on the upper floor of Bandy-Legs. A bar in Knockturn Alley.
A bar he now worked at as a waitress… and there his thoughts went again. Coasting from the
unlikely present over random memories, getting stuck briefly on thoughts of red hair and
freckles… he needed to talk to the twins.
And flames, anger given physical form through his magic caught his reminiscing attention.
That thief Mundungus Fletcher, selling away Sirius’ things without a care! It made Harry’s
skin prickle with surging magic. He would need to do something about that because Sirius
couldn’t right now and Remus didn’t have the authority. What was that man thinking? How
could he ever believe to get away with selling off stuff that so obviously belonged to an
Ancient and Noble House considering most of it bore the family crest?
He groaned again, running his hands through his long tresses agitatedly. He had to focus, he
couldn’t keep drifting off in his own mind.
However he decided to go about this whole drama, the twins, his persona as Harley,
Hogwarts, all the revelations of gender and sexuality, and whatever else he was going to
stumble upon during the next weeks - one thing he already knew for certain: He was going to
write to Remus about Fletcher. Just the thought of the thief had Harry’s magic roiling again,
but thankfully nothing caught fire this time.

Chapter End Notes

(*) about the genders: This story is set in 1996. Gender in general, especially in
western society, was still very much limited to being either male or female
Sooo… rainbow Pygmy Puff. Whatever shall we name them?

Chapter 21: Creeping
Chapter Notes

A little wink to TheAnonymousAuthorFromEarth at the end <3
(and more info dumping... somehow)

See the end of the chapter for more notes

22nd August (Thursday)
If Harry had thought he would just need to scrape up some Gryffindor courage and go talk to
the twins… well, let’s say it wasn’t all that easy. The reality of having a full-time job came
crashing down on him soundly, work taking up his time and before he really noticed a week
had gone by without him managing to visit the twins. But also without the twins (or anyone
else for that matter) contacting him. He was even still waiting on Remus’ letter.
Anxiety was once again starting to be his constant companion - followed and soothed
somewhat by a certain (and by now infamous) Pygmy Puff. The cleansing ritual was set for
the next full moon and Harry still had no true idea what to expect of that. Not to mention
school was set to start in just over one week, which was just three days after the ominous
ritual, and he also still hadn’t decided whether or not he would be going back. And don’t
even get him started on his summer homework.
Speaking of school, his letter was late this year, and as Harry cleaned up the pub in the small
hours of 22nd August, he wondered whether that had anything to do with him supposedly
missing.
It couldn’t be though, right? They couldn’t really justify delaying sending out letters because
one student happened to have gone awol. Then again, maybe it was as simple as his letter not
reaching him, or facing some trouble in trying to reach him, like his OWL results had done.
Well, the woes of assuming another identity. For a moment he thought of those hundreds of
acceptance letters he had received all over the country before his first year. He thought of that
wide-eyed little boy on his first train ride to Hogwarts, full of wonder for the magical world.
It was still there, the wonder, but it had been dulled by growing understanding. Still, what
wouldn’t he give for some good old Bertie Bott’s now, and Ron to share them with…
As Harry put away the self-cleaning rag and looked around the parlour one last time, another
thought niggled in the back of his mind. What if they, Dumbledore, the Order, what-haveyou, were deliberately delaying only his letter to make intercepting him on his way to the
shops easier for them? After all, there wasn’t much time left to do his shopping if he really
intended to go back to Hogwarts this year. It would make sense for them to make the time
window as short as possible… or would it really? The summer holidays were nearly over,

what use truly was there to find him now unless they thought he had been kidnapped. And in
that case it wasn’t likely for him to go shopping for his school stuff in the first place.
Everyone that even remotely knew him (which were, admittedly, not many), knew Harry
Potter considered Hogwarts his home. They had no way of knowing that he was even toying
with the idea of not returning. There was no reason for them to still be looking for him, right?
So, was this simply a case of him being obnoxious, of him being the self-centred dunderhead
a certain potions master liked to label him as? Because did it really have to be about him? It
wasn’t like they, Dumbledore, the Order, the Wizarding World as a whole, had anymore use
for him. Or rather Harry Potter. The threat of Voldemort was gone, truly this time, and he
hadn’t heard any whispers of people believing differently. No, Harry Potter had fulfilled his
‘duty’, he thought bitterly. There really was no reason for them, whoever ‘they’ turned out to
be, to be looking for him all that closely. Which, he had to admit, was probably the actual
reason why he was still enjoying an unchecked summer.
But no. No, he couldn’t risk it. If his life had taught him anything so far, it was paranoia. As
he had heard Fred say the other day, it wasn’t paranoia if they were really out to get you. And
he just couldn’t chance it, not even for one day! He hadn’t decided yet, if he even wanted to
go back at all and he just knew people would expect him to and- …Fred. George. They knew,
or at the very least, they strongly suspected who ‘Harley’ truly was. Had they gone and ratted
him out to the Order?
Just the thought had Harry’s chest tighten as he watched Fergus lock the door and all
windows as he did every night. Unconsciously he raised his hand to rub at an especially
painful spot right above his heart, staring aimlessly while his boss went about enabling the
night wards. The flickering magic of his surroundings turned solid, like fog solidifying,
creating a magical dome over the outer walls of the pub.
But something wasn’t right.
Harry blinked back into focus as Fergus mumbled a distracted goodnight, snatching a
nightcap from behind the bar, before he thudded up the stairs. The fact that his steps could be
heard was strange in itself, but Harry was more focused on the sudden weird feeling he got
from the dimmed parlour. Not trusting his sad eyesight, he concentrated on spreading his
magic more consciously than he normally would inside Bandy-Legs, making sure to cover all
corners, searching for… something.
He heard the old wood creaking, felt the magical echo of the wards, yet nothing stood out.
There weren’t even any critters here like he was sure infested parts of the Leaky Cauldron.
Still, he couldn’t shake the sensation, like a sixth sense telling him something was creeping
about.
He went and checked the door again, like he hadn’t just watched his boss lock it, but
everything appeared to be in order. Silent too. Only the wind could be heard whispering
around the building’s corners. Glancing around one last time, Harry shrugged and turned to
head upstairs.

The hairs on the back of his neck suddenly standing on end had him abruptly whirling back
around, skirt flying in a graceful arc with the motion, and wand at the readyBut there was nothing.
Noxing the lights, shivering as the darkness plunged down around him, Harry went to his
room. And if he once more raised the basic alert wards on his door that he had long since
stopped employing during his stay here… well. If there was nothing there to trigger them, no
one would ever find out.

Later that night a dishevelled Harry woke with a start, long hair falling in his face as he sat up
from his slumped over position on his bed. He was surrounded by books and his cheek still
seemed to be stuck to one of the parchment pages… or maybe it was the other way around.
He winced a little as the page ripped itself out of the book with his motion, but his gaze was
drawn to the window. What had woken him? He didn’t remember falling asleep over his
reading… although it certainly was a likely scenario considering his new habits.
Glancing out the window again, Harry frowned. He could have sworn something had just
moved out there in the dark… Getting to his feet, his wand already in hand (had he ever put it
down?), he took the two steps over and scanned the area beyond the window panes. Well, as
much as his eyes allowed him to anyway. There was a weird fog obscuring his vision, he
realised a few seconds later. Now, fog in London wasn’t all that surprising, but he couldn’t
remember ever seeing it getting into the magical district. Somehow he had just assumed the
weather regulation wards kept stuff like that out, but what did he know, really.
He blinked. Was fog supposed to move like this?
Squinting in frustration, Harry sent his magic out carefully, tasting along the wards he felt
covering his window. They seemed fine. Still, his instincts were restless, like they were trying
to tell him something sinister was afoot. Abruptly pulling the drapes closed, he moved over
to the door to check the rest of the house. Only, he never made it that far.
“You should have known better than to follow a vampire, doll,” a velvety voice purred from
right behind him, sending his pulse racing, his heart jumping and his wand sparking as he
tried to turn again.
“Ah, don’t you bother”, the voice continued, niggling at a memory he couldn’t quite recall.
And really, right now Harry was more concerned with the fact that he couldn’t move.
He couldn’t move.
“Mhm… all this excitement sure sends the blood pumping…,” they whispered behind him
and Harry had the distinct feeling he was being.. sniffed at.
“Who-”

“Pardon me, doll, I thought you knew,” the voice continued, an affected exasperation tinting
it, “After all,” they said, “You did follow me, Harry, did you not?”
And Harry could practically feel the feral smirk on what he now recalled where quite nicely
shaped lips in an aristocratic, yet gaunt, face.
“Sanguini.”
“There you go,” the now familiar voice daunted and Harry would have flinched at the barelythere touch of lips against his neck – if only he could.
The memory of meeting the vampire in Dumbledore’s office flooded his mind then. That cold
December night of last year, back when Umbridge had ruled Hogwarts, back when Harry had
been plagued by repeating dreams of the Department of Mysteries. Thinking on it now, it’s
hard to believe he lived months and months like that, dream-visions of running through
corridors haunting him. He sometimes still wondered if he shouldn’t have known better, if he
shouldn’t have realised they were more than dreams.
But that night, 18th of December 1995, Harry’s dream-visions had changed. Oh he had still
been there down in the bowels of the Ministry, in the damp corridors of the department which
would see the end of Voldemort just half a year later. But in that vision he had not been
running, no, he had been slithering. He had seen the world through the eyes of a serpent, yet
not an innocent animal. Nagini had long been corrupted by the soul piece of her master
inhabiting her form. She had been capable of human emotions, of hate, of loathing. She had
wanted to rip and tear and killA haze-like quality was still hanging over the events that led to Harry, sweat drenched and
panicky, up in Dumbledore’s office telling, warning, begging the headmaster, someone, to go
and help Mr. Weasley. To safe him from what Harry… no, Nagini… had done. As a member
of the Order of the Phoenix, Mr. Weasley had taken a turn standing guard at the Department
of Mysteries, watching that dreaded, useless prophecy. And they, Harry, Nagini, had attacked
him. Ripped into him, torn and bloody and- And up there in Dumbledore’s tower, in the
middle of the night, had been the vampire. Sanguini had been pacing in front of the
headmaster’s desk when Harry and his entourage of friends and teachers arrived. Restless
and… angry, yes, now that he deliberately tried to remember that hazy moment, Harry
thought the vampire had been angry, maybe even desperate somehow.
In the rush of what happened after he had brought his vision to the headmaster’s (and
apparently the vampire’s) attention, Harry had forgotten to ever question what Sanguini had
been doing there. Why had he been at Hogwarts? Was he a friend of Dumbledore’s and if so,
did him finding and recognizing Harry mean…
“Yes and no, lovely, yes and no.”
Blinking back into the present, Harry found himself sat in someone’s lap. They had long,
trouser-clad legs and he could feel the fine material rub against his bare thighs. His skirt, and
why was he wearing that to bed anyway, had ridden up his legs sitting down, innocently
enough, yet Harry jumped into standing before he even fully comprehended the situation.
He’s not meant to sit in this person’s lap. They, Sanguini, chuckled at his reaction, but there’s

no attempt to keep him in place. The eclipse-like gaze that followed him held no malice, no
leer. Just curiosity and maybe a spark of amusement. A strange smell, not quite cigarettes,
was hanging faintly in the air.
The stare-off that followed should have been intimidating, daunting even considering they
were still in Harry’s, or should he say Harley’s, tiny room up in Fergus Wanlockhead’s flat.
It’s not, though, not really. Instead, there’s a sense of awkwardness… and a vampire who had
made himself comfortable in the lonely chair and was just about to put his feet up – on
Harry’s bed.
“Shoes off,” he found himself instructing brusquely. And woah that was not what he had
planned to say, but then he’s a Gryffindor and did he really have a plan in the first place?
There’s a vampire in his room, but Harry’s not about to let him ruin his bedsheets. Said
vampire blinked at him for a moment, narrowing his eyes, and some part of Harry’s mind
frantically wondered what by Merlin’s sock draw he thought he was doing ordering around a
dangerous creature like that. Then Sanguini gracefully slipped out of his shoes and promptly
lifted his feet up onto Harry’s bed. Well. He could live with that – granted he’s going to live
through this encounter.
“Why are you here… sir?” Crap, that kinda took the threat out of his tone. Sanguini wasn’t
one of his professors, nor was he one of the shopkeepers Harry always took care to address
with respect, yet the ‘sir’ had still slipped out.
His question was met with a terrifying smile. Too many teeth, too sharp incisors. But the
damned amused twinkle in dark eyes was still there.
“Sanguini,” he was corrected in a languid way, said vampire stretching lazily across the
whole width of Harry’s room in his slouch. “It’s what you wizards know me as at this point in
time and I quite enjoy the lacklustre irony of this denomination.”
Harry wasn’t sure he caught all that, but he did get it was okay to address the man by his
chosen name. Really now, technically Harry should insist on being called ‘Harley’ just out of
principle. Carefully eying Sanguini, knowing full well his wand was no defence at this short
a distance should the vampire decide to attack, Harry finally settled down again. Sitting on
the edge of his bed, he repeated his question, trying to ignore the way too dark eyes followed
his every move.
“It has been two months, yes?” The way-too-comfy-looking vampire questioned in some sort
of non sequitur, but Harry knew what he was talking about.
Even here, surrounded by all that made up his secret identity, dressed in silk and hair a just as
silky long mess, the memory was never far away. It lurked in the recesses of his mind, the
hollow left behind by the horcrux (secret, dirty, tainted), the rapidly filling void (dreams of
another kind, secret and forbidden, or were they?) calling to him every time he didn’t keep
himself busy. The fight, the end, the beginning of all that he was now… Harry wasn’t sure
what to call that day in the Department of Mysteries when Voldemort was finally defeated
and erased from this world. He gave a short nod to Sanguini’s likely rhetorical question. Not
even two full months ago and yet it felt like a lifetime away.

Reaching for one of the discarded books littering Harry’s small space, Sanguini glanced at
the title before his eyes flitted across the room, taking in the various topics or lack thereof
depicted on the moth-bitten covers and in colourfully marked paragraphs.
“You’re looking into rituals, doll?” He questioned, eyebrows raised in exaggerated
astonishment. “My, my, who would have thought.”
“Cleansing rituals,” Harry stressed, feeling his cheeks heat as if he had been caught doing
something forbidden. And if it wasn’t for this summer’s experiences, that was exactly what
he would be thinking had happened. “I know better than to just assume,” he felt the need to
add. The ‘now’ was left unsaid but heard either way.
“So you do,” Sanguini mused, flipping through the book in his hands. “Yet you’re clearly
lacking sources.” A long, deathly pale finger drifted along a passage in Harry’s edition of
Fantastic Beasts. “Your next best option would be Mabon,” he commented, eyes raising to fix
Harry in place, “Unless, of course, the matter is rather pressing.”
It was not a question, not quite, but the lilt in Sanguini’s voice said it all.
“I see,” the vampire continued as if Harry hadn’t just mutely stared him down. “Well then,
the moon chart seems a good choice,” he nodded at the chart Harry had used as bookmark in
the very book he was holding, a chart he had acquired in his third year when he first learned
about Remus and kept ever since. “It will be the next full moon, yes?”
Harry sighed. Vampires, apparently, could be worse than a certain headmaster when it came
to knowing things they shouldn’t even be aware of, or talking in riddles, or generally doing
the whole seemingly omniscient monologuing.
“Right.”
He was gifted a smile in answer, too bright like everything about the vampire was just that
tiny bit too much. Too deep eyes, too sharp teeth, too graceful movements, too sensual voice.
Not by a lot, no. If Harry hadn’t met the vampires that frequented Bandy-Legs, a group this
very vampire seemed to sometimes belong to, he likely wouldn’t have noticed. But he had
met them, had observed them interact with wizards, werewolves, hags and all the other nonhuman beings frequenting the pub.
“Oh come on, doll,” Sanguini finally groused, actually pouting at the young magi on the bed.
“I’m not going to run to that mighty headmaster of yours. That man has avoided helping us
one time too often, I certainly do not feel obliged to assist him in finding his golden boy.” He
gave Harry a once-over, eyes strangely soft for a moment. “But here I am,” he smirked then,
arms spreading out (and hitting the wall on his right). “A veritable source of information, all
the information a tiny hidden hero could want,” he added with the kind of eyebrow wiggle
Harry had only ever seen on Sirius. It was not a reassuring look…
Harry’s eyes went wide in realisation.

Hours later, first rays of sun starting to creep in through the not quite drawn curtains,
Sanguini was still there in Fergus Wanlockhead’s flat, hands busily gesticulating as he talked
unbeknownst to the actual owner of the place. Harry for his part was rapidly writing in one of
his many notebooks, trying to keep up with the positive deluge of information being dumped
on him.
“…magic is magic, but calling something with bad intent ‘dark’ makes differentiating things
easier, you see? It is a misconception of present times. But, and that is important, there is
black magic, love,” he was just adding, glancing over at his attentive audience to make sure
Harry was truly taking his words in.
Black magic, huh? Was this why the Blacks were called the way they were, Harry mused. He
wouldn’t be surprised if that were the case.
“I only read about this in a footnote,” Harry said, cocking his head thoughtfully. “None of the
books explained what made black magic black, what exactly made it so terrible. There were
just some vague mentions of rituals…,” he trailed off.
Which in turn had Harry wondering about his scar… Horcruxes were some form of ritual,
weren’t they? Not that he knew shit about the disgusting soul anchors. Yes, he technically
knew what they were, what they were meant to do. He even understood they needed human
sacrifice- oh. Well. This might just be it, what made horcruxes black magic. But he couldn’t
ask. No one was to know.
“Ah yes,” Sanguini nodded thoughtfully, “I should add that just like people decided to
categorize between dark and light, they did once upon a time single out the branch of
sacrificial magic and called it black. You did not think those cutesy sacrifices of grain ear
dolls during festivals came out of nothing, did you now?”
Considering Harry’s thoughts had just been lingering on horcruxes and what Voldemort had
done to achieve them… no, actual human sacrifice did not come as much of a surprise. He
wished it had, though.
“But why black?”
“Black as the night, the time humans naturally fear, the time they hide and sleep through if
they can… or used to, anyway.” There was a somewhat wistful look to the vampire as he
stared at the slowly growing sunlight insistently and inexorably making its way into the
room.
And Harry wasn’t going to think on that any further.
“What about the Unforgivables then?” He questioned instead, pausing in his rapid writing
and squinting over at his uninvited guest. “I was thinking… you know, the Cruciatus… it’s
just.. isn’t it technically just stimulating nerve endings?”
Harry would have never dared to voice this kind of theory before this summer, or even in
front of anyone that knew who he really was. Yet here was Sanguini, not only knowing

Harry’s identity, but a big secret, Harley, too and Harry really couldn’t explain why he felt
safe blurting out something like this.
“Oh you’re not all that far off, my darling little magi-child,” Sanguini agreed from where he
was elegantly slouched in Harry’s lonely chair, socked feet propped up on the bed. Harry still
could barely believe he made the vampire take off his shoes through a stare-down. It seemed
surreal to think scrawny Harry, even in nice dresses, was capable of such a thing. “Not far
off, no, but not quite right either,” Sanguini drawled and met his host’s eyes with a lazy
smirk.
Something in that gaze made Harry blush.
The vampire’s smirk grew even wider.
Harry blinked, feeling the heat rise to his ears.
“Now, yes,” Sanguini continued in a too seductive purr, “Stimulating the… nerve endings is
indeed part of this carnal form of magic, doll,” he finally relented, pronunciation of certain
words just this side of suggestive that Harry really didn’t need his last words to cotton on:
“But yes, what is commonly called the Cruciatus Curse these days holds its origins in simple,
yet so wonderfully useful, sex magic.”
He woke with a start. Again. Again? Glancing around his tiny room, Harry blinked in
confusion. Everything was as it should be: books and notes strewn around in strategical heaps
that allowed him a way to the door that led by his new trunk filled with absolutely carefully
folded clothes. The curtains were drawn and he noticed new, more, ink smudges on his
hands… Had he fallen asleep taking notes? It wouldn’t be the first time. Yes, everything
looked as it should be – yet not. Something… there was a smell..
Sanguini.
Throwing the too hot blanket off of himself, Harry chanced a glance at his legs… no skirt. In
fact, he was wearing his sleep clothes and his hair was loosely braided like he had started
doing ever since he learned to make a decent braid. Did that mean Sanguini’s visit had been
nothing but a dream? Memories of the touchy-feely yet so very helpful vampire rose up from
his sleep-addled mind. No, he could still recall the man sitting in his creaky chair, feet
propped up and talking animatedly. Dark magic, light magic, intent, rituals. Purification and
human sacrifice. It was a lot, it was confusing, he really wanted Hermione here, butA ray of the already warm morning sun highlighted a new book sitting on the cushion next to
him. There was no title on the cover, but somehow Harry just knew he would find
information on cleansing rituals and maybe something more, forgotten about information,
inside. And on top of the age-worn book sat an open package of Bertie Bott’s Every Flavour
Beans… including a colourful Pygmy Puff already munching on the questionable
confections.
“I’m so going to call you Botts now, or Bean or something,” Harry muttered, eying the
cheeky thing currently taking a big, for a Pygmy Puff, chomp out of a vanilla-coloured bean.

“And I hope that one is snot-flavour,” he added vindictively as he reached out to save the rest
of his present.

Chapter End Notes

One last chapter in 2020.
I'm not going to try and say some empty words about new beginnings, because right
now they would just feel wrong.
But whatever you endured this year, the fact you're reading this means: You survived.
The sun still rises every morning and so do we. Stay safe.

Chapter 22: Counting
Chapter Notes

Well hello. Fancy seeing you here! It has been… 15 months?
Also, I have no idea what I'm doing here. :D

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Three days later, on a lazy Sunday 25th, Harry finally received an impatiently awaited
response. He had spread out his notes and a certain tome near the back of the empty parlour
for once, a spot of sunlight from one of the skylights warming his feet, a steaming cup of the
tea Fergus reserved for only the two of them sitting at his elbow.
It was with a whisper of wings in the otherwise still air that an owl swooped in, making
Harry look up from an especially engrossing passage about the deeper meaning and
usefulness of numbers used in rituals. (It went a bit over his head, maybe he should go for a
version of ‘Arithmancy for Dummies’ first.) The owl was… different, he supposed described
the obvious unease of the bird. And nervous, or maybe just unsure what to do? They fluttered
about the parlour uncertainly, aiming this way and that, before coming to sit high up on one
of the massive beams beneath the roof. There they kept hopping back and forth, ruffling their
feathers, and generally appearing uncomfortable. Cocking his head, Harry slowly got up,
careful to keep his movement unthreatening.
Up close, or as close as Harry could get, the owl appeared unusually unkempt for a wizarding
post carrier. Not dirty, but somehow missing the carefully groomed appearance he was used
to. The behaviour too was an obvious give-away, but the thought was still unsettling in more
than one way: Was this a wild owl? Who would be using an untrained wild owl to carry
personal mail? And why?
The only reason Harry could think of in that moment was subterfuge. Mail send with official
carriers like those in the owl post office was traceable. Getting a wild bird to deliver
something for you, though, was not only untraceable (unless caught in a spell somehow), but
also meant someone capable of convincing an untamed creature to do their bidding. Or
maybe it was a favour of some sort? To this day he wasn’t sure how Sirius managed to
convince those flashy tropical birds to carry his first letters back after third year all across the
ocean. But what bird would strike a deal with a.. human…
As the bird was bobbing their head agitatedly at the wizard’s approach, Harry felt the literal
Lumos going off in his mind. Of course. There really was only one person right now that
would go the lengths of hunting down a wild owl and somehow managing to convince them
to carry their mail. Remus had all the reasons and possible skills to be that person. Staying at
Order headquarters, keeping an eye on the proceedings of the search for Harry. He could just

imagine how hard it was to have any privacy with people like Mad Eye Moody around, the
memorable holidays he spent at the dilapidated Black estate still fresh in his mind.
Irritable barking brought Harry abruptly back from memory lane.
“You know,” he addressed the bird, “If you want me to hurry up, might come down and let
me take that letter?”
Oh that was the wrong thing to say, apparently. Really, he lived with Hedwig, he should
know better. He got a hiss in warning, a sound he was not completely unused to, but still
unsettling. It just wasn’t a sound one expected a bird to make.
“Yes, I know, that was dumb,” Harry agreed, wincing at the baleful glare sent his way. “Let’s
try again, okay?”
Gentling his voice, but more importantly his magic, he reached out with both. Thank you,
Treasach, for teaching him.
“Hey there, buddy,” he crooned, slowly reaching his arm up to provide a possible landing
space and letting his magic twine around the words. “What you got there, mh?”
Suspicious yellow eyes pinned him in place, eyebrow-like plumage markings drawing down
over the luminous gaze.
“You’re a judgy one, hey?” Harry couldn’t help be amused. That gaze was like a blend
between McGonagall’s unimpressed I-know-you-are-up-to-no-good and Snape’s You-are-fullof-shit-and-you-know-it glare. “I’m Ha- …Harley, by the way. Could I interest you in some
bacon?”
Silently accio-ing said leftovers - the yellow gaze never leaving him - Harry held his offering
aloft with his other hand. That was clearly the right call, as a second later he had a scruffy
bird perched on his arm, claws digging in just a bit too strongly, as the owl gluttoned
themselves on bacon. Harry wisely kept his hands to himself for now, not wanting to spook
his newest acquaintance by trying to touch them.
Post owls had an inherent magic, he had read in one of his first-year books recently, that
would lead them to the correct person even though they quite certainly could not read the
address on their burden. Was that how this one had found him considering the whole HarleyHarry business when not even the headmaster and all his considerable magic had managed to
track him down yet?
Then again, that ministry bird bringing his OWL results had managed it as well, even though
some sort of anonymization had happened on the name… yet no owl bearing Dumbledore’s
well-meaning disappointment had managed to reach him. Just another thing he really should
be looking into. Before, Hedwig had taken care of his personal correspondence, and he had
wanted to get his OWL results, so maybe it was intent? And yes, he had wanted to get a
response from Remus, but wasn’t that a bit too easy? Wouldn’t that mean his own magic lead
the post carriers and decided which one could find him? Also, was there such a thing as
magical owl breeds or could just any owl do it? What was the difference? He couldn’t recall

ever having so many questions churning around his head that weren’t in some form related to
a certain very-much-dead dark lord.
It took a while (and all of the bacon) before the wild owl let Harry take the letter. His fingers
still got nipped rather harshly and the bird was gone in a skittish flutter a second later. The
letter turned out to really be from Remus, bringing back the memory of an angrily scribbled
note and fire…
Cub,
First off: excuse the owl, but needs must. I found him in the park near Padfoot’s place
and thought he was a charming fellow. Only bit me thrice.
Huffing in amused exasperation, Harry plopped himself back down by his tea and notes.
Only thrice, huh?
A word of advice from an old hand in the game: The best way to avoid detection by
wizards is always going the muggle route. I am sure you noticed by now how admirably
ignorant most of us are of the world outside our little niche. But if only magical means
will get the deed done, always use an unregistered carrier. And yes, Harry, Hedwig is
registered. Although she should not be registered as specifically your owl, she would
still be in the system as an official post owl sold to a private buyer.
Well, that was certainly interesting. Harry briefly wondered if the fact Hagrid had gotten him
Hedwig would change anything, and if so, what kind of data really was registered when
selling post owls. Didn’t these kinds of things fall under the secrecy of correspondence? Did
magi have a fundamental right to secrecy of correspondence? Wait. Did the Wizarding World
even have fundamental rights?
Rubbing his forehead in aggravation at his own lack of knowledge, Harry focused back on
Remus’ letter.
Speaking of mischievous owls, I have to say, all this sneaking around and giving the
Order the run-around brings back memories of the Marauders. Your father would have
loved all this. Well, at least the part where his son is leading around a good dozen of
fully grown wizards by their robe strings. You still have them completely blindsided, cub.
They were so sure you would turn up in Diagon Alley at some point. Just to be safe,
though: Keep avoiding the Leaky Cauldron, the ice cream parlour and the Quidditch
store. I know for a fact that Order members have been stationed there permanently and
in Tom’s case have been told to keep a look-out specifically for you.
At this point Harry wasn’t even surprised anymore by the lengths the headmaster was going
to. Well, he was slightly miffed that the man apparently believed Harry’s interests solely
revolved around food and Quidditch. Okay, so maybe that would have been not too far off a
year or so ago…
There was another Order meeting today. I apologize, cub, but this close to the moon it
was impossible not to speak up about Mundungus’ appalling behaviour towards
SiriPadfoot’s legacy. Pads always stood up for Mundungus, I can feel my wolf prowling

just thinking about your message! I do not think they made the connection. But, really,
setting a whole stall on fire, Harry?
The headmaster did make sure to remind Mundungus that the Order’s purpose is the
fight against You-Know-Who. We are not a group of petty criminals, as Molly put it.
Albus then went on by bringing up the blood wards around your home again. He thinks
it of the utmost importance that you return even if just for a day to keep them running.
Harry, whatever else might be said about Headmaster Dumbledore, and as much as I
disagree on ever letting you go back there, I do think he truly believes to act in your best
interests. He honestly believes that you will need your relatives’ home as a safe space
away from the Wizarding World’s hysterics about you.
The thought about Fletcher had not only Moony prowling. Harry still felt the itch to set
something on fire, something more than just that tarp full of stolen goods. And to be honest,
wasn’t that bit about the Order’s purpose a little hypocritical considering Dumbledore had
been using the group’s resources all summer to supposedly find Harry? Voldemort was gone.
There was no bloody need to safeguard Harry like this any longer. And speaking of guarding
him, what was this about blood wards again?! He had been so sure that ship had sailed the
day he decided to go against the headmaster’s direct order and left the Dursley residence
behind.
He was not going back. No matter what else he decided to do with his life, he was not going
back to the Dursleys.
That said, voices of discontent are getting louder in the Order. People do no longer truly
see the need for it as You-Know-Who is gone. They want to leave the past, and all our
losses, behind and I cannot begrudge them this want. I should be glad about this, as
should you, but I cannot help but be disgusted angry on your behalf. With the exception
of a few, they just no longer care even though to them you have vanished from the face of
the earth.
On that note:
I might not have been able to visit Padfoot, but I do have a sneaking suspicion that two
redheaded someones had more luck. The twins looked suspiciously unconcerned with the
whole topic of a missing Potter as well. Anything you want to tell me there?
With a squeak (cough, manly noise of surprise, cough) Harry dropped the letter and slapped
his hands over his rapidly heating face. Counting back from 10, he listened to his fluttering
heartbeat for a moment, before looking up at the ceiling. Well, at least he was alone for this.
It wasn’t as if Remus knew what he had just asked. It just so happened that the last time
Harry had seen the twins he had not only just wanked to an incestuous imagery of them,
using their shower and shampoo while at it, but also had just been perceived as himself - as in
Harry Potter, not Harley. And maybe he had done a runner right after. And hadn’t so much as
sent an owl ever since.

Yeah, it was safe to say that Harry in all his glorious teenage wisdom had very wisely ignored
the obvious Hippogriff in the room and very much not confronted his feelings. His feelings
for a pair of handsome, roguish, mischievous pranksters with too knowing smiles, too much
consideration and, and…
Right. Back to the letter.
Cub, I miss you. Is there a way you could safely make your way to my apartment in
London? The one I sent you the keys to in the last letter. It didn’t look like anyone had
been there when last I went to check which gives me hope that your current
accommodations are much better than the Order has quite theatrically been theorizing.
If you do want to visit, make sure to take the muggle route. You likely know your way
around the subway better than I ever will.
I will be there on the 29th. Me leaving for the moon won’t raise any suspicions as
headquarters currently is quite the busy place, so it should be safe. And Harry, do not,
under any circumstance, come there early.
Take care.
Remus
Ps How is your homework coming along? Have you been to the public library on Vertic
yet?
And there it was, the stab of guilt when thinking about returning to Hogwarts. Harry had
honestly avoided thinking about school, or more specifically thinking about returning to
school, these last days. It was easy enough what with work, his fears about the twins knowing
the truth (although going by this letter, they didn’t seem inclined to share their knowledge
with the Order), and of course all that information suddenly in reach after Sanguini’s visit.
If he was being brutally honest with himself, he knew he didn’t want to return. Oh he would
miss Hogwarts. Dearly. Desperately, actually. But what he wouldn’t miss was the people
there, the expectations, the constant switching from adoration to shunning by the drop of a
hat. He would miss his friends, but hadn’t he made new ones here outside the little bubble
that was Hogwarts? And he could always see Hermione, Ron, Gin, Luna, Neville and all the
others during the holidays. Him staying in the Wizarding World would actually make regular
visits with all of them possible. That is, if he could somehow hide the whole Harley
masquerade while still using it to keep his freedom.
But that last one was a thought for another day, he would confront that issue when it actually
became one. (No, he was not again wilfully ignoring a certain twin situation. He was
prioritising. See? Very mature of him.) He already knew he wasn’t in any danger of being
denied access to his magic because he had managed to pass enough OWLs. And he had read
those leaflets from Lycus’ bookshop, he knew he could take more OWLs at the Ministry and
even go on and take his NEWTs there if he so wished. All he really needed to do was apply.
If he actually dropped out of school, there wouldn’t even be a time limit as to when he had to
take those examinations. Sure, future employers where likely to give him some side-eye if he

took too long, but …let’s be honest, he was Harry Potter. And as much as he disliked the
hubbub that was generally made about that fact, it did have its upsides. Just the media
coverage alone any future establishment would receive for employing him would probably
guarantee him most jobs. Not that he intended to use this little aspect of his unwanted fame.
But it was soothing to know either way.
His thoughts were interrupted once again by the flutter of wings - and agitated noises of
discontent he couldn’t really name as either huffs or screeches or maybe squeaking or“Oh for Merlin’s saggy third ball, not again!”
In came Hedwig, her gorgeous glowy plumage still slightly marred by flecks of browns,
flapping her wings dramatically.
“Is that Bean?” Harry asked his owl aghast at the sight of a certain colourful fluffball caught
in a vicious beak being rudely thrown, caught in deadly talons, and thrown again all over the
parlour. “Hedwig! Let them down!”
Giving him the evil eye, his beloved owl flew up into the highest rafters, settled herself there
comfortably… before unceremoniously dropping her fluffy burden. Of course this would be
the moment Harry forgot the little fact that he was indeed a wizard. Shouting, he dived to
catch his Pygmy Puff, just barely managing and cradling the tiny thing to his chest while
landing face first on the rough parlour floor. Seeker reflexes without a broom weren’t much
to write home about, apparently.
“Oh no no no, please don’t be…” Sitting up, Harry looked down at the little thing in his
hands that he wasn’t yet sure was actually a living breathing being and not just a really clever
enchantment. “Be okay, you hear me? What am I going to tell the twins, if… not that they’re
ever gonna talk to me again, but… oh good, you’re still…er.. alive?”
The big luminous eyes blinking up at him seemed no worse for wear, although Bean’s fur
looked a little ragged. For all but the moment it took for Harry to make eye contact, anyway.
Glowing briefly golden, the Pygmy Puff visibly recovered, using… Harry’s magic to restore
itself? Considering this was quite reminiscent of the moment the fluff ball had decided to
change its colours back in the twins’ store, Harry wasn’t too fazed. Really, he was just glad
the thing was whole again. And maybe wondering why his magic chose to manifest in
rainbow fur.
Glancing up, he frowned at Hedwig. She had been quite the priss ever since Harry had dared
to mar her very recognisable plumage so that she would still be able to carry his post
anonymously. Hedwig also had not taken well to the new addition, but honestly, this was
getting ridiculous.
“Seriously, lovely, this can’t go on,” Harry admonished. “If this is you being territorial, just-”
He rubbed a hand through his hair agitatedly, messing up his braid in the process. “You’re not
replaceable, Hedwig. You know this, right?”
He got a reproachful bark in response, but at least she wasn’t turning away from him like she
had started doing lately. He wasn’t going to try and pet her any time soon, but this was an

improvement at least. Well, for him anyway. With a sigh too heavy for his 16 years, Harry got
up, taking Bean back with him as he went to sit in his corner again.
Running his fingers absentmindedly through rainbow fur, he looked back down at Remus’
letter. Meeting up would be wonderful. There was a reason for his very concentrated effort at
immersion into the newly discovered wonders of the Wizarding World, a reason for the way
he slipped into the role of the unassuming waitress near effortlessly, a reason for the need to
fill his mind with all the new information he could get his hands on. Well, there were actually
a handful of those reasons, but one was a very Sirius-shaped ache in his chest that maybe,
just maybe, would be soothed by seeing Remus… by seeing Sirius’ mate and by the sounds
of it, being forgiven for his stupid, short-sighted mistake.
Merlin, he had managed to not think about Sirius in his tiny dark hospital room for nearly a
week. Harry felt abruptly sick. He was the absolute worst person in the world. Here he was,
spending a relaxed day just leisurely reading (determinedly keeping his mind busy), playing
at romance (deceiving his friends), making plans for a future free of the responsibility his
very name had always carried (running away). And all this time Sirius was laid up in St
Mungo’s, comatose for unknown reasons, because he had come to Harry’s aid. Had come
help Harry when Harry should have known better, when it was Harry’s fault that anyone was
in danger that day in the first placeBean chirped at him.
“Right,” Harry sighed. “You’re right, there’s not really anything I can do about it,” he
muttered to the fluff ball, blinking rapidly to keep from feeling even shittier.
Or was there? Harry was no healer and he couldn’t be sure how adept the middle-aged healer
he had bribed hired to take care of his godfather was. He could really only hope for the best
on that front, but he could actually do something for Sirius’ reputation. Well, at least he could
try. Harry already knew that people, as much as they currently were once again treating him
with misplaced reverence, were never likely to listen to a 16-year-old kid informing them
about their own misgivings. People in general did not like to be made aware of their
mistakes. But Harry had the significant advantage of being on a first name basis with the
current Minister.
Granted, Kingsley was technically only Interim Minister (Or that’s what Hermione had called
it. Really, with the public opinion so obnoxiously light-oriented, Harry thought it would need
Dumbledore stepping up himself for anyone to beat Kingsley to the position now.), but he
was the one in power right now. No time like the present to make use of the hype and fame he
never wanted.
“Do you think I could just write him a letter?” Harry asked the fluff now sitting on the
notebook he had solely dedicated to the heavy tome a certain vampire had left him.
Bean gave him a solemn blink of currently golden eyes.
“Kingsley’s part of the Order and they are.. were…? All looking for me this summer,” Harry
continued, trying to hype himself up a bit for the idea. “If I were to send the letter with

Hedwig and have her go straight to Mione or Ron afterwards, it should be safe, right? And he
would know it’s really me because of Hedwig, and-”
Said owl gave a soft hoot that Harry decided to interpret as agreement.
“Alright.” Harry nodded to himself, pulling a sheet of parchment over. “Let’s do this.”
It was better than beating himself up over things out of his control. At least he could try.

Later, when Hedwig was only a speck of white in the sky, winging her way to the esteemed
Ministry of Magic, Harry turned back to his vampire gift. He would have his purification
ritual in three days, on the 28th which would be the only full moon before Mabon and
therefore Harry’s only chance to clear away the negative magic residue on his core.
Would he even be up to visiting Remus?
Having listened to Sanguini for hours and having immersed himself quite a bit in the book
the vampire had left him, Harry had a better, if still hazy on the exact details, understanding
of what was ahead of him. He understood that the attack in Knockturn that night (and, though
he didn’t tell Fergus this, all his previous encounters of the same fashion) had left him with
more than a bruised back. The physical injuries of magical attacks were the most obvious but
not the only damage that could happen. It was much more complicated and Harry didn’t
delude himself into thinking he had even grasped the most basic rules of it. All he could
really say so far was that magical cores were like very intricately woven balls of yarn (that
got drafted into being Crookshank’s toy), each and every one individual in its make-up and
sensitive to outward influences.
Only they weren’t actually spheres of any kind at all and magi only used the spheric form to
help visualise and- yeah. It was complicated. So he had decided to think of the residue stuff
as bruising on his not-quite-yarn-shaped magical core. He was also loath to call the residue
‘dark magic’ as it was wholly intent based, if Sanguini was to be believed.
But back to his vampire gift.
Feeling quite safe in the empty pub on a Sunday, he had felt secure in reading his newest
book out in the open for once. There was a certain magic to the tome. He had no doubt it was
very much priceless, something he would never be able to find in any common bookstore or
the public library on Vertic Alley that had completely slipped his mind until Remus reminded
him. He should swing by there and check their opening times soon. But this book,
ridiculously heavy and soft from what Harry thought were likely centuries of use, felt
different than anything he had ever touched. And it wasn’t just the material.
He had already filled half of the newest notebook with facts about rituals in general, the
context of magical holidays and possible effects on the magic of the people participating.
And Magic, with a capital M. Magic as an entity. The book stated all these things as facts,
things that had only ever been alluded to in the other texts he had read this summer (which

admittedly weren’t many apart from his old school books) or mentioned in passing in the
conversations he had overheard in the pub. It was… a completely new world in a way.
A world, Harry felt, he actually wanted to be a part of. Which, after his experiences these last
five years, had ceased to be the case a while back. He wasn’t sure when it happened, maybe
sometime around when an 11-year-old obliterated a man and no one so much as batted an
eye. Little Harry back in his first year had been awestruck by the magical world and hell-bent
on being a part of it, never realising he was missing too much context to begin with. Then, of
course, his misadventures all over the school grounds, and in the last two years notably
branching out further, had happened.
Let’s just say it had been some time since Harry had felt he could actually belong. Felt like he
wanted to belong.
There was a tiny chirrup and Harry glanced back up from the heavy pages to find Bean sitting
next to his tea cup. Ever since Sanguini left a bag of Berty Bott’s, the Pygmy Puff had started
showing interest in actual food now and then. Harry, admittedly, hadn’t really thought much
about it considering Fred had made it quite clear the little creatures were beings of magic.
Enchantments? Clever Transfiguration? Or maybe an actual breed? Either way, if Harry had
his CoMC information right, they technically should be powered by either ambient magicks
or the power of their chosen individual. Bean did both, it seemed.
“Want some?” Harry questioned, reaching over to see if his companion was interested in the
last few sips, only to freeze.
There, at the bottom of his very empty tea cup, were the dregs of what used to be Fergus’
preferred blend. Only, the mush formed a picture that even Harry with his questionable
understanding of the craft of Tessomancy could clearly recognise. In a form much too clear to
be coincidental the number 3 showed itself on the bottom of Harry’s cup. Now, if only he had
not left behind the dreaded Unfogging the Future Trelawney had liked to reference in her
class (whenever she wasn’t busy monologuing over unfortunate students and their likely
passing) when he ran for it earlier this summer. (*)
Outside a pub down a shady alley two young men were staring down the washed-out wood of
an ominous door. Getting here in broad daylight opposed to their last visit at night held a
special sort of exhilaration that set their senses on full alert. Nerves prickling with a mix of
the anticipation of solving a mystery that reminded them of their school days in an ancient
castle full of hidden passages, the worry about a dear friend, and something more. Something
deeper and achingly familiar yet new.
Two freckled hands reaching for each other in an unconscious gesture of seeking and
providing comfort, Fred and George Weasley prepared to confront their latest fear. Although
they had known this person for such a short time, the mere thought that they might be wrong
about this was nerve-wrecking.
They might lose a friend today.
They might find one thought lost.

And maybe they would walk home with broken hearts later.
But maybe, just maybe…

Chapter End Notes

Quite a few owls, quite some numbers. Oh and a cliffhanger. Mhm what was this arc
called again...
(*) Tessomancy (aka Tasseography, tasseomancy, tassology, or tasseology) is a
divination or fortune-telling method that interprets patterns in tea leaves, coffee grounds,
or wine sediments.
Also, is it just me or... seriously, I have no idea where the last year went. Sorry about
