Chapter 23: Glowing
Chapter Notes

Hi, excuse the cursing ;)
Because there was some confusion: the snippets in cursive are quotes from the Daily
Prophet articles that I sprinkled through the earlier chapters.
IT'S TIME!

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Sunday, 25th August 1996
The sun’s golden light was casting ever deeper shadows along the dusty alley known not for
its oh so clever name but its inhabitants. Although, George thought distractedly, Knockturn
Alley truly was more known for what people thought they knew about its inhabitants, wasn’t
it? Much like Harry himself. In any case, Harry had picked quite the Slytherin hide-out while
going at it in bold Gryffindor fashion… and maybe George should stop thinking so much in
school house categories?
“(…) colour of the year! The Saviour wasn’t available for comment, but close friends of
his claim Harry Potter always has had a special spot in his heart for the colour… (…)”
Fred at his side gave an impatient sigh – both at the waiting they were doing yet again and
the wandering thoughts he could just tell his twin was once more indulging in. Gred and his
spacing, seriously. How did he even manage to think about anything else but the view right in
front of them?
There was Harley, just visible through the grimy windows, cute ruffled skirt swaying around
those toned thighs with every step, as they focused on prepping the parlour… Fred gave
himself a mental clap on the back. That was the correct way of saying it, wasn’t it? If Harry
was Harley, then he couldn’t go and think of Harley as a ‘she’ – unless that was what Harry
would prefer? But thinking of Harry as Harry and ‘he’ while looking at him dressed up like
this was going to take some real dedication, too, what with society training them to associate
skirts and dresses with girls. And wasn’t it a disguise anyway? Or… was it? Considering how
long this prank had been going, Fred felt his thoughts going all knotty at trying to point out
where one started and the other ended.
He sighed again, now he was spacing too. Damn. It was a headache, but an interesting one,
and Fred really wanted to address that whole point of… well, address, because he was
invested, wasn’t he? So he would go with ‘they’ until he knew more. He could do that much
and it made his brain hurt less. Now, if they could just move already. He gave his feet a
frustrated glance where they felt very much rooted to the spot.

“(…) witnesses claiming to have seen Harry Potter himself at the magical resort
Renaissance Island, Aruba! Surely this glamorous holiday destination (…)”
“Found it yet?” Forge asked Gred.
“Still scraping around,” Gred answered Forge.
Well, Gryffindor courage could sometimes be hard to come by.
“You gonna confront them any time soon?” Fergus’ deep voice rumbled from behind the bar
making Harry twitch in place.
Technically he knew it was hot out and the parlour of Bandy-Legs only moderately cooler
than the outside, but he couldn’t help the shiver running down his back. Well, that and the
cold sweat breaking out every time he so much as glanced at the twins not far outside the
entrance. They were just standing there in the middle of Knockturn Alley like they hadn’t
been attacked here a few days back, like they weren’t garnering suspicious and hostile looks
from everyone passing by while they just stood there baking in the late afternoon sun.
Why were they even here?
And yes, Harry very much preferred pretending he hadn’t noticed them over actually
approaching them. But Fergus was right. It was dangerous for them to stand out there, not to
mention the bar would open soon and customers were bound to start trickling in. He huffed
and stared hopefully (piteously) at his boss. Couldn’t he“No,” Fergus snorted before Harry ever even tried asking out loud. “Don’t get me wrong.
Ain’t too averse to seeing those two menaces taken down ‘nother peck or ten, but you’re the
kind that’s gonna feel bad for them.” He sat down the bottle he had been inspecting, giving
Harry a long-suffering look of his own. “You can play hide ‘n seek all you want, but those
two have the potential to be major game changers one way or the other.” He cracked his neck
lazily, having used up his words for the day. “Wanna make sure they’re on your side then,” he
added in a grumble before moving up the stairs towards his flat, conversation very much
over.
"(…) and what would his parents, the late Lord and Lady Potter, say about their son
traipsing all around the world instead of focusing on his duties at home? But surely the
Saviour would never abandon the good and righteous people of.. (…)"
He wasn’t sure what had finally made them step inside. Maybe they just couldn’t stand the
burning summer sun any longer, or maybe they had finally decided on how to go about
breaking his heart. And didn’t that just sound pathetic?
Either way, when the twins stepped into the parlour, doors creaking ominously where they
were swinging slightly in place before settling down, Harry felt himself go very still. This
was it. They were now going to throw the whole Harley farce into his face, probably laugh at
his disguise while at it – never mind he was wearing one of those gorgeous swaying skirts the
twins had bought for him… for Harley in muggle London.

It didn’t matter anymore now, did it? He had deceived them, decided to keep on deceiving
them more than once and then had gone and busted his own lie in front of them. It didn’t
change the ache in his chest any. Harry could hear tiny Bean meeping softly at him from
inside his apron’s pocket.
The one in front was Fred, he thought, always straight-forward, not meant to wait and
evaluate. But George was just a step behind, holding a package wrapped in brown,
nondescript paper. There was a moment of absolute stillness in which the three stared at each
other from across the otherwise empty parlour. Harry had the absurd thought of a stare down
in a saloon, some random eagle calling dramatically in the background, right out of one of
those wild west movies his cousin enjoyed… oh and there he was again with basing reality in
movies.
“Errr,” Fred finally voiced, awkwardly scratching at the back of his head for a moment,
before straightening. There was a light in his eyes, an expression hidden behind a mien of
determinedness, that Harry couldn’t place. “We need to talk.”
A second.
And another.
Then George started guffawing from behind his twin, stepping up and bumping their
shoulders together.
“Bro,” he snorted. “Did you really just go for the whole ‘We need to talk’ spiel?”
“What was I supposed to say? Comment on the freaking oven that is the weather or
something?” Fred cried in offence, the two dissolving into squabbling between themselves.
Fred and George.
George and Fred.
They were the same as always, yet not.
Harry wasn’t sure how to react. Part of him wanted to just stand there and stare at these two,
drinking in his fill of their presence before they remembered why they were even there. (And
how by Merlin’s sock draw did they manage to look so pristine in this heat, wearing buttondowns and all?)
Another part, the one that had him clenching his hands into his skirt to hide the tremble there
not only from the twins but from himself as well… let’s just say the thought of up and
running, maybe find another place, start over yet again, didn’t sound so daunting anymore.
Really, he could probably get an apartment in the Muggle World now that he officially had
his OWLs and no reason to fear ‘underage magic’ …
He never ran from danger. He always faced it head on, even sought it out as some critical
voices liked to snarkily point out.
Or did he?

Did he always stand up to his fears like the Gryffindor he was supposed to be? Wasn’t he
currently cultivating his inner Slytherin, hiding in plain sight, fooling everyone to his own
advantage? The Slytherin the Sorting Hat had time and again told him he truly was. Harry
had wanted to believe it had been Voldemort in his head the hat had sensed, as disturbing as
that thought was. (Not thinking about it.)
But deep down he had always known the truth.
And if he was being honest with himself, Harry didn’t think using the disguise of ‘Harley’
was in any way shape or form wrong. He was so done with being the figurehead of a war he
had never understood, an ideology he had poked holes into all through summer. He was done
with being everyone’s scapegoat, hailed in class with Merlin one moment, shunned and
treated like the source of all misfortune the next. (And let’s not even get him started on the
ridiculousness that was the house system at Hogwarts. Which eleven-year-old is set in their
ways anyway?)
So why, why was he now contemplating turning tail and just run?
A tiny tongue licking against his cramping fingers managed to finally shake Harry from his
panicky thoughts. Glancing down, he watched Bean make their way out of the pocket and
precariously balancing in the cloth folds.
“Hey, what are you doing?” Harry couldn’t help but question out loud as he carefully cupped
his hands around the fluff ball. “It’s not like you have legs or something, you know?” He
lifted the Pygmy Puff up to eye level, deciding to ignore the sudden silence from the twins.
No more time for panic attacks, all this waiting around for the inevitable wasn’t Harry’s thing
anyway. They were here, in broad daylight. No fear of being seen, even after that attack…
stupid. So very stupid, yet“Well, if it isn’t the estranged feather duster?” Fred commented suddenly very much in front
of Harry.
“Little rainbow-y extra,” George added, checking the Pygmy Puff over from Harry’s other
side. “Did you get an upgrade to sidekick already?”
“Guys-”
Harry didn’t get a say in the matter, though. Bean, loyal thing that they were, puffed up in
Harry’s cupped hands and stared down the looming twins.
“What?” They questioned in unison, looking from the Puff to Harry and back.
Only to be blown a tiny raspberry before Bean turned and cuddled up to Harry’s thumb.
“Oi!” Fred cried out in surprise. “We’re your makers, fuzzy!”
George, eyebrows raised but a calculating glint in his eyes, nodded sagely at his twin’s side.
“Such betrayal-”

“Treason!”
“Perfidy!”
The door swinging open at their backs luckily stopped the twins from once again devolving
into one of their verbal vomit spats. It also spurred Harry into motion as he realised the time
and the fact that patrons were already arriving. Hurriedly tucking an indignant Bean back into
his apron’s pocket, he had his wand out and was directing the last seats from where they had
still been stacked on a few tables.
The first one through the door was a nondescript wizard in robes just this side short of being
shabby that Harry recognized but had never so much as exchanged a word with before. He
was one of those few that tended to come early, stay late, and hold onto a single drink
throughout the whole night. A drink he generally ordered from Fergus, never so much as
gracing Harry with more than a passing glance. Which would be fine normally, but his boss
wasn’t around yet and the twins were already making nuisances out of themselves.
Because, obviously.
“Ah duty calls,” George sighed theatrically.
“Rest, ye matters of the heart,” Fred proclaimed, hand grasping in an overt gesture at the
fabric across his chest.
The wizard stopped short, frowned at the three occupants of the parlour, eyes roaming the
space with a hint of desperation before his gaze settled with a scowl on Harry.
“Ocky Rot, …mate,” he settled on ordering. “And don’t try to give me one of those flimsy
muggle glasses, I want a proper tankard.”
Glancing at the twins for a second, Harry straightened his apron and settled back into his role.
He was Harley, the waitress of Bandy-Legs and this was a customer. With a nod just as much
to the wizard as to himself he was off behind the counter, gently nudging Bean to keep them
in his pocket as he went. The twins had gone silent in the background – never a good sign if
you asked Harry – but he forced himself to concentrate on the low-quality liquor he was
pouring. Berry Ocky Rot was a sort of fruit wine with some 15 or more percent alcohol.
Magi, as he had learned, served the brew hot when coming in tankards and cold when in
glasses, so a warming charm it was. And even though cooling charms still went a bit wonky
for him, warming charms had been added to his repertoire over the last weeks.
As the alcohol-heavy steam of the hot and somehow frothing wine rose to his nose, Harry
hurriedly set the tankard down in front of the still scowling wizard. He could see the twins
from the corner of his eye whispering and watching. Plotting. Yeah, that wouldn’t do.
“Anything else?” Harry politely asked, getting a grunt in reply he was going to interpret as a
negative. “Right. Okay.” He could see Fred edging closer, one hand behind his back and
nope, not gonna happen. “Fergus will be down shortly,” he informed the man before turning
to the twins. “Come on up, I think… I think, you’re right. We need to talk.”

He once again ignored his fluttering nerves, jittery hands and the little voice scathingly
questioning why he was even bothering… Even if they forgave him, they wouldn’t
understand. It was weird what he was doing here, wasn’t it? He was weird, a freak, always
had been. But they were here now and clearly up to no good. Whatever else was about to
happen, Harry wasn’t going to let them do anything that would hurt Fergus’ business. Not on
his watch. Squaring his shoulders, Harry grasped at his vaunted Gryffindor courage. He could
do this. He would do this.
There was a snort and some incomprehensible muttering from the customer, but neither Harry
nor the twins had a mind to check what that was about right then and there. And really,
George’s eyes were busy straying to the hem of a certain skirt as he and his brother followed
Harley up the narrow stairway anyway.

The room was tiny. The air was stuffy. There were pieces of parchment stuck to the walls on
three sides and heavy curtains letting in just enough light to see the neatly folded clothes on
the lonely chair. There wasn’t really any room for three, but the twins followed Harley in,
squeezing into the corners near the door, unwilling to occupy the single bed. Harley too
didn’t seem particularly comfortable standing there in the cramped space between bed and
wall. Their back to them, shoulders tense, and body rigid, Harley looked impossibly fragile
and George found himself wishing they would turn around.
“This is awkward,” Fred next to him stated the obvious, eliciting a little choking sound from
the lean figure in front of them. It sounded like Harley couldn’t decide whether to laugh or
cry and George just wanted to reach out“We brought your dress,” his brother went on, shifting his weight from foot to foot
unconsciously. “Repaired it and all.” Fred glanced his way, question in his eyes, but really,
George was stuck on wanting to grasp his hopefully-soon-to-be-love and hold the fuck on.
“Aw come on, Harley” his brother went on when still no one would say anything. George
silently wondered if tension could be visible or if the slight flickering that seemed to cling to
Harley was their magic manifesting. “Please turn around. I can’t with this tension,” Fred
moaned after no more than three more seconds of continued silence. “Harley,” he whined
obnoxiously.
And yeah, George thought, he really should appreciate his twin’s impatient all-or-nothing
attitude more, because Harley was laughing.

A smile was tugging at his lips. He couldn’t help himself, really. Those idiotic lovable sods.
So he was turning around, daring himself to look straight into their eyes… or George’s
apparently, as the younger twin immediately pushed forward, thrusting the package he had
been holding into Harry’s hands.
Not that Harry was looking, caught in the blue gaze, heart rate accelerating when calloused
hands touched his. Not that he really could bring himself to care for the dress he had honestly

missed when Fred huffed and shouldered his brother just enough aside that the two were
crowding Harry in the small space.
Only, Harry really didn’t feel crowded or trapped.
He felt safe.
Heart skipping, fingers tingling, cheeks warming… but secure. Secure in the sudden
knowledge that whatever else these two might be, they had never and would never be a threat
to him. There was something there, maybe the undefined light in Fred’s gaze, or the neardesperate way George’s hands were still clinging to his.
“Harry,” he heard himself correcting. “It’s Harry to you.”
He should have known. He really should have known better.
Should never have assumed, should never have judged and essentially made the decision for
them. Sure, it had been for his own safety in the beginning. But this was them, this was
George and Fred, this were the prankster twins that had stood by him every time the
wizarding populace decided to do another 180. They did it in their own unique way, but these
two were always on his side. So he wasn’t the girl they probably (maybe?) wanted, but he
was their friend and that counted for something, didn’t it?
Two blinding smiles, bright and relieved and… proud?
“That you are,” George agreed, fingers squeezing around Harry’s.
“That you will always be,” Fred added.
“Though if you wanna continue on as ‘Harley’, or go by ‘Hogwarts’, that’s fine too.”
“Doesn’t change a thing.”
“Makes me less likely to shout across the street, maybe.”
“Would shout it from the rooftops still, me.”
“Oh hoggy Hogwarts, my sweet!”
They shared a short grin before turning back to Harry.
“You’re you,” George settled on with a light shrug.
“And either you can wear them skirts, like wow,” Fred nodded vigorously.
Rapidly blinking up at them, Harry couldn’t and wouldn’t fight his answering smile. This
was… good, right? There was a warm glow slowly but surely spreading all through his chest,
down his extremities and right into his fingertips. They were here, they knew, they knew and
yet it was okay and-

Then the door banged open, making them all jump in place, the package drop, the paper rip,
the dress fanning out very visibly.
“Bunny, why is that grump down there blabbing about trollops ‘n cathouses-”
A curse that would have had Mrs. Weasley up in arms had she heard. An abrupt turn and then
Fergus’ hulking figure was blocking the door, but he was half turned away. Stoically staring
at the ceiling, hands opening and closing at his sides.
Still good, right?

“(…)The best is just enough for our Saviour!”

Chapter End Notes

So we survived another year, let's not break that habit!

Chapter 24: There is a new chapter!
Chapter Summary

Hi!
If you just want to continue reading the story, click right on to the next chapter.
If you need a recap and/or want to know what has happened with this story this last year,
scroll down.
Thanks!

Chapter Notes
See the end of the chapter for notes

What is this chapter?
1. Info about what happened with the story
2. A solid recap of the plot
3. Changes about to happen
4. author says hi

1. What happened here?
My last post here was on 1st January 2023. A lot has changed, yet not.
Firstly, and probably the most important to you, is that the plot is still the same. You
technically can just continue reading where you left off.
But if you're like me and NEED those details of Harry's everyday life, the more detailed
scenes and conversations, and even some Daily Prophet articles, then I would recommend
doing a full re-read.
If that's not for you, though, I've got you. Here is a full recap of the story up to the end of
chapter 22 'Glowing'.

2. Recap
Arc I : Mind

Due to a change of Tom Riddle’s Horcrux-riddled mind (He Who Knows How to Riddle
Riddled Riddles) there are only ever three soul anchors created. Not even Dumbledore
foresaw this turn of events and so Harry Potter manages to defeat Voldemort in the
Department of Mysteries at the end of his fifth year.
During the fight his godfather Sirius Black is hit with a mysterious curse and nearly falls
through the Veil of Death. His friend and mate, the werewolf Remus Lupin, manages to catch
him in time, but Sirius winds up comatose. Meanwhile Harry experiences the horrors of
possession, discovering a disturbing detail about himself, and the Dark Lord’s soul shards
diminish in the ensuing fight.
That summer the Wizarding World celebrates the end of a war they had not even fully
acknowledged and name Harry Potter the Saviour, the second coming of Merlin, while
speculations about his summer adventures run rampant. During all this the boy in question is
once again sequestered away at his relatives’ house, the Dursley’s. Still reeling from the fight
and the discovery of having been a Horcrux, a soul shard of Voldemort, he has a minor
accident that costs him his glasses. His aunt has no use for a half-blind nephew and resorts to
locking him up permanently.
Harry decides he has enough and leaves in the dark of night, spurning all advice, and
especially Dumbledore’s explicit instructions to stay put. During his decidedly non-magical
track, thank you Decree for the Reasonable Restriction of Underage Sorcery, he comes up
with a way to hide in plain sight and for once have an actual summer. Thus Harley is born, a
slightly awkward waitress at a shady pub down Knockturn Alley.
As Harry earns his keep at Fergus Wanlockhead’s bar, he slowly but surely discovers new
sides to himself – new and possibly not compatible with the image of Harry Potter the Hero.
Er. Saviour. Who would have thought that wearing skirts, dresses, and overall pretty things
could be so much fun? Sight still very much impaired, Harry learns about himself and the
Wizarding World from an entirely new perspective, pun not intended. He enjoys the freedom
of anonymity, but also has to work through the obstacles that come with it. His developing
magic helps him along.
Meanwhile a certain set of twins is busy building up their very own business. Of course their
path crosses that of an intriguing waitress with a kick-ass reputation. They take an immediate
liking to ‘her’, testing Harry’s daring until he starts to truly feel secure in his disguise. He
soon starts enjoying his time with the flirty twins, discovering yet another side to himself.
Too bad Fred and George seem to very much adore Harley, not ever even mentioning Harry
Potter in ‘her’ company.
Then one night the two visit their new friend at the bar she’s working at, getting thoroughly
drunk in the process, and end up ambushed on their way back home. It is Harley that saves
them and it is Harley they need to save in return when ‘she’ gets hit by a spell unexpectedly.
The next day Harry aka Harley wakes up at the twins’ flat. He discovers his love for coffee,
very much enjoys a shower, and accepts his secret feelings for Fred and George… when
Molly Weasley floos in. The Weasley matriarch proceeds to hurt feelings left and right.
George has a monologue about how he and his twin only pursue Harley, because ‘she’
reminds them so much of a certain Saviour they adore.

Arc II: Body (up to chapter 22 ‘Glowing’)
We start with a monologue of Fred’s and get a bit of an insight into being part of the entity
that is the Weasley twins. Fred then manages to be a total Gryffindor and spooks Harry out
the shop door and onto a half-blind journey back home. He has a passenger in the form of a
rainbow-coloured Pygmy Puff. To actually make it home, though, Harry has to heavily
employ his new magical sense of ‘seeing’ magic when his eyes won’t properly do the job. He
might also discover a Vampire GPS™.
On his way back into the shady safety of Knockturn Alley, he runs across Mundungus
Fletcher selling off Sirius’ household goods and what Harry clearly identifies as Black family
heirlooms. His magic is not happy and sets fire to the stall.
He makes a run for it only to be confronted by his boss Fergus Wanlockhead about his
disappearance the night prior. Harry then learns he is not the only one with special abilities
around, and that apparently being hit with malicious curses has consequences beyond bodily
ouchies. Thus he learns about cleansing rituals, the apparent failings of all the adults in his
life as Harry Potter, and that he really needs one of those rituals asap.
A date is set, the 28th of August, the next full moon.
Meanwhile George and Fred discuss the obvious and conclude that they cannot confront
Harry about the whole Harley situation just then. For one, they have a business to run, and
secondly, they are unsure how to approach their friend without being left in the dust once
again.
Harry on the other hand does a deep-dive into the differences between Light and Dark magic,
clearly finds the information available lacking and then has a nightly visitor. Sanguini, a
vampire he met for the first time back in his fifth year, makes an appearance. He kindly
educates Harry on a lot of things and leaves him not only with a very informative book, but
also with the knowledge that the creature community isn’t particularly fond of Dumbledore.
A few days later the three have yet to reconnect. Harry receives a letter from Remus which
contains information about the Order of the Phoenix’ moves. He is also reminded of the
existence of a public library in Vertic Alley. His new companion, Bean the Mighty Pygmy
Puff, keeps Harry from becoming too depressed about his godfather’s fate and a letter to the
Interim Minister is written.
On Sunday 25th the twins decide enough is enough and venture back out to Knockturn. They
might be confused about the whole identity-gender-thing, but they like Harley and want
Harry. If that means accepting the strangeness that is both of these people, they are down for
it. Harry agrees.
We left them up in Harry’s tiny room with a cute summer dress and Fergus Wanlockhead
getting the wrong impression.

3. Changes about to happen
If you're doing a re-read, you will notice the chapter-in-a-chapter that is currently Chapter 1:
'Stifling'.
I did not like where the chapter cut off and when I added more details during my editing, it
felt even worse. So I intend to make two chapters out of one.
This might lead to some confusion when updating.
You might get notified about a new chapter when there won't be one - or at least not one you
haven't read yet.
So, just a heads up.

4. Hi!
My name's Rina, English is not my first language, and yes, I am the author of this work.
Why am I telling you this?
Probably, because I never did so before. Because seriously, who cares?
AO3 is so incredibly important to me, has helped me through some of the darkest and hardest
times of my life, and I just wanted to say Thank You. Yes, to you who is taking the time to
read this right now. Even if you never write a comment, just opening my works and actually
reading what I'm throwing at you? It means a lot. Like my words have actual meaning. Like
I'm making any sort of impact.
But it doesn't really matter who I am, because here my work is the important part. The stories
I have to tell, the worlds that live (sadly rent-free) in my head. And that's okay, makes me
actually more comfortable than it being about me as a person.
Yet, I believe that a story always tells you something about the author, whether they make it
obvious, or you have to read between the letters and lines. It's unavoidable. This is something
that tends to make me a bit uncomfortable. I like being this elusive non-entity no one truly
cares to find out more about as long as the chapters keep coming. Therefore, I know my work
tells the observant reader some things about me, but if I just keep writing, no one will bother
to look deeper. And I have to - keep writing, that is. Fellow authors might understand this
absolute need to write things down.
I need to write or my mind might not find the way back from storyweaving one day.
Dramatics aside, this is me, but more importantly: This is my work. Please enjoy.

Chapter End Notes

Hopefully this was as comprehensive yet clear as I wanted it to be. I had fun writing that
recap.
Now, ON WITH THE STORY <3

Chapter 25: Confusing
Chapter Notes

(make sure you go back to chapter 24 for a recap!)

See the end of the chapter for more notes

There was a long beat of silence. Harry wasn’t quite sure why he felt the need to straighten
out his clothes, or why his gaze kept being drawn down to the dress the twins had brought.
His dress. The pretty one he had thought lost when he woke up at the twins’ flat the morning
after the attack in Knockturn. It had been ripped, full of muck and just overall sad looking…
“Ah, good sir-”
George finally, after what felt like the most awkward minute of Harry’s life, started to speak.
“You see, it is not er-”
Fred was trying to jump in and continue his brother’s sentence like he normally would.
‘Trying’ being the operative word as, for the first time Harry could recall, both twins seemed
to be at a loss for words. Glancing between them, Harry noticed with some alarm the deep
blush creeping up two freckled necks. Why were they blushing? And why was Fergus“I know those trash bags in the bar sometimes think like crups with two dicks and talk like
they ain’t ever brushed their teeth, but-” Fergus mumbled to the ceiling before abruptly
clearing his throat and finally glancing at Harry. “Not the kind of conversation a young magi
should be privy to, I get that, but… lass, if I ever gave you the impression we’re that kind of
establishment…”
Again looking up to the ceiling, muttering under his breath, Fergus left Harry blinking in
confusion. Fred next to him made a low whining sound like something wounded. The
description of some of the folks that frequented Bandy-Legs was hilariously on point, but
what did their filthy gobs and mouthing off have to do with this situation? Harry was about to
ask just that when his boss finally seemed to decide enough was enough. Of what, Harry had
no idea.
“And you two,” he growled, fixing the twins with his dark glare. “You had me right fooled
that evening when you schmoozed your way in Harley’s friends’ good graces.” He took a
threatening step forward, looming over the twins like a wilder version of Snape. “Should’a
known better, sleazy freckled devils!” He snarled right in their suddenly pale faces, “Get out
of my house before I let the wards show you just why you don’t go around insulting my
family!”

As he barked his orders, the floor beneath their feet started to tremble, the walls humming in
tune as if gearing up to implode on them, swallow them whole right where they were stood.
“Wait. What?” Harry spoke over the ominous sound, frowning heavily at Fergus.
But of course, no one was listening to him. And why were the twins just standing there,
letting Fergus throw those insults at them? What even was this whole situation?
“Ah no, Harley,” George finally voiced just as Fergus roughly hauled both twins out into the
hallway. “Don’t interfere, he’s right. We shouldn’t have-”
“Indeed, you should not! Out. NOW!” The bartender’s voice boomed and the twins turned
tail, though Fred called back over his shoulder:
“It’s a misunderstanding, don’t worry! We’ll be in touch!”
“THE HELL YOU WILL!”
And then they were gone.
Harry blinked.
What just happened?
Listening to Fergus huffing and puffing down in the parlour, some low voices adding to the
mix from the few patrons already present, Harry stood there for a long moment. He was
trying to get his head around the whole situation, starting with the twins accepting him, over
his boss’ reaction, to the man quite literally kicking the twins out of the pub. This… didn’t
make any sense. Fergus knew George and Fred were Harry’s friends. Why would he..?
Slowly picking up the discarded dress, Harry smoothed the cleaned and repaired fabric. It
looked as good as new, the twins having done the impossible yet again. He was just running
his fingers over the pretty lace, when Fergus came back stomping up the stairs. It was the first
time since they found their rhythm living together that Harry felt uncomfortable in the
hulking man’s presence. He looked seriously pissed off, but there was also a disappointed and
hurt air around him that resonated much deeper with Harry than any aggression ever would.
“Put that flimsy thing away, Harley,” he grumbled, trying to take the dress and presumably
discard it.
“Wha- no!” Harry intervened, hugging his present close. “It was a present! Why would
you… what was all that about?”
Fergus blinked.
His dark eyes lingered on the clothes item before drifting up to meet Harry’s.
“You-” He stared some more. Only to finally groan like he used to back in the beginning
when Harry would still mess up the simplest tasks. “Of course you’re not aware. I keep
forgetting.”

What he was forgetting, Harry had no idea, just like he really had no idea what he yet again
didn’t know about. It had to be some culture thing. He should get a guide.
“Right. Right.. how do I…,” Fergus was mumbling to himself, ruminating in his head until a
shattering glass from downstairs pulled him back to the task at hand. “I get that you didn’t
know, but I cannot be sure about those monkeys. Light buggers they are, upstanding
wizarding folk, my arse. So you’re not going to see them until I am. Right.” He stated with
finality, nodding to himself.
Wait. Did he just ground Harry? Like some errand teenager?
Granted, that was basically the definition of Harry this summer (and probably most of his life
so far), but this time around he wasn’t actually doing anything wrong! Or was he? Did it
count if he didn’t know it was wrong?
“Now get your butt moving, we have customers,” Fergus continued. “And Harley… don’t
bring men- people- anyone up here while you’re alone. You hear me? And don’t accept any
gifts either!” (*)
Harry blinked. Huh. Was that a girl-thing? Like.. would it have been the same if everyone
was aware he wasn’t actually a girl? He hadn’t honestly thought passed getting his friends to
a more private place so they could talk without the worry of being overheard. They used to
sequester themselves away in one of the boys’ rooms back at the Burrow all the time. But
now, looking at it from someone else’s viewpoint, someone not in the know… yeah, he could
see how that might look suspicious. But what of the gift thing?
He sighed. Yet another question for his ever-growing list.
On Monday Harry realised Fergus hadn’t actually grounded him, just forbidden him from
seeing the twins. He was a bit unsure how he felt about it all and whether he thought his boss
even had the right to make this kind of decision for him. It seemed to be sparked by Fergus
being protective of him, but it still rankled.
So Harry wanted to visit the public library on Vertic Alley. He was obviously in need of more
books going by the host of questions that had cropped up in the wake of the twins’ last visit.
Seriously, what was with the gift thing? Going by Fergus’ reaction, it wasn’t exactly
something he wanted to ask about. Being able to discreetly look it up would be so much
easier on his pride. Maybe then he would understand the reactions and the subsequent ban
from meeting each other.
Some part of him honestly planned on making a detour to the twins’ shop on his way. But the
less impulsive part of him that had been nurtured all summer was hesitant. It was probably
better to look a few things up before he saw those two again. Just to be on the safe side,
right?
But coming back into his room after a quick shower derailed those plans. There, on his
window sill, sat a tiny little bird. A chaffinch, he thought, not uncommon at all, but not
exactly known to come inside. Or carrying mail for that matter.

“Hello,” he greeted, watching his feathery visitor curiously. “Is that for me?”
The chaffinch tilted its head, eying Harry sideways for a moment, before apparently coming
to a decision. It hopped off the sill, fluttered the short distance and barrelled right into Harry’s
chest in its attempt to land.
“Oh hey!” Carefully catching the little bird, Harry held it up to his face to get a better look.
“Your feet aren’t exactly made for landing on arms, I think. You’re not an owl, you know?”
He got an appalled chirp in answer.
“Well, you did a good job finding me, though. Let’s see what you’ve got there.”
Haz,
Meet us on the corner to Carkitt Market. We’ll be waiting.
F&G
Oh. Well, that was that. Did they mean right now? It sounded like it. And that way of address.
Harry smiled to himself, his ears turning a shade of pink, as he realised it was as neutral as
they could get while still addressing the message to him. It was the perfect blend of his
names, be it by birth or assumed.
“Right. Okay, thank you,” he told the finch and set it back down on the sill. “Now, what to
wear?”
And wasn’t that the question. The twins were in the know, he didn’t have to play Harley for
them any longer. If he could really still call it playing at being Harley. But he also couldn’t
exactly risk being seen as Harry. Opening his new trunk, fingers running gently over the
multitude of soft fabric, he thought back on the shopping trip. Specifically the reactions of
George and Fred. Would they still react the same now that they knew?

“I’m going to the library,” he called to Fergus as he prepared to leave. “I’ll be back in time
for opening. Do you need anything from the market?”
His attempt at sounding innocent and nonchalant was met with a very unimpressed look.
Fidgeting with the pretty straw hat Fred had chosen for him, Harry tried to wait patiently for
the verdict.
“… I realise I can’t exactly ground you,” Fergus grunted. “But don’t think I was joking last
night. Keep your head down if you have to venture into those areas.”
‘Those areas’, Harry knew, meant basically everywhere outside the safety of Bandy-Legs’
wards. Fergus didn’t reserve his profound distrust for either the folks of Knockturn or those
on Diagon. He was quite set in his ways of simply not trusting anyone outside his walls.

Carefully situating his hat to shield his face a bit from the scorching summer sun, Harry left
the pub. It was about twenty minutes since he received the twins’ message. Would they still
be there, waiting for him? What was he thinking, of course they would. Fred and George
were his friends, always had his back, and now they knew his darkest secret. Or, well, one of
his most secret secrets. He did feel bad about going so clearly against Fergus’ demand, and
he really would feel better if he could look the whole clothes situation up first, but alas, the
shortest way to the library was crossing Carkitt Market. He couldn’t exactly do that and not
meet the twins. At least he didn’t want to do that.
It would be okay. They were his friends.

They looked fabulous. Like they always did, but more. As he neared the corner to Carkitt,
Harry realised with a start that he was not the only one who put more effort into his
appearance today than he normally would.
Fred and George had definitely taken the time to put together one of their more dapper
outfits, clearly playing up the whole twin-thing, but with subtle differences here and there. So
while George went for blue accents, Fred had decided on bronze. While both had styled their
hair back, George had opted to accessorize with a time piece and Fred wore a wrist cuff.
Harry felt his gaze linger longer than necessary on the way fabric shifted across broad chests
and how the warm sunlight made freckled complexion glow. And he wasn’t the only one
looking.
Fred and George Weasley stood out even here on Carkitt Market with its abundance of
colourful people wandering about. People stole glances, but so did the twins. But their eyes
were only on Harry.
“Miss Harley,” Fred winked as Harry slowly closed in on them.
“Haz,” George smiled.

He had worried it would be awkward. Had worried that now the twins knew he wasn’t really
the girl they had flirted with, things would be different. And even though they had clearly
accepted his oddities and forgiven him as well, Harry had still expected them to maybe just
go back to treating him like they used to do back in school.
And in a way they did. But the longer they spent time together on the way to the library, the
more he realised Fred and George hadn’t ever really changed how they treated him. They had
treated Harley like they treated Harry, just with a whole lot more obvious flirting. Obvious
enough for even him to catch on.
The only difference he now noticed was the clear physical distance they kept.
“Is this,” he waved his hand around between them, “because of yesterday? Because of what
Fergus said? Or…” He eyed the way Fred kept his hands in his pockets and George his

behind his back when before they would lead him around, touching innocently but always
close. “Or is this because of me, because I’m… weird.”
A freak.
They abruptly stopped in their tracks.
“If this is about your self-esteem issue, Har- Haz, then no,” George declared firmly.
“Or if this is about something your shitty relatives said, then again, no,” Fred added, glaring.
Chewing on his bottom lip, Harry nodded.
“It’s just… you… you aren’t-,” he tried, but couldn’t bring the words out. Fidgeting in place,
he felt the heat rise up his neck in embarrassment.
And the twins just softened.
Fred glanced around quickly, then grabbed Harry’s hand and pulled him the rest of the way
off of the market and into the entrance of Vertic Alley. Here everything seemed more relaxed,
much less people were out and about. Not far down the street a building rose above the rest
of the surrounding architecture. The library, he thought distantly. He couldn’t recall ever
being here before.
Fred let go of Harry’s hand, but ducked down to meet his eyes instead. When green met blue,
he smiled reassuringly and let his brother take the lead.
“See,” George started, “We’re not truly the right people to ask about all this proper stuff.”
“Truly dreadful rascals we are,” Fred grinned unapologetically.
“But we do know some things,” George continued.
“Bit disconnected, we are, though.”
“Because even with the Weasley family being basically ostracised from Pureblood society,”
George said, glancing around to make sure they weren’t drawing much attention. “We are
still part of the magical world.”
“Were raised in it, weren’t we?”
“Somewhat.”
“Somehow.”
“Soso.”
“Meh.” They shrugged at each other.
“So, it is a cultural thing!” Harry finally intervened. But his interest in all things history and
culture was waking up, he wanted to understand this. “Is it like how you should behave

differently depending on the situation?”
“Yep. Very along the lines of basic Dos and Don’ts,” George agreed. “About how different
genders should be interacting.”
“All proper-like,” Fred added.
Harry frowned. The whole gender-thing was complicating things here. But he could work
with this, he would just have to remember two sets of rules. It wasn’t that different to muggle
society, really, at least as far as he could tell from his own experiences. Girls and boys got
regularly separated, be it for specific classes, any form of changing rooms, dorms… But the
not-touching felt rather dated to his muggle mind. Or maybe it was just him being raised in
England.
“Is there a guideline? Anything?” He questioned, hoping to maybe just pick one up.
“Well,” George rubbed his chin thoughtfully, “See, Haz, we’re boys.”
“Men. Manly men,” Fred corrected, standing a bit straighter.
“Yes. That.” His brother agreed. “We can tell you what we are not meant to do. Like going up
into a pretty girl’s room without an adult chaperone,” he explained chagrined. “That was on
us, we just… didn’t think?”
“Or really being alone with a girl after we turned 13,” Fred added. “And yes, we were kinda
swept away by the whole” He waved his hands about. “You… situation.”
Harry huffed a smile. Well, at least now he understood some part of what happened the other
day. Still, Fergus’ reaction seemed very over the top considering they were just talking.
“But what if they are your friend?” He questioned, waving away their apology.
He was itching to get one of his notebooks out. This was fascinating and confusing at the
same time. He had spent summers at the Weasleys, Hermione too. Why was it okay for her to
spent time with Ron and him alone in a room then? Although, the longer he thought about it,
he couldn’t really remember ever being alone with Ginny for that matter.
“Not after 13, nope. Unless they’re an adult, and then it could still be iffy considering,”
George made a so-so motion with his hand.
“That seems… sexist,” Harry felt the need to say.
“Well, yes. It’s okay if it’s your sister. Not a cousin, though.”
“Because, well, Blacks,” Fred grimaced. “That family would be a good example.”
“Or not so good. You get the gist.”
There was a moment of silence between the three. Harry stared a bit into the distance as he
tried to digest all that he had just been told. It was a lot and yet not really much at all. It

explained some things, but not everything. He frowned as he recalled the way the twins used
to behave towards Harley, starting with the festival where they had danced and flirted, all the
times they met since and the shopping spree. That had been very different to how they
carefully kept an apparently respectful distance now.
“What’s different now?” He questioned, not wanting to stew on this. He knew exactly what
his mind would do with this discrepancy if he let it.
“Ah, that…” Suddenly, George seemed a lot less suave and a bit lost for words. “You see, I
that is, we… that is…”
Harry raised his eyebrows and turned to Fred instead. Who folded instantly.
“Don’t look at me with those big, green eyes!” He wailed. “I’ll tell you everything!”
The sun burned high above the crooked rooftops of Knockturn Alley. The alley stank to high
heaven on a normal day, but summers made breathing here a chore. All that dark magic
coiling around the buildings, dirtying the very air one breathed.
It was disgusting and the last place one would expect the Light’s poster boy.
But his contact had been very clear that he had seen a suspicious individual running into the
darkness, hiding here after setting fire to his stall. Mad-Eye Moody wasn’t so sure
Mundungus hadn’t been drunk out of his mind, or maybe pissed-off some bird had spurned
his advances, but a lead was a lead.

Chapter End Notes

(*) Fergus’ dialect being all over the place was, at first, unintentional, then developed
into a thing. I kinda like it and it makes somewhat sense for the life he lives.

Chapter 26: Venturing
Chapter Notes

(make sure you go back to chapter 24 for a recap!)
Also, it is still 26th Monday 1996.

See the end of the chapter for more notes

“What’s different now?” He questioned, not wanting to stew on this. He knew exactly what
his mind would do with this discrepancy if he let it.
“Ah, that…” Suddenly, George seemed a lot less suave and a bit lost for words. “You see, I
that is, we… that is…”
Harry raised his eyebrows and turned to Fred instead. Who folded instantly.
“Don’t look at me with those big, green eyes!” He wailed. “I’ll tell you everything!”

George made a sound like he was choking on Bean before he grabbed his brother’s shoulder,
shaking his twin in a bit of panic.
“Not the right place!” He interfered, voice strangled with what Harry might call mortification
– if this wasn’t George Weasley. Seriously, he had never seen the twins embarrassed by
anything.
“Okay, fine,” Fred agreed through his brother’s shaking, “But then let’s get a move on to
somewhere better, because I want him to know right now!”
“Them,” George corrected absentmindedly, before turning back to Harry. “This is really not
something we should discuss out here.”
That said, he took both his brother and Harry by the hand, pulling them along down the street
towards the looming library building. Harry was just wondering who ‘them’ was.

Pushing the, frankly, humongous doors open (A feat only possible for him because these
doors were meant to open at a determined touch.) let Harry and the twins into a great marbled
entryway. There were no windows, leaving the immediate area dim. Harry couldn’t see much
beyond the unnecessary broad counter a few good metres in front of them, but that was more
down to being blinded by the light coming from beyond it.

It took him a few moments of blinking to actually parse through what he was really seeing.
They had entered the huge building of the public library on Vertic Alley, but standing inside,
getting a glimpse of what hid behind the entry, made it very clear there was magic at work.
Because obviously. The bigger-than-the-outside hall they had entered was lined with columns
forming a half-circle, a counter bracketed by two gates taking up the far side. There were
three marked points to probably register, but only the one in the middle of the marbled
counter was currently occupied. And going by the startled stare the person at said registry
sent them, visitors weren’t exactly common. Which… weird.
Then again, Wizarding Britain had a very confusing relationship with books in Harry’s newly
bibliophile mind.
Squaring his shoulders and smoothing down his skirt in one move, he stepped forward. For
some reason the elderly gentleman, as he could make out now that they were closer, regarded
the three of them with something akin to… not quite disdain, but definitely not benevolence
either. He looked them up and down through an iridescent monocle (And how he didn’t get a
headache from that, Harry could only wonder.), before completely bypassing Harry and
focusing on George.
“How can I help you… gentlemen?”
Harry could basically feel the twins’ eyebrows creep up their foreheads at the blatant snub.
Well, it was a bit of a convoluted thought process, but in this case, Harry actually hoped it
was a snub. He really did not want a random person, that clearly had some issue with him, to
see right through his disguise.
“Ah, my good sir,” George settled on, smile too sharp to be anything close to polite, “We,
that is my slightly less accommodating brother and I, are merely the plus two in this
ensemble. You see,” he continued, gesturing Harry to the front of their huddle, “This pretty
one is the true mastermind of our humble group. You might want to address your… inquiry
towards them.”
Harry blinked, mind stumbling over George’s speech for a moment, as the man at the registry
finally decided to grace him with his attention. The way he did so, down his nose and clearly
put-upon, had Harry wondering what about him warranted this type of reaction. He was
carefully dressed in one of his wizarding-approved summer dresses, fluttery and pretty, but
demure and adhering to social standards. It couldn’t be…
“If you are in search of household charms, miss, I am afraid you have come to the wrong
place,” was said in the carefully enunciated way one might speak to the slow of mind. “I hear
Mildrid’s Corner Store at the intersection to Diagon carries a reliable collection. You will find
everything about… ah.. fashion there as well,” he added in this way entitled people get when
they decide to be ‘nice’ to those they perceive less fortunate.
Ah sexism. It was as much fascinating as it was infuriating to be on the receiving end of such.
Thinking about it, ever since becoming Harley, he had encountered various instances of what
at first, he thought was mostly a reaction to his clearly poor appearance. Still not nice and not
the done thing, but so common in his every day life as ‘Freak’, it hadn’t really surprised him.

His relatives certainly hadn’t treated him with any respect and had made sure he had no way
of looking ‘respectable’ either. It was human, Harry thought, to judge by appearance, and so
he had not connected the dots at first. But, well, being a waitress, no matter where you’re
working, sexism is very much a part of your daily life.
Now that he had a better understanding of gender roles in the Wizarding World, too, a lot of
things made more sense. And to be fair, after realising what was going on, he very much
exploited it whenever possible. After all, he had only gotten his job because of sexism in the
first place. It was this weird dichotomy of making something, anything, good out of a shitty
situation.
“No, sir,” Harry responded with his now patented unbothered Harley-charm, “See, I am
actually looking for proper resear-”
“The fuck?” Fred next to him certainly had no such compunction. “Even if we were looking
for freaking household charms, you dusty old fart, what sort of assumption is that?” He
growled and Harry swore he could feel the air heat up around his friend.
“Excuse me?” And of course, the …librarian? Did take offense. “Be aware of your
surroundings, young man! This is no place for such language,” he sniffed contemptuously.
“Mate, you really need to get out more often,” Fred snarked, but there was actual anger in his
voice.
Harry could see George pinching the bridge of his nose, but he too looked pissed off at the
man. Right. Time to set these idiots straight.
“Excuse you,” Harry intervened with a glare at all three of them, “But as I was about to say: I
am looking for research materials on a variety of subjects and would appreciate a floor plan
of sorts, if you happen to have any.” As well as the necessary forms to apply for his NEWTS
at the Ministry, but no one else needed to know about that.
Sniffing yet again at Fred, the librarian, a Mr. …no, Magistra B. Omniscius as the name
plaque on his part of the counter read, turned back to Harry. This time he seemed to peer at
him with marginally more interest. (*)
“This is highly irregular, miss, I must admit,” he said. In the background Fred snorted
derisively. “Now, yes, we do in fact carry a layout of this institution. Touch your wand,” he
stopped to frown at Harry for a moment, “I am assuming you are at least old enough to carry
one? I will not allow an unchaperoned …child to just roam around nilly-willy.”
“Yes, sir,” Harry smiled blandly, ignoring the multiple digs and pretending he didn’t hear the
hesitation that scarily sounded like Magistra (And was that a title?) Omniscius was about to
say ‘woman’. “I passed my OWLs as well, if that is of any importance,” he added.
There was some muttering into the magistra’s (Really, it must be a title) beard, but he did
produce a rolled-up parchment. Because of course it had to be parchment.

“Touch your wand to the section of this floor plan you wish to know more about, and the
layout will expand to list which subject areas are stored in the specific section.”
Harry was about to take the parchment, but Magistra Omniscius instead rightened his
monocle and pulled a huge, leather-bound tome towards himself.
“Now, your names, if you please,” he ordered, clearing his throat self-importantly.

One would think that a library, a public one that is, would not be a good place to have a
private conversation. Because that was, why they were here in the… well, second place,
Harry reminded himself. He was first and foremost here to research wizarding culture. And
maybe actually look for more reference material for his homework.
Then again, Harry’s only experiences with libraries were exactly two: the tiny book nook of
his primary school pretending to be more than it was, and Hogwart’s library. They weren’t
particularly similar in any form but the expected silence. This right here? Not even in the
same league. It was a library, alright. That term definitely wasn’t enough to describe what
awaited them after finally being allowed passed the gates and beyond the marble entrance.
The immediate area beyond the gates completed the half-circle of the entrance hall into a full
one. Centrepiece of this half was a replica of what Harry assumed was the library itself, or
perhaps its beginnings, as the artwork showed only one level. But as interesting as the history
of the place likely was to be, right then something else was holding his attention:
The way his magic spread out to compensate his eyesight. He could use this ability usually
quite easily when indoors, so he hadn’t really thought much about just letting his magic do its
thing. But now it was following huge winding stairways on each side of a vast round tower,
winding their way along the massive walls up to an immense sky light. The source of the
blinding light suffusing everything in an ethereal glow. It felt like a cathedral in the way the
space had this transcendent sense of being immeasurable in its sheer size. And the magic of
this place! It was in and around everything, permeating the very air.
So, as it by now was second to breathing to him, Harry let his magic wander. It instinctually
tried to map the place, but… it was just too much. Everything and nothing all at once. Every
little detail from great murals and suffusing light and intricate statues lining bookshelves
and… books, so many books like tiny dots of magic everywhere… and beyond all of it,
beyond the endless halls, multiple levels, deep shadows, and bright light, beyond all of it
there was something asleep. A sense of a slumbering entity, a guardian maybe, orEverything just whited out for a few seconds before his magic retreated into himself abruptly.
“That’s a bit much for some books,” Fred commented.
“Makes Hogwarts feel like a pixy nest,” his brother agreed.
“I can’t see,” Harry stated weakly.

He could, technically, see, if you wanted to call his myopic sight that, but his magical sense
that helped him guide around felt blinded. Overwhelmed. He could see the physical world
around him, the ancient flooring they were stood upon, but everything beyond that? Fuzzy at
best. The fascinating stairs that seemed to climb along the walls like vines where not much
more than darker spaces along lighter walls now. There would be no guidance from that front.
“What?” The twins asked in unison, instinctually grasping onto his shoulders as if to steady
him.
For a second he floundered. Shit, Harley wasn’t supposed to be half-blind, or at least nobody
was supposed to know about it, because that really was just one more well-known fact of
Harry Potter, and… right. Stop. They knew.
They knew, they accepted, they were actively trying to understand.
It was okay.
“You didn’t wonder how I managed my job without my glasses?” Harry questioned instead,
looking from one pair of too blue eyes to the other.
The twins shared a glance that contained a whole conversation. They hadn’t actually
wondered about that, too caught up in the whole Harry-Harley thing, but now that they
thought about it…
“So how do you?”
“Do your job? Get around?”
They spent some time going back and forth on how Harry managed his daily life without his
glasses, right there next to the model of the library. He hedged on exactly what he, or his
magic, was doing, though. Somehow, this all felt like maybe they shouldn’t be talking about
it out in the open. Not that anyone seemed to be around. In fact, Harry couldn’t actually see
(pun intended) anyone moving around at all from where he stood…
The floor plan turned out to be similar to a less helpful version of the marauder’s map, if you
were to ask Harry. No people were tracked, just stationary floor sections labelled by level.
But it was still a fascinating piece of magic in the way it opened up to reveal a sort of threedimensional map of the building hovering just above the parchment. Flanking him on both
sides, the twins encouraged Harry to find what they were looking for.
„Um… okay, let’s see”, Harry fumbled for a second with the large parchment, then poked it
with his finger on the section that showed the entrance hall. “We’re about he- WOAH!”
With the touch of his finger the map highlighted an area, a title reading ‘Entrance Hall, level
0’ glowing up at them. George chuckled next to him.
“Seems there’s some more to talk about,” he commented.
“Later,” Fred agreed with a shit-eating grin.

Harry, though, took one look at the overlapping floors that apparently showed all the different
levels, below and above ground, and thanked magic for this beautiful creation. Although it
did feel like it could have been done in a less convoluted way, it was still amazing and would
hopefully keep him from getting completely lost in this place. That made him wonder… was
he supposed to tap every single section on every level to find the subjects he was looking for,
or could he maybe just…
“Show me Potions,” he commanded and grinned when the library’s floor plan did exactly
that.
But it did point them in the direction of level -3, as in three levels underground. And,
glancing around, for some reason lifts in the Wizarding World were apparently reserved for
the ministry and St. Mungo’s.

Three very long, very winding staircases later (And they didn’t even move!) they finally
reached level -3. It was darker here. A sort of twilit atmosphere, foreboding in how it seemed
to make everything the tiniest bit hazy at the edges, enveloped them as soon as they stepped
off the last staircase. Harry felt like adventures were hiding around the corners of more
towering shelves, but right now he needed to have a certain conversation. This had already
taken them much longer than it reasonably should and his mind was still stuck on all the
implications of what had happened between the three of them.
Ducking out of the guiding, and very distracting, hand on his shoulder that had helped him
not fall down steps he couldn’t see clearly, he pinned the twins in place with a determined
stare.
Fred glanced at George.
George glanced at Fred.
A blush darkened two sets of freckled ears.
With a sigh Harry beckoned them over to a seating arrangement, sitting down with his back
to the wall and frowning at their surroundings. He still could not see anyone, but there most
certainly were people around. As quiet as this place was, it didn’t feel abandoned. He could
almost make out some murmuring coming from in-between the darkened shelves. But sitting
down in armchairs facing him, the twins certainly had no issue casting a sort of silencing
bubble around the three of them.
So here they were, encased in a funny bubble that somewhat distorted the view around them,
but kept their conversations completely private according to the twins. Harry couldn’t help
but think that while the enforced silence was incredibly useful for stealth, the compromised
view of their surroundings was a big disadvantage. His mind kept on pointing out how this
kind of bubble made it easier to sneak up on them.
“Right.” Harry abruptly stopped his scrutiny of the magic surrounding them and turned
towards the twins. There must have been something on his face, because both took a

simultaneous deep breath as if bracing for what was to come. “Spit it out.”
Fred was shifting around his seat, opening and closing his mouth a few times, frowning and
finally making a frustrated sound deep in his throat before turning to his brother. George on
the other hand looked …uncomfortable. And wasn’t that yet another side of the twins he had
never experienced before?
“We’re going about this all wrong,” George finally huffed. “Here we were telling you how
we’re not supposed to be alone with you, and now look at us.”
“Yeah, the privacy bubble really feels like the icing on the cake,” Fred groaned from behind
his hand, rubbing his face until it turned red.
“But you know I’m not a-” Harry started, but interrupted himself. Not a what exactly?
George was giving him another of those thoughtful looks, but soldiered on:
“It doesn’t matter, what’s done is done,” he stated. “We’ll just have to make sure to be extra
careful from now on.”
“Careful of what?” What were they going on about? This was starting to feel like getting
Crookshanks to let go of Hermione’s yarn, one coaxed string at a time.
“See, before it was just… flirting. You were a cute girl that reminded us of a certain hidden
hero,” George started with a half-smile. “Also, before we were always in public,” he glanced
around. “More public than this.”
“Unless you count the one time I woke up at your place,” Harry muttered, blushing at the
memory.
“Well, yeah,” Fred agreed. “But that was down to us being too drunk to think straight.”
“Or walk straight,” his brother added.
“Sleep straight?”
“Certainly not be straight.”
As one they turned towards Harry.
“Anywho~ we’re making it official, Haz.” One blink. A second. “Let us court you. Properly.”
A third blink.
“So… date. You want to… date. Me?”
“Dating is for school, Haz,” George started, tone careful like he was sorting through the
words in his head first. “As long as everyone involved are students, it’s usually fine even for
Purebloods.”

Harry frowned. ‘Usually fine’ sounded ominous at best considering the topic at hand.
“Unless you come from a seriously prejudiced family, that is,” Fred interjected cheerfully.
“Prejudiced?” Harry cocked his head, trying to figure out what the supremacists had to do
with this now. “I thought for Purebloods it was all about blood purity,” he stated, nose
wrinkling at the mere idea. “So, what if a Pureblood just dates another?”
“Well, yeah,” George agreed, but there was a wrinkle in his brow that said something
bothered him. “For some pureblooded families it’s all about the purity rot and who you are,”
he reiterated. “Like what does your family do, how wealthy are you, do you hold a
reasonably important position in society?”
Harry nodded along. He knew all this as much as he loathed it. How could you let stuff like
this decide who you were going to spend your life with? Shouldn’t it be about the person(s),
their personality, their… everything?
“What my handsomely distracted brother is trying to say,” Fred interjected again. “Blood and
status aren’t all that important when it comes to mere dating. Unless you belong to an
extremist family, but again, then you wouldn’t be allowed to date at all.”
“I am so confused,” Harry sighed, rubbing a hand over his face as his hair was currently in a
very pretty braid, thank you very much.
“Mhm..” George rubbed his chin for a moment, eyes straying around them as if checking for
their continued privacy. “Let’s put it like this: Everyone can date in school, unless their
family is really traditional. But more important for us right now is what happens after school,
as you might have noticed, our fiery selves are no longer attendants of Britain’s most
exclusive club.”
Now that caught Harry’s attention.
“So, what happens after school?”
“Then, dear Haz,” the twins spoke up together for once, leaning forward to pin Harry in place
with their blue blue gaze. “Comes the time for courting.”
Oh. That was… well. Oh.
„If you will let us, that is,” George amended.
“If you want us,” Fred added, eyes vulnerable in a way Harry had never seen on the twins. He
was noticing so many more aspects of them lately, it was both endearing as it was humbling.
But did he want them? As more than friends? And was he willing to go through all this
elaborate courting stuff for it? Because it was obvious they wanted to do this, wanted to do
‘right by him’ as they understood it. Who was he to deny them that if it was all the same to
him?

Dancing at the festival. Listening to them banter. Running after them to protect them, and
having them instead step up to protect him. Being able to be just Harry, without having to
hide Harley away. Gentle hugs and soft touches.
“There’s a lot going on in my life right now,” he warned them carefully.
“Yeah, no shit,” Fred snorted,
“But whenever is that not the case?” George agreed.
Harry slowly blew out the breath he had been holding, ignoring how jittery it sounded.
“I want a list. An exact listing of what to expect,” he finally settled on firmly.
A moment of silence passed between the three, the faint hum of the library their only
background, before Fred and George slowly started smiling.
“I believe, brother-mine, that was not a no.”

It was there, hidden in the bowels of Magical London’s library, that Fred and George Weasley
asked to formally court Harry.
Or, maybe, not quite so formally.
“… NO, they did NOT!” Molly Weasley’s voice could be heard bouncing off of the dusty
walls of 12 Grimmauld Place, making Remus Lupin flinch in place.
Groaning at the pull the sudden move caused his sore muscles, the werewolf – and boy, was
he feeling that at the moment, the moon being less than 24 hours away – carefully held onto
his fraying patience. The Weasley matriarch had been on a war path for a while now,
something about the twins not following her expectations for their future yet again.
“Oh yes, they did,” Mad-Eye Moody growled right back. “And they seemed mighty
comfortable in that dark place, too.”
And if it hadn’t been for Fred and George’s suspicious behaviour at the last order meeting,
Remus would have dismissed them messing about Knockturn. No matter what Molly might
think, those two were not only legal adults, but very capable wizards indeed. He would know,
having been their teacher not too long ago.
“No, no, no, my babies wouldn’t…. first that trollop, now this,” Molly muttered, raising
eyebrows around the room with her choice in words. “And you are sure it was them down
that… that alley?”
And there it was. The missing link that might get his cub caught. Last meeting it had only
been Mundungus’ drunken ramblings, but now it was Moody. Now two very uncommon, if
seemingly unconnected, instances had taken place in the same area.

Chapter End Notes

Ah yes, so maybe I have a thing for libraries.

Either his first name is ‘Bibliophilus’ or it is ‘Bob’. Maybe he likes to pretend it is the
first.
