Chapter 27: Ruminating
Chapter Notes

To my lovely beav who helped brainstorm this mess: without you this would have sat on
my dying laptop for another month!
And emeraldoutcast out there on bluesky? You know your scene(s)!

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Watching Harry hurry away from them to get back to work, the twins leaned against each
other, all tension of the moment draining from them. Fred rubbed a hand over his chest, heart
still beating a bit too fast.
“I… did this really just happen, Georgie?”
His brother huffed, resting a bit more of his weight against his twin.
“We did it, brother.”
It was clear their hidden hero hadn’t truly processed what had just happened. But no matter,
they would make this work. For Harry, but also for themselves.
Tuesday, 27th August 1996
He was back in the library for the adventure he had been feeling here the day before. Or,
well, to find a guide for the adventure that had found him here yesterday.
Courting. Bloody hell. How did that even happen?
Wait. Did that actually happen? He had agreed, did that mean he was now officially dating
the twins? George? Fred? Both? At the same time?!
He, Harry Potter, was dating?
Oh.
Oh no.
Breathing out very carefully, Harry pushed open the doors to the entrance hall once more. He
had, yet again, taken care to dress according to wizarding fashion. He was a respectable
witch, ankle-length skirt, accentuated waist, no plunging necklines and …well, he didn’t own
a proper ‘leisure robe’, neither the long ones he was pretty sure were worn over dresses, nor a
shorter one as would be appropriate to wear over his current attire.

He had seen them in the windows of basically any clothing store in the magical district, but
they were expensive. A lot of variation in cuts, beautiful fabrics and intricate stitching that
seemed to carry secret messages and meanings.
Shaking his head at himself, he really had come a long way from thinking a skirt and long
hair made a good disguise, Harry approached the counter again. Magistra Omniscius was
there, just like the day before, and just like then he was eying Harry like he was a bug that
just crawled in from the street.
“Harley Wanlockhead,” he immediately introduced himself once more, smiling despite
himself at the name, “Here to look for research material.”
He was sniffed at, but no derisive comment was made this time around as the magistra pulled
his massive tome over to enter Harry’s pseudonym into the visitor list. And hadn’t that been
something, to introduce himself with his boss’ last name. And considering the magical
registry of the library accepted it as valid, yesterday as well as now… it made Harry feel
warm inside like there was a blooming sunrise in his chest. Fergus Wanlockhead really did
mean it when he claimed Harry as family, even if he just did it out loud in a rant at the twins.
Still, the man had no idea what the gesture meant for Harry. Or maybe he did know. One
could never be too sure with Fergus Ethelred Wanlockhead.
The real issue was navigating the giant building without his magical sense. Like the day
before his magical sense basically whited out the moment he stepped passed the gates. But
this time around Harry had come prepared.
“Your turn, Bean,” he whispered, carefully pulling the little ball of fluff from his pocket.
“You know what to do.”
At least he hoped so. While Bean had started showing more and more signs of understanding
Harry’s words, there was no way of knowing just how far that understanding extended. Was
the Pygmy Puff as intelligent as his lovely Hedwig, empathetic and at the same time capable
of always finding the intended recipient of Harry’s letter without so much as a name on the
envelope? Or was Bean just an extension of Harry’s magic since the bonding that changed
their colours?
Stopping short near the balcony that technically would have given him an overview of the
ground level (if his eyes would let him), Harry blinked. He had always thought of Bean as a
companion he didn’t know enough about to assign a sex, not to mention he wasn’t too sure
how Pygmy Puffs reproduced. He would have to ask the twins considering Pygmy Puffs were
a creation of theirs, made of magic in some form he couldn’t even imagine, but also
somehow related to Puffskeins. So, not knowing what his Bean was, he always went with
‘they’ – not to mention this summer certainly had taught him how gender was not always
connected to sex, how it just seemed to be a social construct, andWas that what Fred and George had meant? Was that why they had talked about Harry as
‘they’?
Blinking again, Harry compartmentalized his revelation in short order, because this was yet
another topic he could possibly look up right here. Slowly he made his way up the stairs, one

hand trailing along the wall, the other holding Bean out in front of him. His tiny familiar was
meeping at him every time some sort of obstacle came along - which was surprisingly more
often than one would expect in a magical library. There were stray books here and there just
floating around (and in one memorable case doing some kind of jig as soon as Harry came
close), statues and portraits that followed his movements, and sometimes a magical lantern
was hanging way too low. And then of course there were the other visitors. Because today the
library was in fact in use.
“OH my GOD that’s so sweet!” A voice squealed just before Bean announced someone’s
immediate presence. Harry blinked at the sudden appearance of a girl a few years older than
him from between the shelves he had just been about to pass. “What is it?”
“Bean,” Harry answered automatically, before grinning self-consciously, “I mean, one of my
familiars. A Pygmy Puff.”
As the girl came closer in a rush, Harry forced himself not to step back. She looked nice,
actually really pretty, he decided as she came close enough for him to properly see her, it was
just… people rushing at him always had him tense.
“Pygmy Puff? That’s a bit racist, isn’t it?” She didn’t wait for him to process that. “Anyway,
are they related to Puffskeins? Oh is it okay for you to have it out here?”
She didn’t sound scolding and more like she was worried Harry might get in trouble. He got
the impression she might not be so lenient if Bean hadn’t been so cute. In fact, the little fluff
ball was preening on Harry’s hand, turning this way and that to show off their rainbow fur.
“I don’t know,” Harry answered cautiously, “But I didn’t want to leave them home alone.
They like to be with their person, I guess.”
The girl cocked her head, staring for a moment longer at the posing puff, before finally really
looking at Harry.
“Whoops, so sorry!” She stretched out her hand for a shake forcing Harry to hurriedly
transfer Bean into his other hand to take it. “I’m Eleanor. Eleanor Mary Taylor, but you can
totally call me Ellie!”
“Ah… Harley. Nice to meet you,” he really tried not to make it sound like a question. Ellie
seemed a bit excitable, but overall nice enough. “Are you a Hogwarts student?”
He couldn’t remember ever seeing her, but to be fair, he rarely had looked passed his own
year mates. But he had to ask, just in case. He really did not need a random acquaintance to
recognise him like this.
“Me? Oh no, I like graduated last year,” she proudly declared, pretending to flick hair over
her shoulder with a wink. “Just got to watch the Triwizard Tournament, me, and then I was
out. Not that it was all that entertaining, mind.” She shrugged in a what-can-you-do way,
likely not noticing how that throwaway comment hit Harry like a bludger. Not all that
entertaining, yeah. “Are you? I don’t think I remember you, but then the kiddies weren’t like
all that,” she added.

Harry nod-shrugged non-committedly, unsure what to do with this whole conversation. But
maybe she could help him find what he was looking for.
“Um… I’m new to this.. the library, that is,” he stumbled over his words, flushing slightly.
Yeah, why was talking to someone closer to his age so damn awkward considering the type
of people he dealt with on a daily basis? Then again, he felt more like Harry than Harley right
then. And Harry Potter had always been socially… compromised.
“It’s a bit overwhelming, for sure,” Ellie nodded encouragingly.
“Yes!” Harry agreed empathically. “What’s up with those lanterns?”
“Oh yea,” she said, waving her hand around, “Those lanterns are totally magical. Like they
deliberately pop up at random places – all over the place!”
She seemed in equal parts scandalized as well as fascinated by this. Harry on the other hand
was trying to figure out why her sentence-structure was tripping him up. Was it a thing with
muggle-raised people? But he didn’t speak like that, Aunt Petunia would have his hide if he
dared pop in so many filler words out loud. Maybe it was a girl thing? But neither Hermione,
the go-to Muggleborn he could think of, nor Luna or Ginny spoke like this. Then again, as
much as he liked to ignore her gossip filled talks, he could vaguely recall Lavender having
similar speech patterns… maybe. Shaking his head, Harry focused back on the conversation
and Ellie talking about all the levels of the very magical library.
“Yes,” he agreed with her again, because hell yes, this library was just SO MUCH and more.
“And the person down at the registry, Magistra Omniscius? Is he always like that?”
Ellie blinked at him, then visibly shuddered.
“You actually came in through the main entrance? Like ew, that guy there… I always take the
floo from uni, you know?”
No, Harry didn’t know. There was floo-connect here? And there was a Wizarding
University?!
“Oh ‘suppose you would have to, not being enrolled and all that,” she mused.
Also, even with the man being such a… what had Fred called him? Dusty fart? Harry wasn’t
really comfortable with her way of talking about the magistra. The man was old-fashioned,
entitled and sexist. But he wasn’t creepy in the way she seemed to imply. Wanting to get the
conversation over with without being rude about it, Harry decided to appeal to Ellie’s need to
share her knowledge.
“With you knowing your way around so well,” he spoke up when she stopped to breathe, “Do
you happen to know where I would find culture related literature? Mainly anything to do with
courting?”
“Cou-what now?” She blinked twice, then frowned. “Oh no, you’re one of them? Didn’t
expect that.”

“One of.. who?”
“A Muggleborn trying to smooch up to the Purebloods!” She declared derisively. “Like if you
were one of them, you would already know that old-fashioned crap. What, did you think
some rich pureblooded jerk will look your way?” There was a story there. “Oh he might for
some round or two in a broom closet, but don’t bother trying to learn that sexist, outdated
rubbish,” she ranted, her voice getting louder with every sentence.
“I’m not-”
“Seriously, why bother? It’s so behind, like they really need to step into the 20th century!”
Ellie was about to continue her rant when an outraged noise caught both their attention.
Another girl about Ellie’s age stepped up to them, a few books hovering behind her as she
approached.
“Taylor, just because you can’t be bothered to learn about the very culture you’re living in,
doesn’t mean everyone wants to be this obnoxiously ignorant,” she declared, coming to a
stop in front of Harry and Ellie.
“Pureblood-nonsense, you mean! You’re all the same, high and mighty and terribly behind
with the times!”
The other girl snorted.
“Ah see, Ms Xenophobia, that’s where you went wrong. I’m not a Pureblood.”
“What do you- That makes no sense, you blooming idiot, we’re both English!”
“And you’re muggleborn and raised.”
“There you go with your damn blood-purity again!” (*)
Deciding to leave the girls and their argument behind, Harry edged away. He had just
remembered he could simply ask the layout plan he got the day before for specific topics.
Now, he wasn’t sure it would be as specific as a courting guide, but he could always ask for
cultural topics…

Just like it had been the case when descending into the underground levels, the library got
even quieter the higher up Harry went. The spiralling staircases went around and around the
lofty cathedral-esque structure, and while he could see all the way down if he dared to, he
could not make out anything above the level he was on at any given time. Beyond the
enormous domed ceiling, that is.
As Harry climbed staircase after staircase, he soon realised why. Every time he reached
another level, it would spread out across all of the space where there technically should have
been air. An illusion, he figured, but it didn’t explain how he could see right through the
floor/ceiling as long as he was on the stairs. Actually, if his eyes would let him, Harry was

quite sure he would be able to make out the entrance with its replica of the original library
building.
At some point he leaned against the railing, staring up into the dazzling skylight, and tried to
order his mind. He had stopped being able to hear the girls’ shouting match as soon as he left
that level, but simply trying to avoid getting distracted by all the knowledge at his fingertips
was the real hurdle.
The layout plan didn’t make the temptation of sidequesting any easier. All manners of topics
were listed, sectioned neatly into areas like ‘language and communication’, ‘art and
entertainment’, ‘architecture and settlements’. All of them had tiny extra labelling hovering
above the parchment when touched, and listing the subsections. Notably missing was
anything relating to sciences and technology, or at least the ones he was used to. There
certainly was a level for ‘magical air craft and travel’ with subsections of broomcraft, sports
and floo. The level for ‘creatures: beings, beasts and spirits’ farther down had had him very
tempted, but he was aiming for the very top: ‘Customs and traditions: Festivals, rituals, and
social customs’. Although the letters for ‘rituals’ seemed to be fading in and out of existence
for some reason.
“I’m scared,” he quietly confided to Bean.
So much could go wrong tomorrow. The twins, the whole courting situation, and then coming
here under the pretence to look that up… well, half-pretence, he really did want to learn
more… had made it easy to ignore the nagging feeling of dread. Tomorrow was the 28th, and
at night the full moon would rise. He would go with Fergus for that ritual that was supposed
to help cleanse his magical core. He had read as much as he could about it in the book
Sanguini had left him, but knowing theoretically what to expect was just not the same as…
truly understanding what it meant. This was something wholly new to him. Something he
would have staunchly thought of as Dark magic a month ago. Something he would have
scoffed at, would have vilified just because.
“Things have changed so much,” he whispered. “I have changed so much.”

Harry was curled up in a cushioned window seat on the highest level of the library when the
twins found him a few hours later. Above him the skylight had turned from bright morning
dazzle into the orangey late midday sun that haunted London these days. He was enjoying the
temperature control charm that kept the library at a reasonable level despite everything up
here being bathed in radiance. It made reading with his eyesight a lot easier than trying to
decipher dancing letters by candlelight.
“What say you, brother-mine,” a voice reached him from beyond a towering bookshelf.
“Bonfire fairy doesn’t seem as accurate today, does it?”
“Well, brother-dear,” another answered, causing Harry to locate the origin, “Are we sure
that’s not a Patronus?”

“The Not-Patronus can hear you, you’re aware?” He called quietly, not wanting to disturb the
serene atmosphere, but also trying to conceal his internal panic.
Not only was this the first time he was seeing the twins since he actually realised what he
agreed to (holy Merlin, the courting guide he had found was sitting very heavily in his
satchel), but he was also surrounded by books relating to tomorrow. As in stuff about ritual
magic. Very much banned ritual magic.
Two grinning redheads turned the corner, saluting him in unison before stepping just that tiny
bit closer than what would be considered casual. And there it was, the fluttering in his chest.
Harry's mind went pleasantly fuzzy around the edges.
“We thought, we might find you here,” George smiled softly, settling in next to Harry.
“Though, you know,” Fred commented, “Didn’t quite expect to have to climb all these
stairs.” He looked hesitant for a second, eyes flickering over Harry and his brother, an
aborted motion making his hands twitch. Then he decided to lean against the window frame
on Harry’s free side.
Sitting there bathed in the warm summer sun with a twin on each side, Harry forgot for a
moment how anxious the coming events made him. He felt wanted and safe here, talking
with the twins in hushed voices, joking quietly about this and that. He could get used to this.
“Er, Haz, my luminous,” George interrupted suddenly, sounding somewhat hesitant. “What
was it you were researching just now?”
Looking over, Harry felt like the floor was tilting sideways for a second. George was holding
one of the books he was rather sure weren’t meant to be publicly available anymore. How he
had come into its possession was another thing altogether. To be precise, George was holding
a book by the title Pure Moon Purification Ritual Circles.
An audible intake of breath from his other side brought him back from his spiralling
thoughts.
“Haz, that…,” Fred stared at the book in his brother’s hands like it was a freaking Death
Eater’s mask, “That’s seriously Dark! Where did you even get this?” he hissed, quickly
checking their surroundings as if expecting an auror to randomly pop into existence.
“It really isn’t,” Harry tried.
“No, Haz,” George frowned, carefully setting the book back down, nudging another with his
foot instead. “I know, it’s all fascinating, but you can’t let yourself get ensnared by this. Dark
magic is dangerous.”
“George is right, this sounds like… like ritual magic?” Fred whispered the last word. “Who
gave you this?" He sounded so protective.
“Guys,” Harry pinched the bridge of his nose, trying to formulate an answer. He wanted to be
pissed off with them, but truth be told, they were actually handling it far better than he would

have not too long ago. At least no one was screaming. Yet.
“Just a sec,” George said, pulling out his wand. Harry watched him cast the same silencing
bubble they had used the day before. “Okay, let’s get this over with and then we need to put
these away. You really don’t want to be seen reading something like this.”
“How are these even here?” His brother muttered under his breath, still keeping a suspicious
eye on the books surrounding them.
Harry made an annoyed noise. They just had their opinion all made up, hadn’t they?
“What if,” he started.
“There is no what-if when it comes to Dark magic, Haz. You know that.”
“No, wait, let me finish!” He glared at them. This was important. They needed to understand
this. “Just imagine for a moment what if magic couldn’t be categorised in ‘good’ and ‘bad’?
What if it truly is all about the intention behind the spell, the intention of the caster?” Raising
his hand to stop the twins from interrupting (and being seriously surprised when they
complied), he continued: “What if Magic is just Magic as it has always been, devoid of
human morality clauses? It just… it makes no sense to me to categorize Magic at all beyond
what the political factions say.”
There was silence as George and Fred digested his words. Well, at least he hoped that’s what
their heavy frowns and silent conversation over his head meant. Maybe he should be sharing
some of the information he had gleaned from Sanguini’s book, but somehow he wasn’t ready
to share that part of himself just yet. Instead Harry did what he does best.
“I’m going to attend a purification ritual tomorrow,” he stated daringly. “Something is
seriously wrong with my magical core and Fergus is helping me getting it fixed.”
The outrage was immediate.
“You WHAT?” Fred shouted, jumping up.
George on the other hand was staring at Harry with wide frightened eyes. Crossing his arms,
Harry glared.
“Are you being serious right now?”
“You can’t be!”
“Not you! Nope, I don’t believe it.”
“People get lost in this, Haz! Please, you know better than this!”
“Dark magic twists your mind, it ensnares your senses until you don’t know right from wrong
anymore. You know this. You taught us yourself just last year how to defend against this very
magic!”

So he told them. Well, some of it. How he had come home to Bandy-Legs back when he had
involuntarily stayed the night at Weasley’s Wizarding Wheezes. How Fergus had
immediately seen the insidious magic clinging to him. How the physical injuries of magical
attacks were the most obvious, but not the only damage that could happen. Of non-aligned, or
more correctly mal-aligned, magic and possible discordance. How the full moon was to help
clear up the residue around his core that had accumulated, not only from that single attack but
really all the times Harry had come into contact with, and been the target of, malicious magic.
“You’re famous for surviving… that night,” George clicked on.
“The Killing Curse,” Fred realised.
Harry just nodded. He was gnawing on his lips, waiting for the verdict. Was this the point
where the twins would finally call in reinforcements? The point they decided he was just too
much, or maybe that he was off his rocker, or…
A large palm smoothed down the side of his head, coming to rest on his cheek. Another
reached for his hand, holding on a tad too tightly to be comfortable.
When he looked up, he was met with two sets of serious blue eyes. The library outside their
little bubble seemed far away, two single points of heat on his body anchoring him to the
moment. A heartbeat. A moment.
“We trust you, Haz,” George whispered, Fred nodding beside him. “If you say it’s okay,
we’re with you.”
“And with that we mean literally there with you.”
The responsibility felt familiar, yet different. But this was too easy. Where was the
vilification? The disgust? Or maybe some claim of him being already under the influence of
some malicious magic?
Closing his eyes, Harry forced the doubt away.

Chapter End Notes

(*)“Ah see, Ms Xenophobia, that’s where you went wrong. I’m not a Pureblood.”
“What do you- That makes no sense, you blooming idiot, we’re both English!”
“And you’re muggleborn and raised.”
“There you go with your damn blood-purity again!”
If you didn’t quite get it: To Ellie being called xenophobic in this context makes no
sense, because both girls are English (not only British, but English). To her there is no
wizarding culture, they are just behind the times in her eyes. So the second girl calls
Ellie xenophobic, because she actively denies the existence of an independent wizarding
culture.

Why are the letters for ‘ritual’ blurred on the map?
And how did Harry find banned books in a public library?
It’s not really all that important, but I wonder if I should write a little interlude for that at
some point. Anyway, next chapter we’re finally leaving the library.
Blessed Samhain! <3

Chapter 28: Intervening
Chapter Notes

It's been 14 days, have a new one! <3
(Sooo... maybe I'm toying with the idea of an interlude. You know, just our Haz on that
first day in the library having a little side-quest of meeting slumbering guardians and
finding banned books.
But for now the story must go on!)

See the end of the chapter for more notes

Later that day, 27th August 1996
They were just going to pack some stuff, post the notice of the shop staying closed for at least
the next two days, and catch a few hours of sleep before going right back to Harry’s side.
There was no way they would risk him leaving to that dodgy ritual without them.
Fred and George were quiet on their way home from the library. The afternoon sun was hot
on their necks, but the revelations of the day still made them shudder occasionally. It had all
started so perfectly, too, with bright summer sun, a hunch of just knowing where to find
Harley that day, coming across their gorgeous mirage bathed in all that light… Only to realise
they had missed out on way more going on in Harry’s life than they thought.
Fred’s mind was switching back and forth between calling them Harry or Harley, because
while looking very much like Harley, they had felt way more like Harry today. He couldn’t
truly pinpoint the difference, it shouldn’t even be there, they were one and the same. Yet there
was a difference, but after today he felt like maybe that was Harry growing into his own.
Their own?
If only he hadn’t chosen to do it this way of all things.
“Do you think,” George started hesitantly, quietly, “Do you think it’s possible?”
“Doesn’t matter,” Fred replied.
He didn’t need to look at his brother to know what he was talking about. While he might be a
bit stuck on the difference in Haz today (yes, he decided, he should just go with ‘Haz’. Less
chance of a headache.), they both were very aware of the real issue here.
“He… they believe it’s true.”
“Doesn’t mean we can’t be prepared.”

Sharing a smirk with his twin, Fred went to open the hidden door to their apartment to
circumvent the currently bustling shop. Oh they would be prepared alright. No one messed
with their Haz. (*)
While the broom did its work in the background, angrily swiping at dust and dirt all over the
place, Harry was busying himself wiping down the tables.
One day. They had made it exactly one day into this whole courting business before
something happened. It wasn’t like he didn’t understand their worry. It would be more than a
bit hypocritical if he pretended not to get where his twins were coming from. Ritual magic
was banned in Wizarding Britain. It was not even spoken about in polite society. Because it
was considered Dark.
It didn’t matter that Harry had had his horizon forcefully broadened on the whole topic, it
didn’t matter that he maybe was still a tiny bit worried himself. For the twins it was a
completely new perspective that went against everything they thought they knew just dumped
on them. And in a way Harry had set an ultimatum as well.
He felt like shit just thinking about it.
They said they would be there, would come with him, would risk it all being a hoax… solely
so Harry wouldn’t have to face any consequences alone. They trusted his judgement – or did
they? They were willing to risk it. For him. In some twisted way that actually meant more to
him than blind trust. The twins understood that Harry, no matter what saviour nonsense
everyone else spouted about him, was simply a person. He could be wrong.
Breathing out carefully as once again the responsibility sat heavy on his chest, Harry patted
his apron pocket to make sure the courting guide was still there. He was torn between the
gnawing worry about possibly endangering his twins and spiralling thoughts about the whole
courting thing.
Maybe he should have waited reading the guide until after the ritual. His thoughts kept on
circling the things he had found in there before deliberately trying to be responsible and learn
more about the ritual to come. Some points kept jumping back at him.
For one there was the age requirement. There was a reason why the twins had said dating was
for school and courting was what came after. You had to be of age to be able to court as it
was apparently exclusively done with the intention of marriage (a whole ‘nother bag of Berty
Bott’s). An exception could be made if the guardian of the underage party gave their express
permission.
Harry’s eyes went a bit hazy as he stared into the middle distance imagining the twins turning
up at Privet Drive to formally ask the Dursleys for permission to court Harry. Yeah. No. Then
again, maybe as his godfather Sirius would be…?
Absent-mindedly rubbing at his faintly aching chest, Harry turned back to cleaning the
parlour. And come to think of it, who would the twins ask to court? As in, would they ask his
guardian to court …Harry? Harry Potter, the boy-with-too-many-hyphened-names? Or would

they ask for Harley, the penniless waitress of a shady pub in Knockturn? And even
pretending they could ask Sirius, how were they going to handle the whole Harley-thing
when it came to the Weasleys? Who were they going to introduce to their family? Did they
even want to (re-)introduce him?
The twins were of age and they weren’t the firstborn (or otherwise designated heir), so going
by what he had read in the courting guide, they did not necessarily need to ask their head of
house for permission to court anyone. Not in modern days, anyway. Technically, their head of
house, and he assumed that would be Arthur Weasley, could still kick them out for it. Could
take away the family name – and you did not want to be nameless in Wizarding Britain. But
he doubted Arthur would do that to any of his children.
Also, hadn’t the twins told him something about Bill currently courting? And their brother
had basically sprung his fiancée on his parents by simply bringing her with him for a longer
stay this very summer. Oh, maybe it really wasn’t the best time to bring the whole thing up
with the Weasley parents…
His hand froze on the doorhandle. The standard wards they had had purchased with the
building included anti-theft, fire-proofing and general weather resistance. But what had Fred
pausing were the wards their brother Bill had added for them: An inbuilt homenum revelio
spell to alert the owner to any other human presence on their property, in this case their flat in
particular.
“Gred, did Lee say anything about coming over today?”
Catching on immediately, George just shook his head minutely. Wand in hand, he took up
position to the side of the door, ready to have his brother’s back.
Fred took a breath, then pushed the door to their living space open. Having closed all blinds
against the heat, the only source of light came from the fire place – the previously unlit fire
place. It caused shadows to dance across the cluttered room catching on a figure sitting in
their only armchair.
Slapping down an order with enough force to make the table hum with the magical backlash,
Harry froze for a moment when an idea occurred to him.
He was the only Potter. So that would make him the head of House Potter, right? He assumed
there were some technicalities involved that he would – again – have to look up, BUT it
would mean there was no one to ask for permission. No one to prevent him from courting the
twins… or being courted, whatever. Well, he supposed it could be argued that the decision
should still be made by his guardian since he was underage. But which one? The Dursleys
were muggles, Sirius was his godfather but also a wanted criminal. His mind shied away
from putting Dumbledore into that spot. That couldn’t be right.
Then again, he might not be of age, but he did acquire wand rights by successfully sitting his
OWLs. As his research into the importance of graduation in the Wizarding World had taught

him, he was technically considered an adult on probation until he turned 17. Was it perhaps a
grey zone of some kind“Errr-” A voice tried to get his attention, but Harry had to follow his train of thought before
he lost it.
He was also the last of his line, so if he were to be pureblood, he would actually be required
by law to have heirs of his own… which, as far as he knew, meant they were missing a few
organs in their courtship. Oh Merlin, he did not just think about that. But he wasn’t a
Pureblood. But he was the last of a Pureblood House“Harley, you in there?” His boss’ voice finally brought him back to the present – and the
staring customers whose orders he had basically spread all over the table. It said a lot about
his reputation in the pub that no one had retaliated.
Fergus’ large hand pulling on his own had him follow the man on auto-pilot. It wasn’t until
the noise level abruptly dropped that Harry realised, they were now in the little space in the
back of Bandy-Legs where the liquors were stored.
“You wanna tell me what’s all that about?” Fergus glared down at him, but Harry could feel
the concern fairly wafting off of the hulking figure.
“I’m fine,” he responded before he could stop the words. “Er- I mean… I’ve got something
on my mind?”
“No shit,” Fergus snorted. “Does it have anythin’ to do with that book you keep on going
back to?”
“How do you-?” Oh he really was an obvious Gryffindor, wasn’t he?
“I don’t have to leave my wards to hunt down redheaded menaces, do I?”
And it just poured all out. He had to tell someone, anyone, about the twins. About what he
agreed to. Fergus would be with him during the ritual and didn’t seem all that concerned, but
Harry felt he needed to warn the man about the impending twin doom to come and the reason
why they decided to accompany them, and“So they made it official,” Fergus muttered when Harry finally stopped his word-vomit to
breathe. “And they pretend to go about it all proper-like, do they now? Courting, huh.”
“Is it… not okay?” Harry couldn’t help but ask. He was pretty sure he had understood the
guide correctly, not to mention the twins would never joke about something like this.
“Well, they forgot to involve your family, now, didn’t they?” Fergus grunted.
For a moment Harry felt taken aback. Fergus couldn’t know he was an orphan, but then
again, no 16-year-old with healthy family relations would turn up randomly in a bar to ask for
shelter. Was he- he couldn’t mean…

“What did’ya think tha’ tartan meant, bunny?” Fergus muttered under his breath as he patted
Harry’s shoulder awkwardly and went back into the pub proper.
He wasn’t sure he was meant to hear that. Fergus could be even more socially …clumsy than
Harry himself, but either way, it felt like a family claim. He had never imagined something
like this would one day happen to him. Sirius had always been a dream, a little flame of hope
to keep him going, but illusive. Unrealistic.
His godfather was a wanted criminal, the one person that could have proven his innocence
probably by now outside the country. Couldn’t be that hard to leave when one’s able to turn
into a rat. Never mind that Sirius wasn’t even awake right nowFergus claimed him as family. Again. With more words anyway.
Smiling to himself, mind both clearer yet fuzzy, Harry focused back on his work. His wolfy
friends were present tonight, but much less rowdy than he was used to. He hadn’t really been
able to talk much to Faolán and his pack yet, but all of them were a bit grouchy and miserable
with the approaching full moon. It was weird, though, that they were out and about at all.
Remus would be too sore to move right now, either curling up in front of a fire, nursing hot
chocolate or not even leaving his bed at all if it was a bad month. And considering his mate
was currently unreachable…
Either way, Fergus had already told Harry he would be keeping the pub closed during the full
moon tomorrow night, because he would be at Harry’s side.
During the ritual.
“You boys have some explaining to do,” Arthur Weasley frowned at his sons.
George edged into the room behind his brother and closed the door once he verified there
really was only their father present.
“What’s this, dad, an intervention?” Fred joked, stuffing his wand away haphazardly. George
didn’t say anything, his focus on Arthur’s face in the flickering firelight.
“You could say that,” their father agreed, levering himself out of the armchair with a grunt.
“Do you need one?”
Sharing a glance, the twins split up. Fred moved towards their father with a fake thoughtful
look; George went straight for the kitchen. This felt like they would either need tea or
whiskey. Possibly both.
“Mhm,” he heard his brother’s voice from the other room, “Depends. We did kinda go a bit
overboard with the latest prank-package we sent ickle Ronniekins, I guess.”
There was a moment of silence that made the hair on George’s neck stand up. He had an
uneasy feeling about this.

“While I really think you shouldn’t torture your younger brother like this,” Arthur finally said
with an audible frown in his voice, “No, son, this is not why I am here today. In fact, I
wouldn’t be here at all, if you hadn’t banned your mother from floo entry.”
Ah. There it was.
“George, get back in here now,” Arthur ordered.
“I’m right here, dad,” George answered quietly, a teapot and cups floating behind him.
He wasn’t surprised their father knew exactly who was who here. Somehow, even though he
spent most of his time either at work or sequestered away in his little shed, their father always
knew. He liked to pretend otherwise, finding their antics amusing, but right now clearly was
not the time for jokes.
(Though, really, if you asked the twins, there was no time that couldn’t be made better with a
well-placed prank.)
Arthur Weasley appeared deeply worried. To be honest, George recognised that very look
from his own face mirrored on his twin. This was close to what they had looked like when
Haz had told them what they planned to do tomorrow. Worry, yes, but also disappointment. It
stung. He never wanted to see that look on his father’s face ever again. Not when Arthur
never criticised their life choices like their mother was so wont to do.
“Are you not going to explain yourself?” Their father frowned at their lack of verbal
response.
But honestly, what was there to explain? Molly had had it coming the way she behaved. Not
only that day, but really as long as they could remember. And then accosting them in their
own home, and saying those shitty things about Haz? The way she just assumed without even
bothering to ask?
“I don’t know what mum told you,” George sighed, shuffling things off the coffee table
without much care. “But no one likes to be treated that way, and in our own home at that.”
“And no one wants their guests insulted, either,” his twin added. Oh yea, Fred was getting
pissed off just thinking about it.
Arthur eyed them for a moment, weary gaze drifting from his sons over the cluttered and
uncleaned living space and back to the twins.
“I have to admit,” he finally settled on, sitting back down in the armchair, “I am unsure what
you’re referring to.” Moving about until he found a comfortable spot, he went on: “But
whatever disagreement you and your mother have right now, it is not what brought me here.
Though, in a way it is, considering it seems to be the reason she could not come through the
floo?”
Fred plopped himself down on the floor and watched George fill some cups. He wasn’t quite
sure where his brother found the tin, so used to drinking coffee now. So, their mother hadn’t

told anyone, or at least not their father, about their little dispute… As it hadn’t gone so well
for her, he wasn’t too surprised.
“Then what is this?” He bluntly asked, mood souring further.
With a sigh Arthur eyed the tea for a moment, maybe considering how save it was for
consumption, before taking his designated cup. He seemed to take comfort in holding it, and
George couldn’t help but lean against his brother for the same. Something was really off here.
“I’ll just come straight to it then, shall I?” Their father muttered into his cup. “You’ve been
seen wandering Knockturn Alley.”
He said that like it explained everything, disappointment heavy in his voice.
“Okay?” Fred questioned. Was he disappointed they had been caught in their shenanigans?
Never mind they had really only been visiting Harley- oh shit. Sharing a panicked glance
with his brother, Fred sent a small prayer to whatever deity was in season that no one had
followed them to Bandy-Legs.
“What do you-” Arthur looked baffled for a moment, before gathering his thoughts. “I never
thought I would have to say this to my own sons, but you do not go there. It is a Dark place
and I thought your mother and I had raised you to know better than this.”
And while they would have probably agreed with him on that before moving out, now Fred
and George couldn’t quite muster the same amount of vilification for the – granted – quite
grizzly alley. Yes, it wasn’t a nice place. It was definitely dangerous after sundown
considering they had been attacked there not too long ago (which they hadn’t told anyone
about for fearing this very reaction, now that he thought about it). But it was also where
Harry had found refuge. It was where their Haz had found friends and shelter, even if said
shelter didn’t seem to be particularly fond of the twins.
Oh. They were being a bit hypocritical here, weren’t they?
Not that their worry would ever stop them from being right there with Haz, having their back
no matter how the situation turned out to be. But it was true that he and his twin were very
biased against everything Haz had told them today. Just like their dad was now. They would
mainly be going to that proclaimed ritual to protect Haz from their wrong choice – or, that’s
what they had thought, but looking at the situation now?
Yeah, both suddenly had a much better understanding of how Harry was likely perceiving the
whole situation. And honestly, if the twins were still willing to walk into what they believed
to be a dangerous and likely permanently damaging situation just so Haz wouldn’t be alone,
they could at least give the whole thing the benefit of the doubt.
Because what if there was something to the whole ‘magic simply being magic’ idea? What if
it truly was simply down to the caster, that no magic was inherently bad?
It went against everything they had always been taught, yet both Fred and George were
willing to look closer at this ‘what if’. First, it had been for Haz’s protection. But now…

He couldn’t sleep. The night was suffocatingly hot, the air outside completely still and
humidity so high every surface inside the pub felt wet. Not that it mattered, Harry’s thoughts
were still all over the place anyway.
After Bandy-Legs closed and the parlour was cleaned up enough there wouldn’t be any big
messes awaiting them in the morning, he was left to his own devices. And normally Harry
would be going straight to bed, exhausted from being on his feet for hours, interacting with
so many people and really just a long day in general. But tonight… tonight he was sat up in
his tiny room, back pressed against the wall for any hint of cooler surface, and Sanguini’s
book open on his lap.
The letters were dancing in the candlelight, making concentrating on them even harder than
the whole situation already did. He was really going to do this. He would go with Fergus
tomorrow after sundown to attend a purification ritual. It was… a lot.
The Harry from a month ago would never have even considered it.
But he did.
All these adults in his life that knew about his continued exposure to malicious magic, illintended magic, yet not one of them ever so much as mentioned the underlying danger.
Fergus himself had told him he really only spoke about it out loud, because in contrast to the
other exposures the last attack had obviously been recent and Harry was so young. And
alone. Which equated to no one being there to help him purify his core.
It was apparently bad manners to talk about someone else’s core. But really, Harry doubted
that was the reason none of the other oh-so-capable adults in his life had ever mentioned it.
Yes, so maybe they couldn’t see it the way Fergus (and Harry to some capacity) could, but
they must know about the theory? They had to be aware of the possible danger? Especially to
a developing core?
Harry had read a lot in Sanguini’s book, had learned about different methods of cleansing
one’s core of the kind of malicious magic that could cling and muddy it up. That could inhibit
development in young magicals, could warp their very magic if left untreated long enough. It
wasn’t such a danger to adults. Their cores were much stabler, less easy to influence, but also
less likely to learn new magical schools… he was digressing.
The point was, every single adult in his life had failed him.
Their dad had left angry. It wasn’t something they were used to, Arthur Weasley rarely ever
got angry. Or at least not to the level they had witnessed today. He could not fathom why his
sons would not even defend their trip (more trips, really, but their dad didn’t need to know
that) down the oh-so-terrible alley.
They had made sure to get the details, they had to protect Haz after all. Those details ended
up not amounting to much and if they hadn’t been so rash in their reaction, they could have
possibly played it all off as a misunderstanding. It really all came down to Moody sticking his

peg leg where it didn’t belong. And wasn’t that just a scary mental picture? Anyway, the exauror was apparently out and about, prowling the magical district to find Haz.
Well, the man was trying to find Harry Potter, everyone’s darling saviour. If that’s who he
was looking for, he wouldn’t find so much as a speck. But he had made it to Knockturn. That
didn’t bode well.
Neither George nor Fred could sleep that night. Their minds kept circling the upcoming day,
the whole wonky situation they were going to get themselves into, the idea of there not
actually being ‘Dark’ magic as they had been taught… but also Haz. Beautiful, clever,
gorgeous, enchanting Haz. Their trouble with their eyesight, but also apparent use of
wandless magic. Them needing a ritual to be healthy.
And then, how neither brother spoke up to tell their father why they were down Knockturn
Alley. That they were courting. That they were utterly in love with Harry Potter and willing
to go against everything they had been taught from a young age on to be there for him.
The twins hadn’t been there at the end of the last school year when Harry and their siblings
went to that accursed department of mysteries. They hadn’t been there when the kids fought
for their lives. But they would be there now. Harry would not have to face this alone.

Chapter End Notes

The stage is set, guys.
(*) SO I tried keeping the twins’ way of addressing Harry in their minds deliberately
inconsistent. They really should be talking to Harry about this and not just make
assumptions, but they are also teenagers that just got exposed to a whole lot of new
angles to view the world they’re living in.
